# Ontology

This page defines the vocabulary of CommandSuite: every term a user, an operator or an
agent can meet, what it means, what it is *not*, and what it is called in the SDK, the
database, the routes, the CLI, the MCP toolbox, the web UI and the docs.

It arbitrates. Where this page and any other page disagree, this page is right and the
other page is a bug — fix it there. Where the vocabulary and the code disagree, the entry
says so and states what the code does, because the code is what ships.

Every entry has one shape. **Names** gives that referent's spelling in each layer.
**Not** separates it from what it is repeatedly confused with. **Values** enumerates a
closed set, **Verbs** the operations, and **Decided** records a naming decision with the
option it was taken over. The **Not** line is the point of the document: 318 evidenced
confounds got into this codebase one plausible local decision at a time, and a
distinction nobody wrote down is one that gets re-argued in the next pull request.

Why a term is what it is, and what it was chosen over, is recorded in
[Ontology decisions](/docs/dev/ontology-decisions). Vocabulary only someone working *on*
this repository meets — gates, doors, the route walk, changesets, the DCO — is out of
scope here and belongs in a contributor glossary that does not yet exist; until it does,
the terms this page pushes across that boundary have no home, and the Not lines that
name them say where they belong rather than linking.

The decision log's "where it is recorded" column predates these headwords, and eight of
its pointers name a term this page settles under a different heading: D1 under
**Objective trace**, D2 under **Member kind**, D10 under **Member binding** and
**Request template**, D15 under **Authenticator secret**, D17 under **Resolved runner
environment**, D25 under **Notification delivery status**, D36 under **Team role**, D54
under **Diagnostics-store health**. The headwords here are the later and arbitrating
ones; the log's column is the side to edit.

`*(defined in code only)*` marks a term no other prose defines. That is policy, not an
oversight: a term belongs here if a user, an operator or an agent can encounter it — on
the wire, in a badge, in a tool description — whether or not a page already explains it.

## Conventions

The entries below settle words that already exist. These rules settle the ones that do
not. Each confound in this ontology was a locally reasonable decision: a pull request
needed a name, no rule existed, the author picked the word that read best in the file in
front of them, and the vocabulary forked. Eight rules close that gap. They are binding on
new names and on renames; they do not by themselves schedule a migration of existing
ones, which is what the [decision log](/docs/dev/ontology-decisions) is for.

Every example below is a live break in the tree, cited so a reviewer can check it.

### Reserved words

**Rule.** The bare words below are reserved. None of them may name a type, a field, a
route segment, an enum value, a CLI flag, an MCP tool argument, or a heading without a
qualifier. Use the qualified alternative instead.

These are the words the audit found naming four or five referents each. A qualifier costs
one word at the call site and is the only thing that stops the next reader from resolving
the term to the wrong subsystem — because every one of these words is already correct
somewhere, so nothing in a code review flags the fifth use.

| Reserved | Say instead |
|---|---|
| context | **team context** (the standing statement every member carries) · **agent context** (the model's conversation window) · **personal instructions** (a member's own directives) · **instruction block** (one composed unit) |
| activity | **activity stream** (the durable per-member record) · **work state** (the live `idle`/`working`/`blocked` signal) |
| session | **runner session** (one `csuite <runner>` invocation) · **web session** (the cookie-backed sign-in) · **agent session id** (the framework's own conversation id) |
| trace | **activity stream** (the record) · **capture pipeline** (the mechanism `--no-trace` turns off) · **activity database** (the storage `prune-traces` deletes) · **exchange capture** (one LLM call) · **objective trace** (the per-objective window) |
| transcript | **session transcript** (the runner's on-disk record of an agent session) · **thread** (a channel or DM message list) |
| role | **team role** (`Role` — title plus prose) · **channel membership label** (`admin` / `member`) · `AnthropicMessage.role`, the provider chat turn (`system` / `user` / `assistant`) · **persona** (the route-walk tier, D36 — contributor vocabulary) |
| legacy | **read-tolerated** · **write-only residue** · **accepted-and-expanded** · **aliased** · **old-protocol peer** · **superseded implementation** · **migration source** |
| gate | **permission gate** (the server-side check and its 403 — the one product sense) · every other sense is contributor vocabulary (D39) |
| endpoint | **notification endpoint** (the configured record) · **route** (an HTTP path the broker serves) |
| capability | **permission leaf** (what a member may do) · **protocol capability** (what a peer's version supports) · **executor state** (what a member's live runners report they can do) |
| source | **tool source** (the registry record) · **capture source** (what produces activity) · `sourceId` (the dedup key on an activity event) · **query source** (work-lane attribution) |
| binding | **member binding** (a member↔resource grant) · **request template** (a custom tool's HTTP shape, D10) |
| delivery | **message delivery** (the ledger row for a pushed message) · **notification delivery** (a webhook receipt) |
| disposition | **message disposition** (a runner's terminal acknowledgement) · **amendment disposition** (`correction` vs `scope_change` on a team-process edit) |

Two of these are already settled elsewhere and are listed so the word stays reserved:
**activity** resolves by D6, which finishes the work-state rename on the wire, and
**gate** resolves by D39, which keeps the runtime permission check on the product side
and puts every repository-process sense in the contributor glossary.

**Broken today.** `Team.context` (`packages/sdk/src/types.ts:164`) spends three lines of
its own doc-comment disowning the other sense — *"Distinct from agent conversation
context"* (`:156-158`) — because the field name cannot carry the distinction. The two
permission leaves make the same collision unresolvable: `team.manage` covers team
context and `members.context` (`packages/sdk/src/types.ts:79`) covers agent context, and
neither name says which context it means. Elsewhere, `role` names three unrelated closed
sets in one file — `Member.role: Role` (`:200`), `ChannelMember.role: ChannelMemberRole`
(`:675`), `AnthropicMessage.role: string` (`:2031`) — and `legacy` marks two exact
opposites: `'amended' | 'event_corrected'` are *"LEGACY READ-ONLY KINDS. No write path
produces either"* (`:1788`) while `outcome_version` is *"LEGACY, unread. Written by the
retired contract-amendment layer"* (`packages/core/src/objectives.ts:69`). One line of
frontmatter breaks two rules at once: `docs/dev/rest-api.mdx:3` promises *"Every HTTP
endpoint the broker exposes … permission gates"*, where **endpoint** means a route and
**gate** means a permission check, in the same document that later says *"Management
endpoints are dual-auth"* (`:777`) about `/notifications/endpoints`, which are records.

### Verb grammar

**Rule.** One verb per operation class, spelled the same way in the SDK, the CLI and the
MCP toolbox: `post`, `get`, `list`, `create`, `update`, `delete`, `bind` / `unbind`, plus
`set` / `delete` for a write-only value slot. A tool or command that mutates never
carries a bare-noun name. The SDK arbitrates, because `packages/sdk/src/client.ts` is the
least-drifted surface in the repository (D42).

An agent reads the MCP tool list as its entire action vocabulary, and an operator reads
the CLI usage block as theirs. When those two disagree the same operation has two names
and neither surface can be learned from the other.

| Operation class | SDK | MCP tool | CLI | HTTP |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| read one record | `get<Thing>` | `<noun>_get` | `get` (aliases `view`, `show`) | `GET /<noun>/:id` |
| read a collection | `list<Things>` | `<noun>_list` | `list` | `GET /<noun>` |
| create a record | `create<Thing>` | `<noun>_create` | `create` (alias `add`) | `POST /<noun>` |
| modify mutable fields | `update<Thing>` | `<noun>_update` | `update` (alias `set`) | `PATCH /<noun>/:id` |
| destroy a record | `delete<Thing>` | `<noun>_delete` | `delete` (aliases `rm`, `remove`) | `DELETE /<noun>/:id` |
| grant / revoke access | `bind<Thing>` / `unbind<Thing>` | `<noun>_bind` / `<noun>_unbind` | `bind` / `unbind` | `POST` / `DELETE …/bindings` |
| set / clear a write-only value | `set<Thing>Value` / `delete<Thing>Value` | `<noun>_set_value` / `<noun>_delete_value` | `set-value` / `delete-value` | `PUT` / `DELETE …/value` |
| emit a message into a thread | `push()` (transport) | `<scope>_post` | `post` (alias `push`) | `POST /push` |

Six verbs stay outside the table because they name genuinely distinct operations, and
merging them would lose a distinction the code argues for: **archive** (a soft delete
with its own column), **write** (a versioned full-text replacement that appends an edit
row with a reason and a disposition), **upload** / **download** (bytes that stream past
the agent's context rather than through it), and the named lifecycle transitions
(`complete`, `cancel`, `reassign`, `approve`, `refresh`, `replay`, `discuss`).

**`rm` and `add` stay in the CLI.** D42 makes the SDK the arbiter for *tool names*, not
for terminal ergonomics: `rm` is shell-native, the alias plumbing already accepts every
spelling, and breaking an operator's muscle memory buys nothing an agent can read. The
rule is that `delete` and `create` become the *documented* spellings — the ones in
`USAGE`, in `docs/reference/cli.mdx`, and in every error message — and `rm` / `add` /
`remove` keep dispatching forever. UI affordance words are out of scope entirely:
**Save**, **New**, **Refresh**, **Dismiss**, **Close** are button labels, not operations,
and a button that reads `Save` may call `update` or `write`.

**Broken today.** The two surfaces are exactly inverted, so no mnemonic works. MCP says
`members_add` (`packages/cli/src/runtime/tools.ts:688`) while the CLI says
`member create` (`packages/cli/src/commands/member.ts:44`); MCP says `members_remove`
(`tools.ts:764`) while the CLI says `member delete` (`commands/member.ts:50`) — and every
*other* registry inverts it back, with `tool_sources_create` beside `csuite tools add`
(`commands/tools.ts:52`) and `tool_sources_delete` beside `csuite tools rm` (`:56`).
Detail reads split three ways on no principle: `team_get` (`tools.ts:652`) beside five
`_view` tools (`tools.ts:359,805,1025,1163,1385`), while `csuite secrets view`
(`commands/secrets.ts:49`) works and `csuite tools view` does not — `show` is the only
spelling that command accepts (`commands/tools.ts:50`). And three tools that mutate carry
bare plural nouns — `secrets_bindings` (`tools.ts:1271`), `variables_bindings` (`:1117`)
and `tool_sources_bindings` (`:934`). The last is described in its own text as
*"Grant or revoke members' access to a source"* (`:936`) while sitting in the tool list
between `tool_sources_list` and `tool_sources_view`, where a model scanning names for
what is safe to call gets no signal that it writes.

### `kind` versus `status`

**Rule.** `kind` is a layer-local discriminator on a union, and the union's vocabulary is
named by the enclosing type — `ActivityKind`, `ToolSourceKind`, `ObjectiveEventKind`.
`status` is a lifecycle enum whose values are states with transitions between them.
Neither word is available for "sort of thing" in general. A verdict on a subsystem is
**health**; how one attempted unit of work ended is an **attempt outcome**; an ongoing inability to
act is **condition**.

Bare `kind` is worth keeping precisely because discriminated-union narrowing depends on
it, and thirty fields already use it correctly. What the rule adds is the requirement
that the *type* be named, which is already the practice at the type level and stops at
the field level.

**Broken today.** `status` holds an HTTP status code twice on the same wire that uses
`status` for `ObjectiveStatus`: `status: number | null` on the captured Anthropic
exchange (`packages/sdk/src/types.ts:2017`) and `status: 401` on the auth-state activity
event (`:2631`). Both should be `httpStatus`, so `status` never means a number. In the
other direction, `Teammate.kind?: 'person' | 'agent'` (`:210`) declares a two-value union
that the only writer never completes — `teammatesFromMembers`
(`packages/core/src/members-domain.ts:265-276`) emits `'person'` or omits the field — so
a consumer trusting the declared union waits for a value that never arrives.

### Identifiers

**Rule.** Three identifier kinds, three grammars, and one rule about qualification.

- **slug** — lowercase kebab, 1–32 characters, human-typed, URL-facing, unique per store.
  Immutable everywhere except channels, because on every other subject a durable
  reference rides on it: the `secret:<slug>` thread tag, the `/hooks/<slug>` ingress URL,
  the `hook:<slug>` sender identity, the `<slug>__<tool>` MCP tool name.
- **id** — opaque and machine-minted. Never typed by a human, never parsed for meaning.
- **name** — the member handle: `^[a-zA-Z0-9._-]+$`, 1–128 characters, the addressing key
  on the wire, in routes, in the JWT `member` claim, and as a member's filesystem home
  segment. A slug is not a name; do not call it one.

**An entity's own identifier is bare `id` or `slug`. A reference to another entity is
qualified — `objectiveId`, `endpointSlug`, `member_name`.** The repository already does
this without exception at the type layer; the rule extends it to MCP tool arguments and
CLI positionals, where every argument is foreign by definition and therefore always
qualified.

**One prefixed-id shape, one separator.** A prefixed id is `<prefix>-<value>` with a
hyphen, and it earns its prefix only when a human or an agent reads it aloud. Everything
else is a bare UUID.

**Broken today.** `var_${now.toString(36)}_${…}` (`packages/core/src/variables.ts:251`)
uses an underscore where the objective id one file over uses a hyphen —
`` `obj-${Date.now().toString(36)}-${objectiveCounter.toString(36)}` ``
(`packages/core/src/objectives.ts:793`) — and `variables` is the structural twin of
`secrets`, which mints a bare UUID. The agent-facing argument names are worse: an agent
must learn four words for "which one" — `id` for objectives
(`packages/cli/src/runtime/tools.ts:361`), `slug` for the four registries (`:804`,
`:1030`), `channel` for channels (`:240`), and `deliveryId` for delivery receipts
(`:1526`). And the slug grammar has two disagreeing validators: `validateVariableSlug`
(`packages/core/src/variables.ts:406`) accepts 64 characters (`:410`) and the pattern
`^[a-z0-9][a-z0-9-]*$` (`:413`), which permits `foo--bar` and `foo-`, under a doc-comment
that claims the opposite — *"Slug grammar, matching the secrets store's"* (`:402`) —
while the request schema validates the same field against the strict 32-character
tool-source grammar.

### Query parameters and cursors

**Rule.** Query parameters are `snake_case`. JSON bodies and TypeScript fields stay
`camelCase`; CLI flags stay `kebab-case`. A paging cursor states its direction in its
name: `after_ts` / `after_id` for an oldest-first walk, `before_ts` / `before_id` for a
newest-first one. A scalar bound carries its arity in its name too — `before_ts`, not
`before`. Decided as D44.

The three-way projection already exists as a working template: `if_offline` on the query
string, `ifOffline` in the type, `--if-offline` on the command line, documented as a
table. Direction has to be in the name because today it is nowhere, and a client that
reuses one endpoint's paging loop against a sibling endpoint does not terminate.

**Broken today.** Three casings coexist on one surface. `c.req.query('stalledMs')`
(`packages/core/src/app.ts:1454`) and `c.req.query('clientKind')` / `('clientVersion')`
(`:4879-4880`) are camelCase on the wire, beside `cursor_ts` / `cursor_id` (`:2036-2037`)
and `if_offline` (`:4212`), which are not. Worse, the same two parameter names page in
opposite directions on sibling endpoints: the telemetry route maps them to
`after: { ts, id }` (`:2064`) and the activity route maps them to `before: { ts, id }`
(`:5219`), so a client that feeds the newest row back as `cursor_ts` on the oldest-first
walk advances the wrong way and never reaches the end of a live stream.

### Time fields

**Rule.** Four temporal names, each with one job.

- **`ts`** — the event's own instant, on the producer's clock. The agent's clock for
  activity, the provider's for telemetry, the broker's for broker-authored messages.
- **`receivedAt`** — the instant the broker took the record. A second clock, and the only
  one an operator can compare across members.
- **`durationMs`** — an elapsed span. Every duration ends in `Ms`; the unit belongs in
  the name.
- **`createdAt` / `updatedAt`** — when an *entity* came into existence and when its
  fields last changed. These belong on configuration rows the system owns, never on an
  ingested event, where the row's insertion time is `receivedAt` and the entity has no
  separate birth.

`completedAt` is the pattern for every other lifecycle instant: `<pastParticiple>At`,
set once when that transition happens, null before it. `expiresAt`, `archivedAt`,
`deliveredAt` and `lastUsedAt` are the same shape. An instant always ends in `At`; a
duration always ends in `Ms`.

The reason to keep `ts` and `receivedAt` apart is stated once in the tree and should be
read as the family rule: *"Server receive time (epoch ms); `ts` is the capture time."*
(`packages/sdk/src/types.ts:2188`). Capture health depends on the split, because agent
clocks differ and broker receipt is the single clock.

**Broken today.** `ActivityRow.createdAt` (`packages/sdk/src/types.ts:2689`) is broker
receipt time wearing the entity-creation name. Its column is `created_at`
(`packages/core/src/member-activity.ts:59`), capture health deliberately bounds on it
rather than on `ts` — *"Capture health bounds on created_at, not ts"* (`:66`) — and a
consumer that reads it the way `Objective.createdAt` (`types.ts:1754`) reads is looking
at a clock the agent never saw, off by a whole upload batch. In the same union,
`ActivityLlmExchange.duration` (`:2413`) is documented as *"Milliseconds between request
start and response end"* while its two siblings in that union spell the identical field
`durationMs`.

### Failure vocabulary

**Rule.** Failure is not one word, and adopting one would flatten distinctions the code
argues for. Four rules keep the registers apart.

- **`error` is thrown, in-process, and never a wire noun.** An `*Error` class is
  something a code path raises and a `catch` handles. On the wire, `error` carries a
  human-readable message and nothing else.
- **`fault code` is the machine token**, carried in a field named `code`. A caller
  branches on `code`; it never parses `error`.
- **Loss words and refusal words are disjoint.** `lost`, `dropped` (as an accident),
  `quarantined`, `truncated`, `evicted` and `gap` describe evidence that went missing.
  `refused`, `rejected`, `declined` and `filtered` describe a choice the system made.
  A word may not cross the line: policy is not loss, and loss is not policy.
- **One casing rule for codes.** `snake_case` for wire and stored codes; dotted
  `<module>.<event>` where a code must name its emitter; `UPPERCASE` reserved for CLI
  operator output; errno values passed through verbatim.

Reading two surfaces and being unable to predict the shape of a third is the whole cost
here, and it falls entirely on the client author.

**Broken today.** The enrollment poll puts a machine code in the field reserved for a
message — `return c.json({ error: 'authorization_pending' as const }, 400)`
(`packages/core/src/app.ts:6042`) — and two of that route's four values describe a
perfectly healthy poll. This one is externally mandated by RFC 8628 and is the ontology's
single named exception; the domain layer underneath it already avoids the word, calling
the same union `PollOutcome.kind`. On casing, `QuarantineReason = 'invalid-utf8' |
'oversized' | 'unreadable'` (`packages/cli/src/runtime/trace/otlp-relay.ts:61`) is
kebab-case where `RUNNER_CONDITION_CODES` (`packages/sdk/src/types.ts:332-339`) is
snake_case, for the same class of thing. And `dropped` sits on both sides of the
loss/refusal line at once: the run summary's counter is documented as *"Events lost:
overflow-evicted, rejected after close, or dropped at final flush"*
(`packages/cli/src/runtime/trace/activity-uploader.ts:90`), an accident, while
`NotificationDeliveryStatus`'s `'dropped'` is *"Every member target was offline and the
policy said drop"* (`packages/sdk/src/types.ts:1251`), a decision. Rename the counter
**`lost`**, which is the word its own comment already uses.

### Values

**Rule.** Enum values are lower `snake_case` on the wire and in durable columns. The
casing is a layer signal and already reliable: `kebab-case` is client-only view state,
`UPPERCASE` is CLI operator output, `dotted.lower` is a permission leaf. A value that
crosses the wire or lands in a column is `snake_case`, full stop.

**Records of what happened are past participle** — `completed`, `bound`, `member_added`
— because an action record answers "what happened to this row", not "what should be
done". Imperative values belong on requests, where the caller is asking for something.
US spelling throughout (D34).

**Broken today.** Three tenses live in one file. `ChannelAuditAction`
(`packages/sdk/src/types.ts:715-722`) is imperative — `'create' | 'rename' |
'description' | 'member_add' | 'member_remove' | 'member_role' | 'archive'` — and two of
its members are not actions at all: `'description'` and `'member_role'` record *which
field changed*, inside an enum whose siblings record what happened. In the same file,
`ObjectiveEventKind` (`:1778-1786`) is past participle — `'assigned'`, `'blocked'`,
`'completed'`, `'watcher_added'` — and so is `EnvironmentEvent.action`
(`:2536`): `'value_set' | 'bound' | 'unbound'`. A consumer rendering an audit trail
cannot write one formatter. These are stored values, so aligning the channel enum costs a
migration; whether to pay it is D51.

## People and seats

There is exactly one stored team participant — a row in the `members` table — and the
codebase reaches it under eight nouns: *member*, *teammate*, *seat*, *slot*, *user*,
*agent*, *viewer* and *director*. The distinction that matters: **the seat is a member;
what occupies it is a person or an agent; the thing that grants access is a permission
leaf.** None of those three is a role, and none of them is the connection the seat is
currently reachable on.

### Member

A member is a seat on the team: a name, a role, a private instructions block, a set of
permissions, and credentials to act with. People and agents hold the same kind of seat —
"the identity is the member, not the process" ([Members](/docs/concepts/members)) — and
the `members` table has no kind column to separate them.

- **Names** — SDK `Member`, `CreateMemberRequest`, `UpdateMemberRequest`,
  `ListMembersResponse` · core `LoadedMember`, `MemberStore`, `SqliteMemberStore` · DB
  `members` · route `GET /members`, `POST /members`, `PATCH /members/:name`,
  `DELETE /members/:name` · CLI `csuite member list|create|update|delete` · MCP
  `members_add` (becomes `members_create`, D42) `members_update` `members_remove`
  (becomes `members_delete`, D42) · UI "Members" · docs
  [Members](/docs/concepts/members)
- **Not** — Teammate (the public projection of this same row, without `instructions`) ·
  Channel membership label value `member` (a per-channel row value, owned by
  access-and-authority) · `Role.title` default `'member'` (`DEFAULT_MEMBER_ROLE_TITLE`
  in `apps/server/src/wizard.ts:50`, an editable job label, so a fresh team's roster line
  reads `member  member`) · Member kind (whether the occupant is a person or an agent —
  a property of the seat, never the seat) · "Director" (no such concept — `isDirector` in
  `Transcript.tsx:49` is `permissions.includes('members.manage')`, and the word is live in
  `ObjectiveCreate.tsx:242`, six MCP tool descriptions in `packages/cli/src/runtime/tools.ts`
  and the login placeholder `director-1`; D40 names the set by its leaf, and
  `bootstrap-member-permissions.test.ts` already forbids persona nouns in the permission
  vocabulary) · "slot" (a retired synonym that is not comment-only: it reaches agents in
  the MCP roster empty state `team roster: (no slots defined)` and in the `fs_*` tool
  descriptions) · "user" (internal only — `handleUser` behind the `member` verb, plus the
  `member.ts` header's `csuite enroll --user`, which names a flag that does not exist; it
  is `--member`) · `persistMembers` (not the member store: a no-op call whose *presence*
  gates six mutating routes with a 501 that names an internal hook to an HTTP caller)
- **Verbs** — get, list, create, update, delete

### Teammate

The public projection of a member — "the subset visible to other members in the roster
and instruction packet", omitting the personal `instructions` field. It is a shape, not
a second kind of participant.

- **Names** — SDK `Teammate`, `TeammateSchema` · core `teammatesFromMembers()` · route
  `GET /roster` → `RosterResponse.teammates`, `GET /instructions` →
  `InstructionsResponse.teammates`, `GET /team/status` → `TeamStatusMember.member`,
  `POST /members` → `CreateMemberResponse.member` · CLI `csuite roster` · MCP `roster` ·
  docs [MCP tools](/docs/dev/mcp-tools)
- **Not** — Member (the full record, adding `instructions`; what a `members.manage`
  holder reads from `GET /members`) · member (the seat itself — never write "teammate"
  as a bare synonym for the row) · Connection presence (live connection state for the
  same name)

`Member extends Teammate` in the type layer, but nothing on the wire matches the
declaration. `teammatesFromMembers` emits `kind` and `loadedToMember` does not, and
neither emits `identityId`. So a caller holding `members.manage` gets `GET /members` rows
*without* `kind` while a caller without it gets rows *with* it — under one declared
response type, `ListMembersResponse { members: Member[] }`, which the second branch does
not return.

### Member kind

Whether a seat is held by a person or by an agent. Derived server-side at one function
from one fact — the row has a TOTP secret: "the auth plane is the only person/agent
signal we have: humans enroll TOTP for the web UI, agents authenticate by bearer token
alone." An agent seat that enrolls TOTP is silently reclassified as a person, because
nothing else is consulted.

- **Names** — SDK `Teammate.kind` · schema `TeammateSchema.kind` · core
  `teammatesFromMembers()` (the only writer) · web-UI `memberKind()`, `data-kind`
  attribute, `senderTextClass()` · docs [Members](/docs/concepts/members),
  [Glossary](/docs/concepts/glossary)
- **Not** — Agent (execution-runtime: the LLM child process a runner spawns and holds a
  handle to) · `ActivityLlmExchange.agent` / `ActivityToolAction.agent` (which capture
  framework produced a record — a free `string`, `'claude'` or `'codex'` in practice, not
  a member and not an enum) · `GenAiInference.agentName` (a *named subagent* inside one
  run — `general-purpose` for the builtin; null for the main thread, and never a member
  name) · `ClientIdentity.kind` (what a live connection claims it is; its `runner` value
  is a client kind, not an occupant kind) · `credentialMode` (how a credential is minted:
  `bootstrap` or `pending`) · Member (the seat itself — `csuite push --agent <name>` and
  the "VIEW AGENT" button in `Transcript.tsx:145` both address a member, which may be a
  person; the canonical spelling is member)
- **Values** — `person` — the row has a TOTP secret · *absent* — it does not, and
  consumers render the neutral (agent) treatment rather than guessing. `'agent'` is
  declared in the union and in the zod enum and no code path emits it;
  `apps/server/test/teammate-kind.test.ts` pins "absent, not `'agent'`".
- **Decided** — D2: `kind` is a heuristic over the auth plane and is named for what it
  measures, over promoting person/agent to a stored `members.kind` column (2026-09-03)

### Team role

A member's job description: a short freeform title plus a prose description, shared
publicly in the roster and in every member's instruction packet. It is descriptive only
— permissions decide what a member can do. CommandSuite has no RBAC role.

- **Names** — SDK `Role {title, description}`, `RoleSchema` · DB `members.role_title`,
  `members.role_description` · route `PATCH /members/:name` · CLI `csuite member
  create|update --title --description` · MCP `members_add` / `members_update` params
  `title`, `description` · UI "Role title", "Role description" · docs
  [Members](/docs/concepts/members)
- **Not** — Permission leaf (the only thing that grants access; owned by
  access-and-authority) · Channel membership label (`ChannelMemberRole`, `admin | member`
  on a channel row, whose `admin` value is legacy observational only — `channels.manage`
  authorizes) · `Presence.role` (a first-register-wins copy stamped at connect: "the
  registry is authoritative about online/offline, not about role changes", so after a role
  edit `connected[].role` disagrees with `teammates[].role` in the same response until the
  member's last connection drops) · `AnthropicMessage.role` (the provider chat turn — a
  free `string`, `system` / `user` / `assistant` / `tool` in practice) · Persona (the
  route-walk permission tier `baseline | admin`, spelled `--role` in
  `scripts/route-walk/walk.mjs` and `scripts/release-prepare.mjs` today; contributor
  vocabulary) · Role description (the instruction block that carries this same string) ·
  "role tokens" (the `--ef-*` design-system variables the web-ui changelog calls that;
  the tokens themselves are `--ef-border`, `--ef-text`, and name no role)
- **Values** — `title`: 1 to 64 characters, freeform and not enumerable — "engineer",
  "qa-lead", "researcher" are the doc-comment's examples, not a set ·
  `description`: freeform prose, no upper bound, defaults to `''`
- **Verbs** — update
- **Decided** — D36: route-walk's `--role` becomes `--persona`, over keeping `--role`
  and disambiguating it in prose (2026-09-03). The flag is a contributor script fixture
  with no wire, database or published-CLI cost, and freeing the word is the point.

### Roster

The team's member list joined to live presence, in one response: `teammates`,
`connected`, `restartPending`, and `activityWindowMs` — the window the broker applied when
deciding whether a work-state report is recent.

- **Names** — SDK `RosterResponse` · route `GET /roster` · CLI `csuite roster` · MCP
  `roster` · web-UI `roster` signal
- **Not** — the `members` table (storage; its own comment calls itself "roster" and
  should not) · `GET /members` (the management list — full `Member` records for a
  `members.manage` holder, with no presence attached) · `GET /team/status` (per-member
  objectives and stall verdicts) · Connection presence (live connection state on its own)
- **Verbs** — list

### Viewer identity

Who the signed-in viewer is inside this team, as the host has already resolved it: member
name, role, permissions, and when the authentication expires. `Identity` is the resolved
value the shell reads; `SessionState` is the host's `loading` / `anonymous` /
`authenticated` machine that produces it. Login, logout and expiry are the host's
responsibility, not the shell's.

- **Names** — web-UI `Identity {member, role, permissions, expiresAt?}`, prop `viewer`
  (the signed-in member's name) · web-host `SessionState` · SDK `SessionResponse` · route
  `GET /session`, `POST /session/totp`, `POST /session/logout` · core `c.get('member')`
  (a `LoadedMember` on the server request context)
- **Not** — Client identity (`ClientIdentity` / `RunnerIdentity` — observational facts a
  subscriber reports, which never authorize) · Identity id (the stable member id, never
  emitted) · Member (the stored row; `Identity` is a client-side resolved copy of part
  of it)

### Client identity

The typed, observational identity a subscriber reports during its presence handshake:
which kind of client it is, its build version, and for a runner the full
`RunnerIdentity`. "Never authorizes" — it describes a connection, not a seat, and one
member can hold several connections reporting different client identities.

- **Names** — SDK `ClientIdentity`, `ClientReport`, `RunnerIdentity`, `RunnerReport`,
  header `X-CSUITE-Client-Identity` (`CLIENT_IDENTITY_HEADER`) · core
  `IdentityContext.clientIdentity` · route `GET /roster` → `Presence.clientReports`,
  `Presence.runnerReports` · docs [Presence](/docs/concepts/presence)
- **Not** — Viewer identity (`Identity` — who is signed in, and what the UI gates on) ·
  Identity id (the stable member id) · Member (the seat) · Permission leaf (the only
  thing that authorizes) · Member kind (person vs agent, derived from TOTP enrollment,
  not from what a connection claims)
- **Values** — `kind`: `runner` `browser` `cli` `sdk`. A `browser`, `cli` or `sdk` client
  reports `clientVersion` and nothing else. A runner reports its whole `RunnerIdentity` —
  the runner verb (`claude`, `codex`, `stub`), the model id the adapter resolved, the
  CommandSuite version, whether the artifact was built from npm or main, its optional
  protocol claims, and an optional supervision claim that is "observational only; never
  authorizes".

## Access and authority

Authority in CommandSuite is one flat set of **permission leaves** held by a
member and checked by the broker on every request. There is no rank, no tier and
no RBAC role: every other word in this family that sounds like authority — `role`,
`admin`, template, preset, capability — either describes something the holder does
or negotiates something between processes, and none of them grants anything. A
leaf is held by a *member*; `canWrite` is computed for a *viewer and one object*;
a protocol capability is advertised by a *process*.

### Permission leaf

One dotted, resource-first string naming one elevated action. "The set of
elevated actions gated by membership policy. Baseline participation … is NOT a
permission — it's what it means to be on the team. Only actions that touch other
members or shape the team itself are permissions." `PERMISSIONS` in
`packages/sdk/src/types.ts` is the vocabulary: `PermissionSchema`,
`PermissionLeafSchema` and the CLI's leaf list derive from it, and a parity test
holds `PERMISSION_META` to it. The prose lists did not derive from it and had
drifted three ways, each page listing a different set under a heading that
counted a third. Under D14 exactly one docs table survives — the one in
[Permissions](/docs/concepts/permissions) — and `scripts/check-vocabulary.mjs`
holds it to `PERMISSIONS`, refuses a second enumeration anywhere in `docs/`, and
refuses prose that names a count at all.

- **Names** — SDK `Permission`, `PERMISSIONS`, `PermissionSchema`,
  `PermissionsSchema` · core `PermissionLeafSchema` · DB JSON strings inside
  `members.raw_permissions` · route literal arguments to `hasPermission` · CLI
  `csuite member create --permissions <leaf,...>` · MCP `members_add`
  (D42: becomes `members_create`) and `members_update`, field `permissions`
  ("Permission leaves.") · UI checkbox grid built from `PERMISSION_META`; the
  member profile renders each held leaf as a badge whose tooltip is `labelFor`
  · docs [Permissions](/docs/concepts/permissions)
- **Not** — Protocol capability (feature negotiation between processes; the
  glossary's "a named capability a member holds" is this entry and should say
  leaf) · `canWrite` (computed per file entry per viewer, never held) · Role (a
  member's display label, which gates nothing) · Legacy permission preset and
  creation template (named sets *of* leaves, not leaves) · Channel membership
  label `admin` (a decorative per-channel value) · "Director", still in MCP tool
  copy an agent reads, which means "holds `members.manage`" and is not a
  category
- **Values** — `PERMISSIONS`, enumerated once in
  [Permissions](/docs/concepts/permissions) and nowhere else (D14). Three of the
  names understate what they grant, and those glosses live here:
  `members.context` is control over *another* member's runner context, since
  doing it to your own is baseline; `objectives.create` grants creating **and**
  reading the team-wide objective ledger; and rows written before the rename
  still say `process.manage`, which resolves to `team_process.manage`
- **Verbs** — granted by writing the whole list on `POST /members` or
  `PATCH /members/:name`; checked with `hasPermission`; never granted
  individually
- **Decided** — D40: name the set by the leaf, "`members.manage` holder", over
  reviving "Director" (or "admin") as the informal noun for it (2026-09-03)

### Resolved permissions

The deduplicated, canonically ordered `Permission[]` a member effectively holds —
"Resolved leaf permissions (presets expanded)", with retired names mapped forward
by `LEGACY_PERMISSION_EXPANSIONS`. Every gate reads this array, and no column
stores it: it is recomputed on every read.

- **Names** — SDK `Teammate.permissions`, `Member.permissions` · core
  `resolvePermissions(raw, presets, context)` · CLI `csuite member list`
  permissions column · MCP roster line ` permissions=a,b,c;` · UI the leaf
  badges in the member profile, ordered by `sortLeaves`
- **Not** — Raw permissions (the verbatim strings on disk, which may still name a
  preset or a retired leaf) · Baseline (this array when it is empty) ·
  `AddMemberInput.permissions`, which is documented as this array and is honored
  by only one of the two member stores: `SqliteMemberStore.addMember` ignores it
  and re-resolves from `rawPermissions`, while the in-memory
  `MapMemberStore.addMember` writes it verbatim. Pass a value here that
  disagrees with `rawPermissions` and which store you are on decides the answer
- **Verbs** — read only; it is derived, never assigned

### Raw permissions

The verbatim strings on the member row: "Preset names + leaf permissions as
written on disk; preserved for round-tripping." It has no wire name and should
keep none — the write path normalizes first and persists
`rawPermissions: [...resolvedPerms]`, so only a caller bypassing HTTP can store
a retired name today. *(defined in code only)*

- **Names** — core `LoadedMember.rawPermissions`, `RawPermissionsSchema`,
  `validateRawPermissions()`, `AddMemberInput.rawPermissions` · DB column
  `members.raw_permissions TEXT NOT NULL`
- **Not** — Resolved permissions (what the same row resolves to on read) · the
  request field `permissions` on `POST /members`, which is validated by
  `PermissionsSchema` — a legacy-alias preprocess wrapping
  `z.array(PermissionSchema)`. It accepts the two legacy aliases and rejects a
  preset name outright, contradicting the comment above the store call that says
  preset names are accepted as compatibility input

### Legacy permission alias

A retired permission key mapped forward to what replaced it, applied on every
wire parse and every read and never written back: "Team configs and stored
presets written before the consolidation carry these; every permission parse and
resolution path maps them forward so an existing team loads unchanged." Two
entries exist, and they are two different operations riding one table — one
aggregate expansion and one rename. *(defined in code only)*

- **Names** — SDK `LEGACY_PERMISSION_EXPANSIONS`, the `z.preprocess` step inside
  `PermissionsSchema` · core the alias branch of `resolvePermissions`, applied
  both to a direct member entry and to a leaf inside a preset · docs "resolve to
  this leaf unchanged" in [Permissions](/docs/concepts/permissions)
- **Not** — a migration; no row is ever rewritten, which is why a member saved
  under an old name needs no deployment step · Legacy permission preset (a named
  bundle looked up by name, not a key mapped to a replacement) · a deprecation
  window; both names are accepted on write today
- **Values** — `objectives.manage` → every `objectives.*` leaf, an
  aggregate that no longer exists as one leaf · `process.manage` →
  `team_process.manage`, a one-to-one rename. The constant's name describes the
  first case only

### Baseline

Holding zero permission leaves: a full team member with no elevated authority.
It is not a stored state, a preset, or a default set — it is the absence of
leaves, and the surfaces that print the word all test `permissions.length === 0`.

- **Names** — CLI `csuite member list` prints `baseline` · UI "baseline" button
  (clears the selection) and `PermissionSummary.kind: 'baseline'` · web host
  "Baseline" button on the enrollment create form · docs
  [Permissions](/docs/concepts/permissions),
  [CLI reference](/docs/reference/cli) ("Omitted means baseline")
- **Not** — a permission that everyone holds; baseline participation is gated by
  nothing at all · the route-walk persona named `baseline`, which is contributor
  tooling for a seeded member holding no leaves · the mutation-testing baseline
  in the contributor gates documentation, which is a reference run · "matches the
  default permission set", a reading the stale `PermissionSummary` doc-comment
  invites and the implementation does not support

### Legacy permission preset

A durable, server-side, named bundle of leaves in the `permission_presets` table,
referenced by name inside a member's raw permissions and expanded on every read.
"Legacy named bundles retained only to read teams created by older versions." At
HEAD no shipped surface can create one: `setPreset` and `deletePreset` are
reachable from a test helper and from no route, command or wizard, and no wire
shape accepts a preset name.

- **Names** — SDK `PermissionPresets`, `Team.permissionPresets` (`@deprecated`),
  `PermissionPresetsSchema` · DB table
  `permission_presets(name, permissions, updated_at, updated_by)` · core
  `getPresets()`, the preset branch of `resolvePermissions`
- **Not** — Creation template (a client-side copy that survives nothing) · a
  role, tier or category; expanding a preset produces leaves and the reference is
  invisible afterwards · Legacy permission alias (a fixed map in code, not a row
  an operator ever wrote). Never call either one a "bundle" — that word is the
  third name for this row and is retired

### Creation template

A labeled set of leaves the create-member form copies into the selection once.
"Permission templates are a create-form convenience only: applying one copies its
leaves into the selection and creates no durable role or privilege category."
Nothing about the template survives the click.

- **Names** — UI `PermissionTemplate`, `MEMBER_CREATION_PERMISSION_TEMPLATES`,
  "Templates" label, tooltip "Copy the \"X\" permission template" · web host the
  same buttons on the enrollment create form · docs "Creation templates" in
  [Permissions](/docs/concepts/permissions); the
  [glossary](/docs/concepts/glossary) headword is "Permission template"
- **Not** — Legacy permission preset (durable, server-side, and expanded at every
  read, so editing it changes every member that names it) · Permission leaf · the
  leaf table's sentence "Members and templates saved under its former name …
  resolve to this leaf unchanged", which means presets: nothing about a template
  is saved
- **Values** — `Full access`: every leaf in `PERMISSIONS` · `Coordinate
  objectives`: the four `objectives.*` leaves

### Permission gate

The server-side check that a caller's resolved permissions contain a required
leaf, plus the 403 it returns. "The server checks permissions on every action,
every time … the server's check is the boundary."

- **Names** — SDK `hasPermission(permissions, required)` · route 403 bodies such
  as `'channels.manage is required to administer channels'` and
  `'creating members requires the members.manage permission'` · UI
  `<ErrorCallout title="Restricted">` · docs "Enforcement" in
  [Permissions](/docs/concepts/permissions)
- **Not** — the trimmed MCP tool list and the disabled UI control, which are
  courtesies on top of the gate and decide nothing · Relationship bypass (lets a
  caller through with no leaf at all) · Sole-holder invariant (refuses a
  resulting state, not a caller) · `canWrite` (answers a question about one file
  entry, not about the caller)
- **Verbs** — `hasPermission` is the one spelling; `permissions.includes(...)`
  through the CLI runtime is the same check written differently, and is why
  finding every gate currently takes two greps

### Relationship bypass

An allowance that turns on the caller's relationship to the object — self,
originator, assignee, watcher — rather than on a leaf. "Self is baseline,
someone else needs the leaf … Reaching into a TEAMMATE's running conversation is
the elevated act."

- **Names** — core `isSelf`, `isOriginator`, `isParticipant` (assignee,
  originator or watcher) short-circuits ahead of `hasPermission` · route 403
  `'context control requires members.context, or self'` · docs "Owner and self
  exceptions" in [Permissions](/docs/concepts/permissions); auth labels
  "manage OR self", "read OR self", "self" in the
  [REST reference](/docs/dev/rest-api); "an explicitly documented relationship
  bypass such as self or objective originator" in
  [Architecture](/docs/dev/architecture)
- **Not** — Baseline (a property of a member's permission set; a bypass is a
  property of one request) · an implicit leaf; a bypass is enumerated per route
  and confers nothing anywhere else · a blanket owner rule: reassignment has no
  bypass, so moving your own objective to someone else still needs
  `objectives.reassign`

### Sole-holder invariant

The rule that a team must keep at least one member holding `members.manage`:
"Preserve at least one holder of the membership-management capability." Deleting
that member, removing the leaf from them, or revoking their last bearer token is
refused with 409, and the web UI disables the control rather than letting the
request be made.

- **Names** — route errors `'cannot remove members.manage from its sole holder —
  grant it to another member first'`, `'cannot delete the sole members.manage
  holder — grant it to another member first'`, `'cannot revoke the last token of
  the sole members.manage holder — grant it to another member first'` · SDK
  `updateMember` doc-comment ("Enforces the \"at least one member with
  `members.manage` must remain\" invariant") · MCP `members_update`, on its
  `permissions` field ("Enforces \"at least one members.manage holder
  remains\".") · UI prop `isSoleMemberManager` · docs
  [Permissions](/docs/concepts/permissions),
  [CLI reference](/docs/reference/cli)
- **Not** — Permission gate (refuses a *caller* who lacks a leaf; this refuses a
  resulting *state*, and it fires on callers who hold every leaf) · Bootstrap
  member (the first member is usually the sole holder, but the invariant is about
  the leaf and follows it to whoever holds it)

### Bootstrap member

The first member a deployment creates, granted `[...PERMISSIONS]` — every leaf —
so that someone can grant the rest. Its authority is the leaf set and nothing
else: the name is a convention (`CSUITE_ADMIN`, default `admin`), no code reads
it, and a second every-leaf member is an ordinary `members.manage` create.
*(defined in code only)*

- **Names** — server `bootstrapMember` from the first-run wizard · CLI
  `csuite setup` (`seedBootstrap`; role title `member`, `BOOTSTRAP_ROLE_TITLE`)
  · env `CSUITE_ADMIN` in `compose.yaml` and `scripts/bootstrap.sh` ("bootstrap
  member (holds every permission). Default: admin") · docs
  [Deployment](/docs/deployment) ("First member (holds every permission); names
  the secret files.")
- **Not** — an `admin` role, tier or category; the retired member categories
  were replaced by leaves and a test forbids `director`, `admin` and `operator`
  from re-entering the leaf vocabulary · Channel membership label `admin` · the
  route-walk persona `admin`, contributor tooling that walks *as* this member ·
  a permanent status; every leaf it holds can be removed from it, subject only
  to the sole-holder invariant

### Channel membership label

The per-channel `role` value on a channel membership row — `admin` or `member` —
reported back to the viewer as `myRole`. It is read only to be displayed and to
sort admins first: "Legacy observational value; grants no administrative
authority." Every channel administration route gates on `channels.manage`
instead. It is still writable on a fresh membership, so it is inert rather than
gone.

- **Names** — SDK `ChannelMemberRole`, `ChannelMember.role`,
  `ChannelSummary.myRole`, `AddChannelMemberRequestSchema.role`
  (`ChannelMemberRoleSchema.default('member')`, still accepts `'admin'` on
  `POST /channels/:slug/members`) · DB
  `channel_members.role TEXT NOT NULL CHECK(role IN ('admin','member'))` · core
  `roleOf()` · UI the membership row in channel settings · docs
  [Channels](/docs/concepts/channels)
- **Not** — Permission leaf `channels.manage`, which is the actual authority over
  a channel · Role, the member's job label carried in the roster · Bootstrap
  member, conventionally *named* `admin`, whose authority comes from holding
  every leaf
- **Values** — `admin`: displayed, sorted first, grants nothing · `member`: the
  default and the only value a new membership needs

### Protocol capability

An additive feature flag one process advertises to another so that peers of
different versions can negotiate. "Absence means an old runner, never support."
This is the only sense of the word "capability" with a `capabilities` identifier
behind it, and the ontology reserves the word for it.

- **Names** — SDK `HealthResponse.capabilities` · route `GET /healthz` returning
  `capabilities: { rawBodyAck: true }` · docs `deliveryProtocol` and
  `livenessProtocol` in the [REST reference](/docs/dev/rest-api)
- **Not** — Permission leaf (member authority; the glossary currently calls a
  leaf "a named capability") · `canWrite` · the "capability-gated" management
  tools in the [MCP tools](/docs/dev/mcp-tools) page, which means gated on a
  permission leaf · the `CAPABILITY` class in the contributor-side web-UI gate
  audit, which that document already records does not mean server capability

## Assigned work

An objective is the one record CommandSuite keeps for assigned work: who holds it,
what "done" means, and every transition it made. Three separate records trail behind
each one — the append-only `objective_events` audit log, the `obj:<id>` discussion
thread, and the `objective_open`/`objective_close` markers on the assignee's activity
stream — and the word "lifecycle" has been used for all three. They are different
things written by different layers, and this family keeps them apart.

### Objective

"An objective is assigned work with a definition of done: its `outcome` says what must
be true when you finish" (`packages/core/src/instructions.ts:278`). It is push-assigned
and single-assignee: bound to exactly one member at creation, with no unclaimed queue
and no claim verb.

- **Names** — SDK `Objective`, `ObjectiveSchema`, client `getObjective` /
  `listObjectives` / `createObjective` / `updateObjective` / `completeObjective` /
  `cancelObjective` / `reassignObjective` · DB `objectives` · routes `GET /objectives`,
  `POST /objectives`, `GET /objectives/:id`, `PATCH /objectives/:id`,
  `POST /objectives/:id/complete`, `POST /objectives/:id/cancel`,
  `POST /objectives/:id/reassign`, `POST /objectives/:id/discuss` · CLI
  `csuite objectives` · MCP `objectives_list` `objectives_view` `objectives_create`
  `objectives_update` `objectives_reassign` `objectives_discuss` `objectives_complete`
  `objectives_cancel` ·
  UI nav label "Objectives", pages `/objectives`, `/objectives/new`,
  `/objectives/{objectiveId}` · docs [Objectives](/docs/concepts/objectives)
- **Not** — Job (a member's standing role; members are durable identities, and
  "if you're creating members per task, you wanted objectives",
  `docs/guides/multi-agent-team.mdx:181-182`) · Channel post or DM (an ask with no
  record, no assignee and no definition of done) · "Apex task primitive" (the retired
  framing; the SDK doc-comment now carries the shipped definition — "assigned work with
  a definition of done: push-assigned, outcome-required, single-assignee",
  `packages/sdk/src/types.ts:1727-1729`)
- **Verbs** — `list` · `get` (SDK `getObjective`, route `GET /objectives/:id`, MCP
  `objectives_view`) · `create` · `update` · `complete` · `cancel` · `discuss` ·
  `reassign`. Reassignment has two wire spellings, deliberately identical —
  `PATCH /objectives/:id` with `assignee`, and `POST /objectives/:id/reassign` with
  `{to, note}` — same gate, same unknown-assignee 400, same idempotent no-op when the
  target is already the assignee, so the two "can never disagree"
  (`packages/core/src/app.ts:4707-4712`). The CLI, the MCP tool and the store method all
  take the dedicated route.

### Outcome

The tangible condition that must be true for the work to count as done, written by the
originator at creation. It is required non-empty, and no code path updates the column —
the only statement that writes `outcome` is `insertStmt`
(`packages/core/src/objectives.ts:367-371`), so an outcome that turns out wrong is
cancelled and replaced, not edited.

- **Names** — SDK `Objective.outcome` · DB `objectives.outcome` (`NOT NULL`) · CLI
  `csuite objectives create --outcome` · MCP `objectives_create.outcome` · UI "Outcome",
  help text "The definition of done — short and checkable, naming who verifies" · docs
  [Objectives](/docs/concepts/objectives), [Glossary](/docs/concepts/glossary)
- **Not** — Result (the completion claim the assignee writes at `done`, argued against
  this) · Body (optional background; `objectives.body`, UI "Body (optional)") ·
  Block reason (why work stopped, not what finishing means). The glossary and the
  concepts page both define this correctly. Where the word still slips is the surface an
  agent reads: `objectives_create.outcome` describes it as "the tangible **result** that
  defines 'done'" (`packages/cli/src/runtime/tools.ts:1928`) and `Objective.outcome`'s own
  comment says "the tangible **outcome** that defines 'done'"
  (`packages/sdk/src/types.ts:1739`) — one of them borrows the name of the field beside
  it, and the tool text is the one an agent learns from.

### Result

The completion claim: what was actually delivered, written by the assignee when the
objective moves to `done` and read against the outcome by whoever verifies. Required
non-empty after trim at completion (`packages/core/src/objectives.ts:563-566`), and
written by no other path.

- **Names** — SDK `Objective.result` · DB `objectives.result` · route
  `POST /objectives/:id/complete` · CLI `csuite objectives complete --result` · MCP
  `objectives_complete.result` · UI "Result", help text "How was the outcome met? Argue
  it against the definition of done on the left" · docs
  [Objectives](/docs/concepts/objectives)
- **Not** — Outcome (the acceptance criteria this argues against) ·
  `ActivityObjectiveClose.result` (the terminal-state hint on an activity marker, values
  `done` `cancelled` `reassigned` `runner_shutdown`, of which the runner only ever
  writes `done` — it stamps the default unconditionally because the tracker cannot tell
  the three apart, `packages/cli/src/runtime/runner.ts:626-633`; joining it to this field
  yields the string `"done"` where a reviewer expects the completion narrative) ·
  `ObjectivesMutationResult` (the core store's return envelope `{objective, events}`,
  `packages/core/src/objectives.ts:213-216`, not a field on the wire)

### Objective status

The four-state machine the store enforces on every transition: "`done` and `cancelled`
are terminal; `active ↔ blocked` is the only back-and-forth" (`packages/core/src/
objectives.ts:13-16`). The same four values are literal in the SDK type, the SQLite
`CHECK`, the CLI validator and the UI badge maps.

- **Names** — SDK `ObjectiveStatus`, `ObjectiveStatusSchema` · DB
  `objectives.status CHECK(status IN ('active','blocked','done','cancelled'))` · query
  `GET /objectives?status=` · CLI `csuite objectives list --status`,
  `csuite objectives update --status` · MCP `objectives_update.status` (narrowed to
  `active` and `blocked`) · UI `STATUS_BADGE`
  (`packages/web-ui/src/components/ObjectivesPanel.tsx:39-44`) and `StatusBadge`
  (`packages/web-ui/src/components/ObjectiveDetail.tsx:1111-1119`) · docs
  [Objectives](/docs/concepts/objectives)
- **Not** — `open` (not a status: the `active`+`blocked` union, accepted as a fifth
  value by `objectives_list.status` and applied client-side because the wire enum has
  four — see Plate) · Stalled (a derived silence verdict, not a stored state) · Work
  state (`WorkState` — the runner's live agent state, a different family; under D6 the
  wire field is still spelled `Presence.activity`, `packages/sdk/src/types.ts:264`) ·
  "Closed" (the UI's collapsed `done`+`cancelled` section in `ObjectivesPanel`; the
  permission leaf's word "ledger" means the team-wide list of all objectives, not that
  section)
- **Values** — `status`: `active` (the assignee holds it) · `blocked` (`blockReason`
  says what stopped it) · `done` (terminal; only the assignee sets it, and only with a
  `result`) · `cancelled` (terminal; the work is no longer wanted)
- **Verbs** — `update` moves `active ↔ blocked` only, and only for the assignee or an
  `objectives.cancel` holder · `complete` is the sole path to `done` · `cancel` is the
  sole path to `cancelled`

### Plate

A member's plate is the non-terminal work assigned to them — `active` plus `blocked`,
scoped by `assignee`. It is what the runner refetches on every objective event and what
`context_refresh` re-briefs the agent on after a restart or compaction. *(defined in
code only)*

- **Names** — SDK `InstructionsResponse.openObjectives` ("Objectives currently assigned
  to this member with status === 'active' or 'blocked'",
  `packages/sdk/src/types.ts:544-545`) · route `GET /objectives?assignee=<self>&status=active`
  + `&status=blocked` · CLI runtime `OPEN_OBJECTIVE_STATUSES`
  (`packages/cli/src/runtime/tools.ts:79`) · MCP `objectives_list` with
  `status: "open"` **and** `assignee: <self>` · agent instructions "your open plate"
- **Not** — Related objectives (`related=<name>` — assigned **or** originated **or**
  watching; wider than a plate for anyone who originates or watches work) · Open (the
  adjective for the two non-terminal statuses, not a set of objectives) ·
  `objectives_list` with `status: "open"` alone (that call sends `related=<self>` and
  returns the wider set — see below)
- **Verbs** — `list` only; a plate is a query, not a stored object
- The code and the tool text disagree, and the code wins: `objectives_list` calls its
  result "your whole open plate" but queries `related` and applies `assignee`
  client-side (`packages/cli/src/runtime/tools.ts:2593-2611`), so the plate comes back
  only when the caller also passes its own name. The re-brief and `openObjectives` are
  assignee-scoped without being asked.

### Assignee

The single member bound to an objective — stored `NOT NULL`, changed only by the
reassign path, and the only identity permitted to complete the work
(`packages/core/src/app.ts:4650-4652`).

- **Names** — SDK `Objective.assignee` · DB `objectives.assignee` · query
  `GET /objectives?assignee=` · CLI `csuite objectives create --assignee`,
  `csuite objectives reassign --to` · MCP `objectives_create.assignee`,
  `objectives_update.assignee` · UI "Assignee" · docs
  [Objectives](/docs/concepts/objectives)
- **Not** — Originator (who created it, and keeps standing over it) · Watcher (an
  explicit observer with no duty to finish) · "Slot" (an informal synonym for member in
  the CLI usage block, the runner and agent-facing tool text; the canonical word is
  member, and this same CLI's error messages already say `--assignee <name>`)
- **Verbs** — Reassign: two wire spellings of one act, deliberately identical —
  `PATCH /objectives/:id` with `assignee`, and `POST /objectives/:id/reassign` with
  `{to, note}`; same gate (`objectives.reassign`), same unknown-assignee 400, same
  idempotent no-op when the target is already the assignee, "so the two spellings of one
  act can never disagree" (`packages/core/src/app.ts:4707-4712`). Refused on a terminal
  objective. Unless the previous assignee is the originator or already a watcher,
  they are promoted to watcher in the same transaction with a `watcher_added` event
  carrying `{reason: 'reassigned-from'}`, so `watchers` is not purely author-supplied and
  repeated reassignment grows the list — an accepted cost, recorded at
  `packages/core/src/objectives.ts:646-648`.

### Originator

The member who created the objective, stamped by the server from the authenticated
caller and never taken from the request body (`packages/core/src/app.ts:4490`). The
originator keeps standing over the work: they may always cancel it
(`packages/core/src/app.ts:4678-4681`) and always manage its watchers
(`packages/core/src/app.ts:4580-4586`), with no leaf required.

- **Names** — SDK `Objective.originator` · DB `objectives.originator` · MCP
  `objectives_list` / `objectives_view` rows · docs
  [Objectives](/docs/concepts/objectives), [Glossary](/docs/concepts/glossary),
  [Permissions](/docs/concepts/permissions)
- **Not** — Assignee (who executes it; the two are frequently the same member and are
  still separate fields) · Channel creator (provenance without authority — "Creation is
  provenance, not authority: the creator gets no special administrative standing",
  `docs/concepts/channels.mdx:21-22`; the asymmetry is deliberate and neither name should
  be normalized into the other) · `members.manage` holder (standing over every objective,
  granted by a permission leaf rather than by having created this one)

### Watcher

A member explicitly added to an objective who receives every lifecycle event and every
discussion post without being the assignee. Watchers are a stored list; the code grants
them read **and** write on `/objectives/<id>/` through the objective-membership ACL
(`packages/core/src/files/filesystem-store.ts:332-342`).

- **Names** — SDK `Objective.watchers` · DB `objectives.watchers` (JSON) · wire
  `addWatchers` / `removeWatchers` on `PATCH /objectives/:id` · events `watcher_added`,
  `watcher_removed` · MCP `objectives_update.addWatchers` / `.removeWatchers` · UI
  "Watchers", "Initial watchers (optional)" · permission `objectives.watch` · docs
  [Objectives](/docs/concepts/objectives), [Files](/docs/concepts/files)
- **Not** — `members.manage` holder (an implicit observer of every objective thread,
  deliberately absent from this list; the UI create screen calls them "Directors"
  (`packages/web-ui/src/components/ObjectiveCreate.tsx:242`), a word that exists nowhere
  else in the product and should not be learned — the detail screen one component over
  says "members.manage holders") · Objective party (the stored trio this belongs to) ·
  Trace access (watching grants none — reading the assignee's trace still requires
  `activity.read`, `packages/core/src/app.ts:5215-5219`)
- **Verbs** — add · remove, both through `objectives_update` / `PATCH /objectives/:id`
  as `addWatchers` / `removeWatchers`. The SDK also exports `UpdateWatchersRequest`
  `{add, remove}`, a store-call shape that validates no route — the wire names
  `addWatchers` / `removeWatchers` on `PATCH /objectives/:id` are the canonical ones.
  `ReassignObjectiveRequest` `{to, note}` is different: it is the validated body of
  `POST /objectives/:id/reassign` (`packages/core/src/app.ts:4723`).
- The write grant is documented, but not where a reader looks first:
  [Files](/docs/concepts/files) states "full read-and-write access"
  (`docs/concepts/files.mdx:50-52`) while the glossary still says a watcher is added
  "to observe it" (`docs/concepts/glossary.mdx:164-166`). Whether write is intended is a
  maintainer decision; the ontology records the grant the code makes.

### Objective party

The stored relationship: `{assignee, originator, watchers}`. This is the predicate the
list route answers as `related`, the objective-detail read gate calls "thread
participant", and the filesystem ACL calls membership of the objective — one set,
computed identically in three places under three names. *(defined in code only)*

- **Names** — core `isParticipant` (`packages/core/src/app.ts:4443-4446`), `relatedTo`
  (`:4406-4409`), `objectiveAcl.isMember` / `isObjectiveMember`
  (`packages/core/src/files/filesystem-store.ts:324-329`; `apps/server/src/run.ts:572-579`)
  · SDK `ListObjectivesQuery.related` ("assigned, originated, or watching") · query
  `GET /objectives?related=`
- **Not** — Thread audience (this set plus every `members.manage` holder — the push
  fan-out, which is strictly wider) · Watchers (one of the three terms, not the set) ·
  Plate (the assignee term alone). A member's **related objectives** are the objectives
  they are a party to; that is the symmetrical name, and it is the wire's.
- Two code paths disagree about `members.manage` holders and the disagreement is
  unresolved: they are in the push audience and may post to the thread, yet
  `GET /objectives/:id` answers them "not a thread participant; viewing requires
  objectives.create" (`packages/core/src/app.ts:4441-4445`). Whatever the gate ends up
  being, its error text must stop naming thread membership for a test that is not thread
  membership.

### Thread audience

The set an objective push actually fans out to: the objective party plus every member
holding `members.manage`, plus — for one event only — the previous assignee on
`reassigned` and the removed watcher on `watcher_removed`, so each learns of their own
exit. One function computes it, and two call sites use it: the lifecycle-event publisher
(`packages/core/src/app.ts:4319`) and `POST /objectives/:id/discuss` (`:4728`). Watcher
changes reach it as `watcher_added` / `watcher_removed` events through the publisher —
the separate `/watchers` endpoint the function's own doc-comment still names was folded
into `PATCH /objectives/:id`. *(defined in code only)*

- **Names** — core `objectiveThreadMembers` (`packages/core/src/app.ts:4291-4311`) ·
  route error `user '<name>' is not a member of objective <id>'s thread` · MCP
  `objectives_discuss` description "thread members" · docs "The assignee, originator,
  watchers, and members holding `members.manage` see lifecycle events and can post"
  ([Objectives](/docs/concepts/objectives), `docs/concepts/objectives.mdx:116-117`)
- **Not** — Objective party (the stored trio, without `members.manage` holders) ·
  Delivery audience (the broker's per-message recipient list, a different family) ·
  Watchers (the explicit additions only). The `objectives_discuss` tool description tells
  agents the thread members are "the originator, the assignee, and all directors on the
  team" (`packages/cli/src/runtime/tools.ts:429-430`) and omits watchers entirely; the
  code includes them, and the code is right.

### Objective event

A row in the append-only `objective_events` audit log: one transition, written in the
same transaction as the state change it records. "The table is append-only — there is no
delete or update path. A changed mind is a cancel-with-reason plus a fresh objective,
not an edit" (`packages/core/src/objectives.ts:23-27`).

- **Names** — SDK `ObjectiveEvent`, `ObjectiveEventKind`, `ObjectiveEventKindSchema` · DB
  `objective_events` (`event_id`, `objective_id`, `ts`, `actor`, `kind`, `payload`) ·
  route `GET /objectives/:id` → `GetObjectiveResponse` `{objective, events}` · CLI
  `csuite objectives view` · MCP `objectives_view` · UI the event log on the objective
  page · docs [Objectives](/docs/concepts/objectives)
- **Not** — Objective discussion post (conversation, carried as a message with
  `data.kind = 'objective_discuss'` in the `obj:<id>` thread and deliberately never in
  this log) · Activity marker (`objective_open` / `objective_close` on the assignee's
  activity stream, written by the runner from a diff of its own open-set cache — a
  different layer with a different clock) · Lifecycle push (the broker message with
  `data.kind = 'objective'` that announces an event; the event is the row, the push is
  the announcement) · `AmendmentDisposition` (`correction` / `scope_change`, which sits
  in the objectives section of `types.ts` but is read only by team process, and belongs
  to that family). "Lifecycle" alone belongs to the status machine: the team-status
  `lifecycle` stale signal reads *this table*
  (`packages/core/src/team-status.ts:40-41`), not the activity markers.
- **Values** — `kind`: `assigned` `blocked` `unblocked` `completed` `cancelled`
  `reassigned` — the state machine of the work · `watcher_added` `watcher_removed` —
  the audience of the thread · `amended` `event_corrected` — legacy, read-tolerated
  only; no write path produces either since the contract-amendment layer was retired
  (the dead `objectives.outcome_version` column, "LEGACY, unread", is its other residue)
- **Verbs** — append (implicit in every mutation) · read. No update, no delete. Creation
  appends exactly one event, `assigned`, whose payload names every initial watcher
  (`packages/core/src/objectives.ts:460-476`).

### Objective thread

The discussion channel that exists for every objective, keyed `obj:<id>`. Lifecycle
pushes and discussion posts both land on it; it is a thread tag on ordinary messages,
not a stored entity of its own.

- **Names** — thread key `obj:<id>` · core `OBJECTIVE_THREAD_PREFIX`
  (`packages/core/src/event-log.ts:75`) and two bare literals in `app.ts` (`:4324`,
  `:4800`) · web UI `OBJ_PREFIX` (`packages/web-ui/src/lib/messages.ts:28`),
  `objectiveThreadKey()` · message `data.kind = 'objective_discuss'` and
  `data.kind = 'objective'` · route `POST /objectives/:id/discuss` · MCP
  `objectives_discuss` · UI "Thread" panel · docs
  [Objectives](/docs/concepts/objectives)
- **Not** — Objective folder (`/objectives/<id>/`, the objective's shared folder —
  *objective namespace* in the code,
  whose `fs_entries.owner` column carries the same `obj:<id>` string via
  `OBJECTIVE_OWNER_PREFIX`, `packages/core/src/files/paths.ts:37,118-124`; one string,
  two readings — a message thread tag and a filesystem ACL owner) · Channel (a named team
  thread with its own record; an objective thread is derived from an id and cannot be
  renamed, archived or joined) · Objective event log (the audit table, which discussion
  never enters)
- **Values** — the lifecycle push carries exactly `{kind, event, objective_id,
  objective_status, thread, actor}` (`packages/core/src/app.ts:4338-4345`); a discussion
  push carries `{kind, objective_id, thread}` (`:4762-4767`). Nothing else is in the bag
  — the objectives tracker reads `data.assignee` from an objective push
  (`packages/cli/src/runtime/objectives-tracker.ts:83-88`), no producer sets it, and the
  branch it guards is empty.
- **Verbs** — post (`objectives_discuss` / `POST /objectives/:id/discuss`) · read via
  history on the thread key. `--note` on `csuite objectives update` is **not** a way to
  post here: the server ignores `note` unless the same call changes `assignee`
  (`packages/sdk/src/types.ts:1933-1935`), and a `note` on its own is rejected 400 by
  `UpdateObjectiveRequestSchema`, whose "at least one of" list omits it
  (`packages/sdk/src/schemas.ts:491-499`).

### Objective activity window

The span the objective detail page's trace panel reads:
`[objective.createdAt, objective.completedAt ?? now]` against `objective.assignee`'s
activity stream (`packages/web-ui/src/components/TracePanel.tsx:89-95,138-141`).
`completedAt` is set only on `done`, so a cancelled or still-active objective's window
runs to the current moment. *(defined in code only)*

- **Names** — web UI `TracePanel`, mounted at
  `packages/web-ui/src/components/ObjectiveDetail.tsx:402` · route
  `GET /members/:name/activity?from=&to=` with `from = createdAt` · UI the "Trace" panel
  on the objective page
- **Not** — Objective trace (the marker-bounded per-stint slice the glossary, the docs, the SDK and
  the activity store all define: "the slice of the assignee's activity between the moment
  an objective opened for them and the moment it closed",
  `docs/concepts/activity-and-traces.mdx:91-93`; `docs/concepts/glossary.mdx:156-157`;
  `packages/sdk/src/types.ts:2286-2288`) · Activity marker (`objective_open` /
  `objective_close`, which bound the trace and which this window ignores) · Member
  timeline (`clipToObjective` / `clipCallsToObjective`,
  `packages/web-ui/src/components/AgentTimeline.tsx:426-448,479-497` — the marker-based
  clipping exists, on the member profile, not on the objective page)
- The code and the docs disagree, and the code wins: the objective page shows every
  captured exchange the *current* assignee logged over the objective's whole calendar
  life. After a reassignment the docs' promise that "each assignee's trace covers their
  own stint" (`docs/concepts/activity-and-traces.mdx:99-100`) is false of this panel.
  D1 settles which window the word names — the marker window — so this panel is a defect
  and not a second sense of "trace": do not cite the word for what the objective page
  renders.

## Conversation and delivery

This family covers where a message goes and what happens to it afterward: the
channel it lands in, the key that groups it into a scrollback, the audience the
broker fanned it out to, and the ledger row that tracks whether the recipient's
runner ever said what it did with it. One distinction carries most of the
confusion: a **channel** is a persisted room with a database row, a **thread
key** is a derived string with no row and no owner, and the `<channel>` block an
agent reads is neither — it is one message rendered as ambient input, and it
wraps DMs and objective posts too.

### Channel

A named, persisted team room that partitions team chat: "any team member can
create one, the creator joins as an ordinary member, and members explicitly
join." `id` is opaque and immutable, `slug` is mutable and user-facing, and
messages reference the id — so a rename never orphans history.

- **Names** — SDK `Channel`, `ChannelSummary` (per-viewer projection), `ChannelMember`, `ChannelAuditEntry` · core `ChannelStore`, `createSqliteChannelStore()` (the class `SqliteChannelStore` is module-private) · DB `channels.id`, `channels.slug`, `channel_members`, `channel_audit` · route `GET /channels`, `PATCH /channels/:slug` (rename), `DELETE /channels/:slug` (archive), `POST /channels/:slug/members`, `GET /channels/:slug/audit` · MCP `channels_list`, `channels_post` · UI "#\<slug\>" · docs [Channels](/docs/concepts/channels)
- **Not** — Ambient event (one message rendered for an agent, carrying the `<channel>` tag, `ChannelEvent` and `ChannelEventSink` — no room involved) · Thread key (the derived grouping string; `chan:<id>` points at a channel, it is not one) · `ChannelMemberRole` `admin` (observational only; authority is the `channels.manage` permission leaf, and every administering route checks that leaf, never the role)
- **Values** — `slug`: 1–32 chars, lowercase ASCII letters, digits and single dashes, starting and ending alphanumeric, no consecutive dashes · `archivedAt`: `null` while active, epoch-ms when soft-archived · `ChannelMemberRole`: `admin` `member` · `ChannelAuditAction`: `create` `rename` `description` `member_add` `member_remove` `member_role` `archive`
- **Verbs** — list · create · get · rename · archive · add member · remove member · read audit

### `general`

The one channel every member is in without joining: `id` and `slug` are both
the literal `general`, it has no `channel_members` rows, and its pushes take the
broadcast path because its membership is implicit. Six code-and-wire spellings
name it, four independent sites re-implement the equivalence between them, and
one of those four lives in raw SQL.

- **Names** — core `GENERAL_CHANNEL_ID` (declared in the event log), `GENERAL_CHANNEL_SLUG` (declared in the channel store, importing the id) · wire tag `data.thread = 'chan:general'`, **or** no tag at all with `to: null` · web UI `PRIMARY_THREAD` = `'primary'`, aliased as `GENERAL_THREAD` · UI "#general" in the nav, "#team" in a toast, "Team Chat" in an inbox row, "◇ Net is quiet" as the empty state · docs [Channels](/docs/concepts/channels)
- **Not** — Channel, in the parts that make a channel a channel: general cannot be joined, left, renamed, archived, or given member rows, and `isMember` is always true for it · Broadcast (the routing path general's pushes take, not a second destination) · `primary` (a browser-local map and unread key that never leaves the client and is not a wire value) · Default feed (the unscoped read, which is wider than this channel)
- **Values** — a message is in general when `data.thread` is `chan:general` **or** when `to` is null and `data.thread` is absent. The MCP `broadcast` tool, the web-UI composer and `csuite push --broadcast` all emit the untagged form; only the external-notification dispatcher emits `chan:general`. The four sites that collapse the two forms are `queryGeneralStmt` (SQL), `matchesChannel` (in-memory log), `threadKeyOf` (web UI) and `extractChannelId` (runner forwarder).

The web UI's comment calls `primary` a *persisted* last-read cursor; `unread.ts`
states the unread state is non-persistent and resets on reload. The code is
right: `primary` survives only for the in-memory message map.

### Thread key

A derived string that groups messages into one scrollback. It has no table, no
row and no minting authority — it is an optional `data.thread` tag on a message
and a Map key in the browser, minted at six independent sites across four
subsystems with no registry. Only its `chan:` form points at something with a
row. *(defined in code only)*

- **Names** — wire `Message.data.thread` (the **thread tag**) · core `CHANNEL_THREAD_PREFIX`, `OBJECTIVE_THREAD_PREFIX`, `SECRET_THREAD_PREFIX`, `channelThreadTag()`, `isScopedThreadTag()` · DB no column — the expression index `events_thread_ts_idx ON events (json_extract(data,'$.thread'), ts)` · web UI `threadKeyOf()`, `messagesByThread`, `lastReadByThread`, route kinds `thread-channel` and `thread-dm` · docs "objective thread", "DM thread" in [Events and messages](/docs/concepts/events)
- **Not** — Channel (the room, addressed by slug on every route and by id in every tag) · Routing class (`ThreadType` — what the runner puts in the agent's `thread=` attribute) · the "Thread" panel heading in the objective detail view, which renders `obj:<id>` and should read "Discussion" to match `objectives_discuss` · a Node or Codex worker thread in [IPC protocol](/docs/dev/ipc-protocol)
