- **Values** — `chan:<channel id>` · `obj:<objective id>` · `secret:<slug>`, `tool:<slug>`, `variable:<slug>`, `hook:<slug>` (registry fan-outs, audience narrower than the team) · `dm:<other member>` and `primary` (browser-only; neither is ever written to the wire). `isScopedThreadTag()` returns true for every prefix above except `chan:general`, which is deliberately unscoped because general's membership is implicit.

### Routing class

How a message reached the agent, from the agent's point of view — computed by
the forwarder from the broker's authoritative state, never trusted from the
sender's payload. It is the value the agent actually reads in the `thread=`
attribute of its `<channel>` block, which is why an agent never sees a thread
key. *(defined in code only)*

- **Names** — CLI runtime `ThreadType`, stamped onto the meta key `thread` and reserved by `RESERVED_META_KEYS` · agent-facing `<channel … thread="primary">`
- **Not** — Thread key (a differently-typed value under the same name on the other side of the runner boundary: the sender's own `data.thread` is dropped by `RESERVED_META_KEYS` before the agent sees it, so `thread="channel"` and `data.thread="chan:abc"` are not the same field) · Channel (the `channel` meta key beside it carries the channel id and `channel_slug` the slug, so the agent can reply with `channels_post`)
- **Values** — `primary`: broadcast to the team channel · `dm`: addressed to this member · `channel`: posted into a non-general channel this member belongs to

### Message

One utterance: an immutable record with a broker-minted `id` and `ts`, a
nullable `to`, a severity `level`, a free-form `data` bag and an attachment
list. Its `from` is the "authoritative sender name, stamped by the broker based
on the caller's authenticated member. Never trusted from the request payload."

- **Names** — SDK `Message`, input `PushPayload`, result `PushResult` · DB `events` (`id`, `ts`, `to_name`, `from_name`, `title`, `body`, `level`, `data`, `attachments`, `recipients`) · route `POST /push`, `GET /history`, `GET /subscribe` · CLI `csuite push --body <text> (--agent <id> | --broadcast)` · MCP `broadcast`, `send`, `channels_post`, `objectives_discuss`, `recent` · UI "Send" · docs [Events and messages](/docs/concepts/events)
- **Not** — Event (the docs' umbrella noun for anything that moves between teammates; the `events` table holds exactly `Message` rows and nothing else) · Ambient event (this message rendered for one agent) · Activity event (the durable observation stream) · Web Push (`POST /push` carries a message; `/push/subscriptions` registers a browser device)
- **Values** — `level`: `debug` `info` `notice` `warning` `error` `critical` — message severity, six values, exported from the SDK as `LogLevel`. `csuite-core` exports a different four-value `LogLevel` for server logging — `debug` `info` `warn` `error` — that spells the third one `warn`; the type names are identical, `packages/cli/src/runtime/tools.ts` imports from both, and a value legal in one is illegal in the other.
- **Verbs** — post. One act has five current spellings: wire `push`, CLI `csuite push`, MCP `broadcast` / `send` / `channels_post`, UI "Send".
- **Decided** — D41: converge the three posting tools on one verb with the scope in the noun — `team_post`, `members_post`, `channels_post` — over recording `broadcast` / `send` / `channels_post` as deliberate agent affordance (2026-09-03). The SDK's grammar arbitrates (D42) and `broadcast` and `send` are the only chat tools with no noun; the scope stays explicit at selection time because it moves into the noun. `objectives_discuss` is a distinct referent — its own endpoint, a computed fan-out, a 16,384-character body cap against `channels_post`'s 65,536 — and keeps its verb.

### Addressee

The single member a message is addressed to, or null for a broadcast. It is one
name: not a list, not a count, and not a seat kind.

- **Names** — SDK `PushPayload.to`, `Message.to` · core local `targetName` · DB `events.to_name` · CLI `csuite push --agent <id>` / `-a` · CLI runtime meta key `target`, stamped only on true DMs · MCP `send` argument `to`
- **Not** — Delivery audience (`recipients`, the list a scoped fan-out reached; a channel post has an audience and no addressee) · `DeliveryReport.targets` (a count of registered recipients, sharing a stem with an address while being neither) · `PendingMessageDelivery.recipient` (the subject of one ledger row) · Member (the seat — `--agent` takes a **member name**, and members are frequently people)

### Delivery audience

"The delivery audience the broker fanned the message out to — the channel's
members, an objective thread, the holders of a secret." Decided at send time and
persisted on the event row so a later read answers the same question the live
fan-out answered. Absent from the wire deliberately; no client ever sees it.
*(defined in code only)*

- **Names** — core `PushContext.recipients`, `EventLogAppendOptions.recipients`, `ChannelStore.recipientNames()`, and the visibility rule `feedVisibleTo()` · DB `events.recipients` (JSON array or NULL) · docs the "Who sees it" column in [Events and messages](/docs/concepts/events)
- **Not** — Read access, which is channel membership evaluated at read time: `GET /history?channel=<slug>` gates on `isMember` *now* and its SQL ignores `recipients` and the viewer entirely, so a new member reads back the whole pre-join history the recorded audience would have withheld from their feed. That backfill is deliberate; the readback of untagged scoped fan-outs (`instructions`, `context_control`, `environment`) through the general query is D5, a defect question, not a naming one. · Addressee (`to`, one name) · `DeliveryReport.targets` (the count reported back to the sender)
- **Values** — `null` or omitted: no narrower audience — a DM, or a team-wide broadcast. It does **not** mean "audience unknown". · `[]`: a real audience that happens to be empty, whose message is not team-visible afterward. The two must never collapse.

### Default feed

What a member reads back when they name no scope: `GET /history` with neither
`channel` nor `with`, and the MCP `recent` tool called with no argument.
`feedVisibleTo()` computes it from the viewer's name — DMs in both directions,
every team-wide broadcast, and every scoped row whose recorded audience contains
the viewer, so objective posts, registry chatter and instruction notices arrive
interleaved. Three surfaces call it the general channel and none of them is
right. *(defined in code only)*

- **Names** — route `GET /history` with no `channel` and no `with` · core `feedVisibleTo()`, `EventLogQueryOptions.viewer` · MCP `recent` with no scope argument · CLI runtime the empty-state string "no messages in `<team>` team channel" and the header "recent `<team>` team chat" · docs [Events and messages](/docs/concepts/events), "Agents call `recent` — no argument for `general`"
- **Not** — `general` (the channel; `GET /history?channel=general` runs `queryGeneralStmt`, a different query that matches `data.thread = 'chan:general'` OR `to_name IS NULL AND data.thread IS NULL` and applies neither `recipients` nor the viewer) · Inbox (the web UI's computed attention list, not a query) · Delivery audience (the send-time list this read filters against) · Redelivery (the runner's replay of unacknowledged work, which the feed does not carry)

Which way this resolves is a product decision, not a naming one: either the
unscoped call narrows to `channel=general` and matches every description of it,
or the tool description, the empty-state string and the docs sentence stop
calling the viewer's whole feed a channel.

### Message delivery

One row per `(message_id, recipient)` in the broker's ledger: the record that
this message is owed to this member, created at push time for each recipient the
push tracks and expiring after 24 hours. It is the obligation, not the outcome.

- **Names** — core `MessageDeliveryLedger`, `InMemoryMessageDeliveryLedger`, `SqliteMessageDeliveryLedger`, `PendingMessageDelivery`, `ExpiredMessageDelivery`, `MESSAGE_DELIVERY_TTL_MS` · DB `message_deliveries`, primary key `(message_id, recipient)`, columns `state`, `attempts`, `first_sent_at`, `last_sent_at`, `expires_at`, `disposition_at`, `reason_json`, `accepted_owner` · SDK the rollup `DeliveryReport.acknowledgement` · docs [REST API](/docs/dev/rest-api)
- **Not** — Message disposition (the runner's acknowledgement, which lands *on* a delivery as a state) · `NotificationDelivery` (the receipt for one inbound webhook request, with its own nine-value `NotificationDeliveryStatus`) · Delivery audience (who the message went to; a member whose only connected runner predates the ack protocol gets counted, not tracked)
- **Values** — `state`: `pending` `accepted` `acted` `handled` `refused` `unreported`. `unreported` here is per-delivery and means the recipient's runner is structurally incapable of acknowledging. The per-member word that reads like it is `Presence.executor` — an `ExecutorState` whose `unreported` means the broker understands the field but no connected runner supplied enough evidence to classify it; `Presence.unreportedConnections` is a third thing again, a count of live connections that reported no validated client identity. Same word, three subjects.
- **Verbs** — `track` · `noteSent` · `settle` · `releaseOwner` · `pending` · `expire` · `markUnreported`

### Message disposition

A runner's acknowledgement of one delivery, sent as a `message_disposition`
frame on its own subscribe socket. It reports what the agent did with the
message, not whether the bytes arrived. Nothing in the tree says what separates
`acted` from `handled`, or when a runner should choose `deferred` over
`refused`. *(defined in code only)*

- **Names** — SDK `MessageDisposition`, `MessageDispositionFrame`, `MessageDispositionReasonCode`, capability `deliveryProtocol: 'disposition-v1'` · core `Broker.disposition()` and the module-private gate `supportsDisposition()` · CLI runtime `ChannelDeliveryReceipt.accepted()` / `.settle()` · docs [REST API](/docs/dev/rest-api)
- **Not** — `AmendmentDisposition` (`correction` | `scope_change`, the editorial classification of a team-process edit, offered as a `disposition` argument on `team_process_write` in the same MCP surface an agent holds) · `DeliveryReport.acknowledgement.status` (`pending` | `settled` | `refused` | `unreported`, an aggregate computed from counts, never any single row's state) · `ContextControlOutcome` (`applied` | `declined` | `unsupported` | `failed`, a runner's answer to a control verb) · `NotificationDeliveryStatus` (`delivered` | `pending` | `expired` | `dropped` | `rejected` | `filtered` | `duplicate` | `coalesced` | `failed`, an inbound webhook receipt's fate). These four answer one question in four vocabularies that share `pending`, `expired` and `refused`/`rejected`, and they are not interchangeable.
- **Values** — `accepted`: leased by a live subscription; non-terminal · `deferred`: returns the row to `pending` and has no ledger state of its own · `acted`, `handled`, `refused`: terminal. `refused` has a second producer with no frame behind it — the 24-hour expiry sweep, stamping `reason.code = 'expired'`. `MessageDispositionReasonCode`: `degraded` `turn_failed` `expired` `unsupported` `operator_policy`.

### Redelivery

The broker's replay of every pending ledger row, in original message order, when
a runner attaches. It is runner-only: a browser subscriber gets nothing.
*(defined in code only)*

- **Names** — core `Broker.redeliverPending()`, called from the `/subscribe` handler on attach and again from `Broker.runnerCondition()` when a degraded runner reports ready
- **Not** — Read-back (`recent`, `GET /history`) — what a person scrolling or a fresh agent orienting itself does. [Events and messages](/docs/concepts/events) says offline members get no replay and read back instead; that is true for people and false for runners, and the code is authoritative. · A capability gate: the gate is `clientIdentity.kind === 'runner'`, not `disposition-v1`. A runner advertising the protocol has its replayed rows marked sent and still owes an acknowledgement; a legacy runner gets the same replay with its rows stamped `unreported`, because no acknowledgement will ever arrive. A browser subscriber is neither and gets nothing.

### Ambient event

One broker message rendered for agent consumption: "the body text plus flat
string metadata (`from`, `thread`, `ts`, `kind`, …)". Every message an agent
receives becomes one — DMs, objective posts and registry fan-outs included — so
the `<channel>` wrapper an agent reads is not evidence that a channel was
involved. *(defined in code only)*

- **Names** — CLI runtime `ChannelEvent`, `ChannelEventSink`, `RunnerOptions.channelSink`, `runForwarder()`, `formatChannelEvent()` · agent-facing the `<channel …>` block · docs "ambient input" in [MCP tools](/docs/dev/mcp-tools); [REST API](/docs/dev/rest-api) calls instruction, secret and variable fan-outs "channel events"
- **Not** — Channel (the persisted room; the field was renamed *into* this collision when `RunnerOptions.notificationSink` became `channelSink`) · Message (the durable record; an ambient event is a transient rendering of one, with the ten `RESERVED_META_KEYS` — `msg_id` `level` `ts` `ts_ms` `thread` `from` `title` `target` `channel` `channel_slug` — stamped by the forwarder from broker-authoritative state and dropped from `data`, so a sender cannot spoof `from`, `level` or `thread` on the receiving side)

### Inbox

The web UI's computed "what needs my attention" feed: unread threads, objectives
assigned to the viewer that are active or blocked, and watched objectives that
moved to blocked. "This is a pure computed: no new network traffic."
*(defined in code only)*

- **Names** — web UI `InboxItem`, `inboxItems`, `inboxCount`, `InboxPanel.tsx`, route `/inbox` · UI "Inbox"
- **Not** — the **Inbox:** field in [Jobs gallery](/docs/guides/jobs-gallery), which names what wakes a job — a cron, a webhook, an RSS alert — and is a trigger list rather than a queue · the anti-pattern in [Events and messages](/docs/concepts/events), "agents don't check an inbox", which describes delivery and not this route · Default feed (a server query; the inbox is a projection over messages the client already holds)
- **Values** — `kind`: `thread-unread` · `objective-assigned` · `objective-watched-blocked`

`inbox.ts`'s own header says the unread source is "primary + DMs". The loop
walks every key in `messagesByThread`, channel and objective threads included,
and `threadTitle` has no label for those — it falls through and renders the raw
thread key. The code is wider than its comment.

### System actor

The identity the broker stamps on messages it originates itself — instruction
changes, environment changes, session-online notices, context-control results.
Two literals name it today, in two different fields, with no shared constant.
*(defined in code only)*

- **Names** — core `PushContext.from = 'csuite'`, and the module-private `SYSTEM_ACTOR = '__system__'` as `created_by` on the seeded general row · DB `events.from_name`, `channels.created_by` · agent-facing `<channel from="csuite" …>`
- **Not** — `hook:<slug>` in `Message.from` (an external endpoint spoke: "member names can't contain `:`, so the sender identity is collision-free and visibly foreign") · `hook:<slug>` in `data.thread`, which is a thread tag on endpoint-registry chatter and never a sender · Member (a real seat; `NameSchema` permits both `csuite` and `__system__` as member names, and no reserved-name guard exists)
- **Decided** — D33: one system actor, spelled in the collision-free foreign-identity form `csuite:` that `hook:<slug>` already established, over keeping bare `csuite` in `Message.from` beside `__system__` in the channel store (2026-09-03). This is the identity whose messages carry the most authority in an agent's context and the only one not using the `:` trick the repository invented for exactly this problem. It is wire-visible in text agents read, so it ships behind the one-release alias window the `process_document_*` → `team_process_*` rename set as precedent.

## Observation and capture

This family is everything the broker keeps about what a member actually did: the
durable activity stream, the full-fidelity record of each model call beneath it,
the verbatim bytes beneath that, and the operational telemetry beside it. Two
distinctions carry the family. **Activity** is the durable per-member record and
nothing else — the live `idle` / `working` / `blocked` reading is *work state*.
And **trace** is always derived and always qualified: there is no table, no route
and no type named `trace` anywhere in the tree, and the bare word names four
different things.

### Activity stream

The per-member append-only timeline of everything a member's runner observed —
"the source of truth for 'what did this member actually do'". One table, one row
per activity event, appends idempotent on `(member_name, source_id)` and
delivered to live-tail subscribers synchronously after the write commits.

- **Names** — SDK `ActivityEvent`, `ActivityRow`, `ActivityRowSchema` · core
  `ActivityStore` (interface), `InMemoryActivityStore`, `createSqliteActivityStore`
  / `SqliteActivityStoreHandle` · DB `member_activity` · route
  `POST /members/:name/activity`, `GET /members/:name/activity`,
  `GET /members/:name/activity/stream` (WebSocket) · permission leaf
  `activity.read` (gates reading another member's stream, inferences and
  telemetry; your own are always visible) · UI "Activity" tab, `AgentTimeline`,
  `ActivityInspector` · docs
  [Activity and traces](/docs/concepts/activity-and-traces)
- **Not** — Work state (the momentary roster reading, held in memory and
  forgotten; `Presence.activity`, `activityWindowMs` and `POST /presence/activity`
  are its legacy wire spelling, which D6 replaces with `Presence.workState`,
  `workStateWindowMs` and `/presence/work-state`) · Activity database (the SQLite
  file holding this table and four more) · Message (the chat row `EventLog`
  appends to table `events`) · Objective trace (one objective's slice of this
  stream) · `csuite-server` — the SQLite implementation lives in `csuite-core`
  beside the interface and is exported from there, whatever the `ActivityStore`
  header says; the server only consumes it
- **Verbs** — `append` (self only), `list`, subscribe

### Activity event

One entry in the activity stream: a nine-variant discriminated union keyed on
`kind`. Every variant carries an optional `sourceId` — the id of the source
record the runner mapped it from — and the broker dedups on `(member, sourceId)`,
so a re-read transcript or a retried batch cannot write the same activity twice.
Dedup is a property of the capture source, not of the stream: the Claude
transcript reader stamps every event, the codex rollout parser stamps none, and
driver-minted brackets carry none either.

- **Names** — SDK `ActivityEvent`, `ActivityKind`, `ActivityKindSchema`,
  `ActivityEventSchema`, wire field `sourceId` · DB `member_activity.kind`,
  `member_activity.source_id` (partial unique index on
  `(member_name, source_id) WHERE source_id IS NOT NULL`) · UI `KindFilter`,
  `DEFAULT_FILTERS`
- **Not** — Message (the `EventLog` row in table `events` — `EventLog.append` and
  `ActivityStore.append` read alike and write to unrelated tables) · Diagnostic
  record (`diagnostic_event`, the completeness-diagnostics store) · OTLP log
  record (a telemetry-wire record, carrying `event.name`) · `raw_exchange.kind`
  (`request` or `response`, which side of a body a row holds) · `PanelRow.kind`
  (`turn` or `sidecar`, a `TracePanel` row discriminator)
- **Values** — `kind`: `session_start` `session_end` — the run bracket, one pair
  per runner invocation · `objective_open` `objective_close` — the objective
  bracket · `llm_exchange` — one model turn · `tool_action` — one
  natively-captured tool run · `user_prompt` — the prompt that woke a turn ·
  `context_control` — the acknowledgement that a `compact`, `clear` or `reload`
  reached a terminal outcome on the runner, `applied` / `declined` /
  `unsupported` / `failed` · `auth_state` — the agent's credentials went
  `blocked` on a 401, or `recovered`. Nine. `ActivityKind`, `ActivityKindSchema`,
  the broker's route filter (which parses through that schema) and the UI's
  `DEFAULT_FILTERS` carry all nine; every prose list in the repo under-counts,
  and `auth_state` passes the UI filter but reaches no renderer in `buildThread`.
- **Verbs** — `append`, `list`

### Objective trace

The slice of one member's activity stream covering one objective: everything that
member did while that objective was open. Always derived on read from a range
query — there is no per-objective stored blob — and always qualified, because
bare "trace" names four different things.

- **Names** — SDK `ActivityEvent` doc-comment ("Objective 'traces' are a
  time-range slice of this stream between `objective_open` and `objective_close`
  markers for a given objectiveId") · core `ActivityStore` header · route
  `GET /members/:assignee/activity` with `from`, `to` and `kind=llm_exchange` ·
  UI `TracePanel`, "Trace review" disclosure on the objective · docs
  [Glossary](/docs/concepts/glossary),
  [Activity and traces](/docs/concepts/activity-and-traces)
- **Not** — Capture pipeline (the mechanism that produces the stream; what
  `--no-trace` turns off) · Activity database (the storage; what `prune-traces`
  deletes) · Exchange capture (one model exchange — the payload type
  `AnthropicMessagesEntry`, whose doc-comment still titles itself "Trace
  capture") · `packages/core/src/trace/` and `packages/cli/src/runtime/trace/`
  (capture-pipeline code, a source namespace and not an entity)
- **Decided** — D1: the marker window — the `objective_open` → `objective_close`
  spans carrying that objective id — over the calendar window
  `[objective.createdAt, completedAt ?? now]` (2026-09-03). Four surfaces already
  define the marker window and the marker-window code already exists as
  `clipToObjective` / `clipCallsToObjective` in `AgentTimeline.tsx`;
  `TracePanel.tsx` is the lone calendar-window implementation, and because it
  queries the objective's *current* assignee over that window, a reassign makes
  it show the wrong member's unrelated work.

### Capture pipeline

The runner-to-broker mechanism that turns each agent's native instrumentation
into stored observation: the runner's capture host, the transcript reader, the
OTLP relay, the broker's ingest routes and the correlator. Nothing in it
intercepts network traffic — "NOTHING HERE SEES NETWORK TRAFFIC" — so an exchange
the agent's own instrumentation does not report is not captured at all.

- **Names** — CLI runtime `CaptureHost` ("the capture SINK the runner wires every
  adapter into"), `ActivityUploader`, `TranscriptReader` /
  `attachTranscriptReader`, `startOtlpRelay` · core `GenAiCorrelator`,
  `createGenAiCorrelator` · CLI flag `--no-trace` (leaves `captureHost === null`)
  · docs [Trace pipeline](/docs/dev/trace-pipeline), [Traces](/docs/tracing)
- **Not** — Objective trace (one objective's slice of one member's stream) ·
  Activity database (where this pipeline's output lands) · Capture tier (what an
  adapter declares its agent's instrumentation provides) · a proxy or a wiretap
  on the provider wire — there is no proxy, no certificate on disk and no
  interception

### Activity database

The second SQLite file a broker opens, holding five tables: `member_activity`,
`gen_ai_inference`, `telemetry`, `raw_exchange` and `raw_blob`. All five are
heavy-write per-member streams, kept off the main broker write lock, and the file
is assembled from whichever stores a deployment wired — a broker without gen-ai
capture has no `gen_ai_inference` table.

- **Names** — core `pruneActivityDb`, `PruneActivityResult` · server option
  `activityDbPath` · CLI `--activity-db <path>` · env `CSUITE_ACTIVITY_DB_PATH`
  (default: the main DB path with `-activity` inserted before its extension —
  `/path/foo.db` → `/path/foo-activity.db`) · docs [Traces](/docs/tracing)
- **Not** — Activity store (the `ActivityStore` interface over `member_activity`
  alone — the database contains the store, they are nested and not synonyms) ·
  "trace store" (an obsolete name for the activity store, surviving in one
  comment at `apps/server/src/run.ts:298`; drop it) · the main broker database
  (members, objectives, channels, files)

### Activity-database retention

The one-pass delete of everything older than a cutoff across the whole activity
database. `member_activity`, `gen_ai_inference`, `telemetry` and `raw_exchange`
go by their own timestamps; `raw_blob` goes by reference from either direction —
`raw_exchange.hash` or a retained `gen_ai_inference`'s `request_sha256` /
`response_sha256` — because blobs are content-addressed and deduped, so deleting
them by age would cut the bytes out from under retained rows.

- **Names** — core `pruneActivityDb`, `PruneActivityResult` (`memberActivity`,
  `genAiInference`, `telemetry`, `rawExchange`, `rawBlob`, `total`) · CLI
  `csuite prune-traces --older-than <duration> [--activity-db <path>] [--yes]`,
  `runPruneTracesCommand`, per-table output "activity" / "inferences" /
  "telemetry" / "raw bodies" · docs [Traces](/docs/tracing)
- **Not** — Objective trace: `prune-traces` is the last place "trace" means the
  whole activity database. Its one-line help still says "delete activity rows
  older than the cutoff" while the confirmation prompt and the report name all
  four categories, so an operator reading only the help under-estimates what is
  destroyed. · Runner-side sweeps (quarantined raw bodies at 14 days,
  `QUARANTINE_MAX_AGE_MS`, and per-pid session logs at a week,
  `SESSION_LOG_MAX_AGE_MS` — swept at the next run start, never by this command)
- **Verbs** — prune

### Turn

One model call-and-response cycle, recorded as one `llm_exchange` activity event.
It is the organizing unit of every capture view: each turn renders exactly one
block — its own response — and the conversation is the sequence of blocks, never
a replay of each turn's request prefix.

- **Names** — SDK kind `llm_exchange`, variant `ActivityLlmExchange`, payload
  `AnthropicMessagesEntry` · core "marker" / "exchange marker" ("The domain is
  MARKERS, not `gen_ai_inference` rows") · UI "turn", "THE TURN IS THE SPINE",
  `TurnJoin.exchange` · fixture `fixtures/trace-join-conformance.json` "exchange
  marker", fields `responseId`, `startedAt`, `endedAt`, `model`, `querySource` ·
  docs "model turns" [Activity and traces](/docs/concepts/activity-and-traces)
- **Not** — Inference record (the broker's own full-fidelity row for one API
  call; a Claude turn is usually one call and a codex turn aggregates several.
  A Claude marker carries `entry.response.responseId` and joins on it; a codex
  marker carries none — a codex turn spans several Responses-API calls, so no
  single id exists to carry — and joins by interval containment instead) ·
  `RunnerTurnFrame` (`kind: 'runner_turn'`, a liveness frame on the runner's
  control channel, not an activity record) · Orphan call (a model call with no
  turn marker at all)

### Inference record

One row per model API call, carrying the complete input context actually sent on
the wire — mutations and compaction included — with the system prompt kept
separate from the chat history, plus the response, shaped after the OpenTelemetry
GenAI semantic conventions.

- **Names** — SDK `GenAiInference`, `GenAiInferenceRecord`, `GenAiInferenceSummary`
  (the same row without the content arrays), `GenAiMessage`, `GenAiUsage` · core
  `GenAiStore`, `anthropicToGenAi`, `openaiResponsesToGenAi` · DB
  `gen_ai_inference` · route `GET /members/:name/genai`,
  `GET /members/:name/genai/:id`, `?view=summary` for the light projection ·
  UI "genai ledger", "full context" · docs "inferences" [Traces](/docs/tracing)
- **Not** — Turn (the activity marker this record hangs under; the marker is
  always present, this record is rich but intermittent) · Raw body (the verbatim
  bytes this row is derived from and points back at by sha256) · Telemetry record
  (the cost and token accounting beside it, not the content) · "full context" is
  a UI affordance label for this row, never a second entity · independent of the
  activity stream — the store's own claim is about modules, and it holds there:
  no import, no listener. The schemas are joined by `responseId` in
  `capture-health.ts` and pruned against one cutoff in `activity-retention.ts`.
- **Verbs** — `list`, `get`, `create` (`POST /members/:name/genai`, self only —
  the codex ingest route)

### Raw body

The verbatim request or response bytes an agent's own instrumentation produced,
content-addressed by sha256 — the fidelity layer under the inference record.
`raw_blob` is one row per distinct body, gzipped at rest, with
`sha256(gunzip(bytes))` required to equal the `hash` primary key; `raw_exchange`
is one row per capture event, and many exchanges may share one blob.

- **Names** — core `RawBodyStore`, `RawBodyEnvelope`, `appendBody`, `getBlob` ·
  server `SqliteRawBodyStore` · DB `raw_blob`, `raw_exchange` · route
  `GET /members/:name/genai/:id/raw`, response header `X-CSuite-Body-Sha256`,
  ingest acknowledgement `X-CSuite-Raw-Bodies` · docs
  [Trace pipeline](/docs/dev/trace-pipeline)
- **Not** — Inference record (the queryable derived view over these bytes) ·
  provider wire bytes — CommandSuite never sees one. "Verbatim" means this store
  never rewrites its input, and the subject differs by ingest route: for codex it
  is the broker-redacted payload, for claude the OTLP attribute value the broker
  received. Nothing in `raw_exchange` or `raw_blob` records which route a body
  took.

### Telemetry record

One OTLP log record or metric data point exported by a member's agent — the cost,
token and lifecycle accounting behind a trace, kept separate from the content so
a burst of either never distorts the other.

- **Names** — SDK `TelemetryRecordRow`, `ListTelemetryQuery` · core
  `TelemetryRecord`, `TelemetryStore`, `parseOtlpLogs`, `parseOtlpMetrics` · DB
  `telemetry` · route `POST /otlp/v1/logs`, `POST /otlp/v1/metrics`,
  `GET /members/:name/telemetry` · docs [REST API](/docs/dev/rest-api)
- **Not** — Inference record (the content: what was sent and what came back) ·
  Diagnostic record (`diagnostic_event`, the broker's own completeness
  diagnostics, an unrelated store on an unrelated schedule)
- **Values** — `signal`: `log` `metric`
- **Verbs** — `list`

### Turn spine

The join that hangs inference records under their turn markers. Each
`llm_exchange` gets `calls: T[]` — nought to N records — and records belonging to
no turn come back as `orphans`; it is turn-centric, not row pairs, because a
codex turn genuinely aggregates several API calls.

- **Names** — UI `joinTurns`, `TurnJoin`, `JoinResult`, `JoinableCall`,
  `INTERVAL_SLACK_MS` (2s), "turn spine" · fixture
  `fixtures/trace-join-conformance.json` ("the two independent trace joins"),
  executed from both sides by `packages/web-ui/test/trace-join-conformance.test.ts`
  and `apps/server/test/capture-health-conformance.test.ts`
- **Not** — One predicate. There are two, and they must stop sharing the word
  "matched". **Turn attribution** is the UI's: which record renders under which
  turn, and it is exclusive, because a renderer must not show one record under
  two turns. **Capture satisfaction** is the broker's, in `capture-health.ts`:
  were this marker's bytes stored, which is non-exclusive, because content
  addressing is many-to-one and one record can satisfy two markers. The fixture
  case `two-markers-one-record` declares the split as "a genuine semantic
  difference neither side is wrong about". · Containment is UI-only: the broker
  has no containment predicate, so an id-less codex marker is outside its domain
  and reads `unevaluated`, never `ok`
- **Values** — `match`: `exact` — the marker's `responseId` equals the record's ·
  `containment` — the record's timestamp falls inside the marker's
  `[startedAt, endedAt]` ± 2s, same source class, and the same model where both
  sides carry one · `unmatched-exact` — an id-bearing marker with no record,
  which never falls back to containment · `unmatched`

### Orphan call

An inference record that matched no turn marker — subagent work, a server-tool
sidecar call such as web search, an away summary, or a turn whose activity
capture was missed. It is the one computed set the UI renders under three names.

- **Names** — UI `JoinResult.orphans` (the set), `variant: 'model-call'` and
  `ModelCallRow` in `AgentTimeline` ("ghost row"), `PanelRow` `kind: 'sidecar'`
  and `SidecarRow` in `TracePanel`, the label "model call" from
  `describeQuerySource` · docs "Side work shows up as indented rows"
  [Activity and traces](/docs/concepts/activity-and-traces)
- **Not** — sidecar, which names the *cause* — a server-tool call the model made
  — and not a row type; "ghost row" and `kind: 'sidecar'` are two renderings of
  the same `orphans` array and neither is the concept's name · Capture gap (the
  broker's finding that a marker's bytes are missing: the broker has no orphan
  concept at all, because its domain is markers and a record with no marker is
  invisible to it)

### Capture health

Whether a member's verbatim bodies are actually reaching the broker: over the
current session, do completed exchange markers have their stored bodies? A marker
is satisfied when its exact `gen_ai_inference` row exists and both of that row's
bodies are stored and addressable — a claim about availability, not about the
bytes being readable.

- **Names** — core `CaptureHealth`, `CaptureHealthStore.forMember`,
  `CAPTURE_GRACE_MS` (15s) · SDK `CaptureHealthState`, `CaptureHealthStateSchema`
  · route `Presence.captureHealth` on `GET /roster` · UI `presenceCaptureWarning`,
  badges "NO CAPTURE" and "CAPTURE UNCHECKED", per-turn "not captured"
  *(defined in code only)*
- **Not** — Diagnostics-store health (`RetentionHealth` = `healthy` / `degraded` /
  `unknown`, surfaced as `Presence.diagnosticsRetention` on the same roster row
  and rendered as an adjacent badge — it is the diagnostics store's own retention,
  and it is broker-wide despite sitting on a per-member field, because the roster
  fills every member's copy from one no-argument `diagnostics.health()`; the two
  spell "good" differently, `ok` against `healthy`) · Capture tier (what an
  adapter declares, not what happened) · Capture accounting (the runner's own
  count of what it shipped)
- **Values** — `CaptureHealthState`: `ok` — evaluated, no gap · `gap` —
  evaluated, definitive gap · `unevaluated` — this broker positively states it
  did not evaluate this member, because the adapter mints no response id ·
  absent — an older broker with no opinion, which is a different claim from
  `unevaluated` and must not be collapsed into it. The core type additionally has
  `pending`, deliberately never surfaced and mapped to `ok` on the wire: healthy
  correlation lag leaves every normal turn briefly unmatched, so a user-visible
  "maybe" would flicker continuously on healthy traffic.

### Capture tier

An adapter's declaration of what its agent's native instrumentation actually
provides. A tier is a declaration, not an aspiration, and not a bar: a tier-0
runner is a legitimate runner, and what a runner does *not* capture is part of
its contract.

- **Names** — CLI runtime `CaptureTier` · wire `captureTier` on the
  `session_start` activity event and in adapter `meta` · UI "capture: `{n}`" on the
  session-started row · docs [Conformance](/docs/dev/conformance)
- **Not** — Capture health (whether capture is in fact arriving right now) ·
  Capture host (the runner-side sink that does the capturing) · a support level or
  a quality ranking
- **Values** — `0` Operable — spawns, serves the MCP toolbox, receives ambient
  events, clean teardown · `1` Observable — plus a live work-state signal from
  native instrumentation · `2` Traceable — plus normalized `llm_exchange` /
  `tool_action` / `user_prompt` content, redacted, in the activity stream ·
  `3` Full fidelity — plus verbatim request/response bodies into the inference
  and raw-body layer

### Capture accounting

The runner's own count, as of teardown, of how many activity events its uploader
saw, shipped and dropped. `dropped > 0` means the broker's copy of this run is
incomplete.

- **Names** — SDK `ActivitySessionEnd.capture` — `enqueued`, `uploaded`,
  `dropped`, and optionally `peakQueuedEvents`, `peakQueuedBytes`; the wire
  schema also accepts `retained` and `evictedWhileBlocked`, which the runner's
  `ActivityUploaderStats` produces and the TypeScript interface does not yet
  declare · CLI runtime `ActivityUploaderStats`, `RunSummary` · structured log
  record `msg: "run summary"` (`packages/cli/src/runtime/agent-session.ts:790`; the runner
  id rides the record's own `runner` field, not the message) · UI rendered on the
  `session-end` row · docs [Conformance](/docs/dev/conformance)
- **Not** — Run summary is not a second artifact: it is the operator-facing
  framing of these same facts, the structured log record and the stderr line at
  the end of a run. Under `--no-trace` there is no capture host, so no
  `session_end` event is uploaded at all and the log record carries
  `capture: null`. · Capture health (the broker's finding about stored bodies —
  the runner can report every event uploaded while none of the bodies arrived,
  which is exactly the failure capture health exists to catch)

### Run summary

The operator-facing account of one runner session at teardown: "machine-readable account
of one runner session, emitted on every exit path as the final structured log line"
(`packages/cli/src/runtime/agent-session.ts:86-87`), plus the human-readable stderr line
beside it.

- **Names** — CLI runtime `RunSummary` (`packages/cli/src/runtime/agent-session.ts:91-99`),
  `finishRun()` (`:750`), `emitSummary()` (`:784`) · structured log record
  `msg: "run summary"` (`:790`) carrying `runner`, `member`, `reason`, `exitCode`,
  `durationMs`, `agentSessionId` and `capture` · stderr line
  `csuite <runner-id>: session ended — exit <n> (<reason>) after <duration> · <capture>`
  (`:797-800`) · docs [Conformance](/docs/dev/conformance)
- **Not** — Capture accounting (the same numbers as wire fields on `session_end`; the run
  summary is their operator rendering, and it carries `capture: null` under `--no-trace`,
  where no `session_end` event is uploaded at all) · Session transcript (the agent's own
  record of the conversation, not the runner's account of the process) · Preflight (the
  checks before the run, not the account of it) · Generation (one agent-process lifetime
  inside the session; a runner session that restarts several times still emits exactly one
  summary, at teardown)
- **Verbs** — emitted, never requested

### Query source

Which interleaved lane of a member's work made a model call — the main
conversation, a named subagent, a server-tool sidecar, an away summary. It is
what stops a main-thread turn's time window from swallowing a subagent call that
ran inside it.

- **Names** — OTEL attribute `query_source` ("names the interleaved thread"),
  companion attribute `agent.name` · DB `gen_ai_inference.query_source` and
  `.agent_name`, `raw_exchange.query_source` and `.agent_name` · TS `querySource`
  and `agentName` on `RawBodyEnvelope` and `JoinableCall` · UI `sourceClass()`
  (collapses the main-thread spellings), `describeQuerySource()` → "main thread"
  / "subagent · X" / "web search" / "web fetch" / "away summary" / "model call"
  *(defined in code only)*
- **Not** — a database query or a query parameter: "query" here is the model call,
  a word inherited from an OTEL attribute the broker does not own, which is why
  the UI translates the value on every render · Agent name (`agent.name` names the
  named subagent, and only for named agents; `query_source` names the lane)
- **Values** — `repl_main_thread` and `codex_main_thread` (both collapse to the
  class `main`, as does an absent value) · `codex_subagent:<id>` ·
  `agent:<name>` and `agent:builtin:<name>` · `web_search_tool` ·
  `web_fetch_apply` · `away_summary`. Anything that is not main-thread is its own
  class and joins only its exact counterpart.

## Live state

A roster row answers three questions that the code keeps strictly apart and that
prose has repeatedly merged: is the link alive (**connection presence**), what is
the agent doing on it (**work state**), and can its runner act at all (**executor
state**). Four separate comments in the tree call these "orthogonal"
(`packages/sdk/src/types.ts:9-14`, `packages/core/src/work-state.ts:3-5`,
`packages/cli/src/runtime/trace/busy.ts:11-13`,
`packages/web-ui/src/lib/roster.ts:34-36`). None of the three is evidence for
either of the others, and the roster's published state of work is a *derivation*
over two of them rather than a copy of what any runner said.

### Connection presence

Live connection state for one member — "any member currently on the wire, whether
they're a human with a browser tab open or an agent with its MCP link alive"
(`packages/sdk/src/types.ts:228-232`). It is held in memory only, with no table
behind it, and every registered member appears in the roster list whether or not
anything is connected.

- **Names** — SDK `Presence`, `Presence.connected` (a count of live WebSocket
  subscribers) · broker `PresenceRegistry` · route `GET /roster` · CLI `csuite
  roster` column `connected` · MCP `roster` `connected=<n>` / `offline` · UI
  "ONLINE" / "OFFLINE" · docs [Presence](/docs/concepts/presence)
- **Not** — Work state (what the agent is doing on the link; a member can be
  connected and idle, or working and about to drop) · Link state (one runner
  process's own view of its single socket) · Executor state (whether that
  member's runners can act; connected members are routinely `unreported`) ·
  `RosterResponse.connected` (the list of every registered presence, not the
  connected ones — the field name is a count on the row and a list on the
  response) · `sessions.last_seen` (a durable column on a browser-session row,
  touched on cookie use; `Presence.lastSeen` is in-memory and bumped by
  `registerOrGet`, so on a long-lived socket it reads "connected at" despite the
  comment above it claiming it "reflects recent activity")
- **Verbs** — list (`GET /roster`); register and subscribe bump `lastSeen`

The glossary's current **Presence** headword — "whether they're connected, and
whether they're idle, working, or blocked" (`docs/concepts/glossary.mdx:78-79`) —
merges this entry with **live state** and is superseded by both. Presence is the
connection axis alone.

### Link state

One runner process's own three-state view of its single WebSocket to the broker,
flipped by the event forwarder around each subscribe attempt and read by exactly
one listener, the HUD strip at the bottom of `csuite claude`. It never reaches
the wire. *(defined in code only)*

- **Names** — runtime `PresenceState = 'connecting' | 'online' | 'offline'`,
  `createPresence()` (`packages/cli/src/runtime/presence.ts`) · CLI HUD dot in
  `csuite claude`
- **Not** — Connection presence (the broker's member-scoped `Presence` record,
  which counts every subscriber across every device) · Work state (this says
  nothing about turns or tools)
- **Values** — `connecting` a subscribe attempt is in flight · `online` the
  stream has yielded · `offline` the stream threw or closed, with a 250 ms
  debounce on the return to `online` so clean reconnects do not flicker

The ontology calls this **link state**; the type is spelled `PresenceState`
today, which collides with the broker's own `PresenceState` in
`packages/core/src/registry.ts` and with the wire's `Presence`. Three unrelated
records, one word, and no file references another.

### Work state

What an agent is doing right now on its link: `idle | working | needs_input`,
derived runner-side with priority `needs_input > working > idle` from two
in-flight counters (`turn_active`, `tool_inflight`) plus a human-blocking flag,
and reported to the broker on each transition and on a 10-second heartbeat while
non-idle. The broker holds the last non-idle report for 30 seconds; `idle` is
the absence of an entry, so a crashed runner lapses to `idle` rather than staying
stuck, while a live one keeps its hold alive by heartbeat.

- **Names** — SDK `WorkState`, `ActivityReport` *(D6 renames to
  `WorkStateReport`)* · route `POST /presence/activity` *(D6 renames to
  `/presence/work-state`)* · broker `WorkStateTracker`, `WORK_STATE_TTL_MS` ·
  SDK client `setActivity()` · runtime `ActivitySignal`, `ActivitySource`,
  `startActivityReporter()` · docs [Glossary](/docs/concepts/glossary)
- **Not** — Live state (what `/roster` publishes; a `working` report never
  reaches it) · Objective status `blocked` (an objective its assignee has
  sequenced behind something — the entrenched owner of that word, rendered in
  the same caution color two components away) · Activity stream (the durable
  record of what a member did; work state is forgotten after 30 seconds) ·
  `WorkStateTracker.isBusy()` and `ActivityReport.busy` (booleans that mean
  "`working`" and cannot express `needs_input`, so any reader taking one as the
  whole signal loses the state that asks a human to look; the broker parses the
  reported `busy` and ignores it, deriving the boolean from `state`) ·
  Connection presence
- **Values** — `idle`: no turn open and no tool in flight · `working`: a turn is
  open or a tool is in flight, for the whole turn and not just tool windows ·
  `needs_input`: waiting on a person for an approval or an answer it cannot
  self-resolve — a person should look
- **Verbs** — report (runner to broker), get
- **Decided** — D7: `needs_input` · over keeping `blocked` · 2026-09-03. The
  wire still spells it `blocked`; every human surface already says "NEEDS
  INPUT", so the code catches up with the UI and `ObjectiveStatus.blocked` keeps
  the word.

### Live state

The state of work `/roster` publishes for a member, and a derivation rather than
a report: `needs_input` when the runner reported it inside the window, `working`
when the executor recorded proven action inside the same window, `idle`
otherwise — in which case the field is omitted. A runner that reports `working`
and does nothing else reads `idle` on the roster; that is deliberate, so that
scheduling telemetry can never make a member look capable.

- **Names** — SDK `Presence.activity` *(D6 renames the wire to
  `Presence.workState`)*, `Presence.busy`, `RosterResponse.activityWindowMs`
  *(D6 renames to `workStateWindowMs`)* · route `GET /roster` · web
  `presenceActivity()` · UI "WORKING" / "NEEDS INPUT", a11y "working" / "needs
  input" · docs [Presence](/docs/concepts/presence)
- **Not** — Work state (the runner's own report; the tracker's `working` is
  input to nothing on the wire) · Proven action (the evidence `working` is
  derived from, published separately as `lastActedAt`) · Executor state (the
  ability to act, not the state of work) · Connection presence
- **Values** — `working`: proven action within `activityWindowMs` · `blocked`
  (D7: `needs_input`): a runner report within the same window · `idle`: neither,
  and the field is absent

`Presence.activity`'s own doc-comment describes it as the runner's report; the
code has not worked that way since the projection was rewritten
(`packages/core/src/app.ts:1366-1391`), and a test pins the current behavior
(`apps/server/test/presence-activity.test.ts:121-144` — a `working` report
yields `activity: undefined`). Read the derivation, not the type comment. D6
renames the field to `Presence.workState`, which gives a derived field the
reporter's word: the two remain separate concepts, and this entry and **work
state** say which is which.

The field also exists only on `GET /roster`: `GET /team/status` carries the
identical `Presence` type built straight from `broker.listPresences()`, so it
never has this field, and neither `csuite roster` nor the MCP `roster` tool
renders it — an agent deciding who to hand work to cannot see that a teammate
needs input. And `activityWindowMs` publishes `WORK_STATE_TTL_MS`, one constant
doing two jobs under a third name: the tracker's report expiry and this
projection's "acted recently" window. Tuning one moves the other.

### Executor state

A member-level roll-up of whether that member's connected runners can act:
`ready` if any runner subscription reports ready, `degraded` if at least one
exists and all of them are degraded, `unreported` otherwise. It is computed
every time presences are listed — for `/roster` and `/team/status` alike — and
never stored, so a member with one ready runner and one degraded runner is
`ready`, an answer no single runner gave.

- **Names** — SDK `ExecutorState`, `Presence.executor` · broker
  `PresenceRegistry.list()` · route `GET /roster`, `GET /team/status` · CLI
  `csuite roster` column `executor`, `csuite team status`
  `executor=<state> reason=<code>` · MCP `roster` `executor=<state>` · UI
  "DEGRADED · `{code}`" · docs [Presence](/docs/concepts/presence)
- **Not** — Runner (one connected agent process, with its own identity and
  version; the executor is the roll-up over that member's runner subscriptions,
  and with two runners disagreeing it equals neither) · Runner condition (one
  subscription's own statement, the input to this aggregate) · Work state (a
  `working` member may still be `unreported` here) · Connection presence
- **Values** — `ready`: at least one runner subscription says it can act ·
  `degraded`: every runner subscription has classified itself degraded — a
  subscription that has sent no condition frame is not a degraded one, so a
  single silent runner beside a degraded one leaves the member `unreported`; a
  revoked credential is classified degraded with reason `auth_blocked` even when
  the runner itself said nothing · `unreported`: this broker understands the
  field and no connected runner supplied enough evidence to classify it
- **Verbs** — get (as part of a roster or team-status read); it is never written
  directly

The docs gloss `degraded` as "runner subscriptions exist and none is ready"
(`docs/concepts/presence.mdx:88-90`). The code requires more than that: every
classification must be `degraded` (`packages/core/src/registry.ts:171-174`).
Silence is not degradation.

### Runner condition

One runner subscription's last statement about whether it can complete turns,
sent as a control frame on its own subscribe WebSocket. The reason is a fixed,
code-owned string: "adapters must never forward raw payloads", because adapter
error text can carry credentials, endpoints, or prompt fragments.

- **Names** — SDK `RunnerConditionFrame`, `RUNNER_CONDITION_CODES`,
  `RunnerConditionCode`, `RunnerControlFrame` · broker
  `Broker.runnerCondition()` · runtime `RUNNER_CONDITION_DETAIL`,
  `classifyRunnerFailure()` · UI "Runner degraded: `{code}`"
- **Not** — Executor state (the member-level aggregate over these frames, where
  `degraded` means every subscription is) · Auth blocked (a credential fact the
  broker synthesizes into a condition; no runner emits `auth_blocked` about
  itself) · Live state (a degraded runner can still be reported as needing
  input)
- **Values** — `state`: `ready` `degraded` · `reason.code`: `no_credential`
  `invalid_model` `model_unavailable` `server_overloaded` `auth_blocked`
  `unknown`

### Proven action

Evidence that a runner did something outside itself — a tool call or an outbound
effect — recorded from turn frames and published as the time of the most recent
one. Receiving a message or opening a turn is not action: an open turn without
evidence never projects `working`, and a turn that completes without it records
`no_action`, which is what makes silent burn visible.

- **Names** — SDK `RunnerTurnFrame`, `ExecutorState.lastActedAt`, `.activeTurns`,
  `.lastTurnAt`, `.lastTurnOutcome` · broker `Broker.runnerTurn()` · CLI `csuite
  roster` column `last_acted`, `csuite team status` `last-acted=` · MCP `roster`
  `last-acted=`, `active-turns=` · docs [Presence](/docs/concepts/presence)
  ("Recent action")
- **Not** — Work state `working` (the runner's claim about itself, which this
  does not read) · Live state `working` (this evidence aged against the 30 s
  window) · Activity stream (the durable record of what was done; a turn frame
  deliberately omits the tool name, because liveness proves that a seat acted,
  not what it did)
- **Values** — `outcome`: `acted` evidence arrived · `no_action` the turn closed
  with none · `failed` the turn did not close on its own, including via the
  8-hour exitability backstop, which force-fails open turns and also marks that
  subscription `degraded` with reason `unknown`

### Degraded

The reserved sense of the word: a runner subscription, or the member-level
roll-up over that member's subscriptions, cannot complete turns. It is always
about the ability to act, and it always carries a code-owned reason.

- **Names** — SDK `RunnerConditionFrame.state` `'degraded'`,
  `ExecutorState.state` `'degraded'`, `TeamStatusMember.stalledReasons`
  `executor_degraded` · CLI `csuite roster` `executor` column, `csuite team
  status` `executor=degraded reason=<code>` · MCP `roster`
  `executor=degraded(<code>)` · UI "DEGRADED · `{code}`", "Runner degraded:
  `{code}`" · docs [Presence](/docs/concepts/presence)
- **Not** — `Presence.diagnosticsRetention` `'degraded'` / core
  `RetentionHealth` (the diagnostics store is shedding detail; it prints on the
  same roster row and says nothing about whether the member's runners can act) ·
  `MessageDispositionReasonCode` `'degraded'` (why one message could not be
  delivered: the subscription that held it degraded — a consequence of this
  sense, not a second one) · Work state (a degraded member can still report
  `needs_input`) · Executor `unreported` (no classification arrived, which is
  not a negative classification)

`stalledReasons`' `executor_degraded` is the spelling to copy: it names the
subsystem beside the word. Every unqualified `degraded` outside the
runner/executor pair should acquire one.

### Unreported

The reserved sense of the word: *this broker understands the field, and no
evidence arrived*. It is a claim about the arrival of evidence, never about the
thing the evidence would have described, and it is distinct from an absent field,
which means an older broker with no opinion at all.

- **Names** — SDK `ExecutorState.state` `'unreported'` · CLI `csuite roster`
  `executor` column, `csuite team status` `executor=unreported` · MCP `roster`
  "unreported (broker predates executor evidence)" · docs
  [Presence](/docs/concepts/presence)
- **Not** — `Presence.unreportedConnections` (live connections that sent no
  validated client identity — an identity fact, better named
  `unidentifiedConnections`) · delivery-ledger `unreported` (a message pushed to
  a runner whose subscription predates acknowledgement support, so no ack can
  ever arrive — a protocol-version fact stored in `message_deliveries.state`
  beside real outcomes, better named `unackable`) · a supervision claim rendered
  "unreported" (the runner made no claim about how it was started) ·
  `versionSkew.brokerVersion: '(unreported)'` (the broker declined to name its
  own version) · an absent field

Three of these print within fifteen lines of one another in a single `csuite team
status` block, and three more in one web roster row. Keep the word for the
executor sense and rename the rest.

### Stalled

A derived verdict, not a state: an `active` or `blocked` objective is stalled when its
newest measured signal is older than a window the caller supplies —
`generatedAt - lastSignalAt > duration` — and a member is stalled when any of their
non-terminal objectives is, or when their executor is degraded. Equality is not stalled;
an objective with no signal at all is stalled; and with no duration passed, nothing is
stalled at all. Every other expiry in this family is a fixed constant, and this is the
only one the caller sets.

- **Names** — SDK `TeamStatusObjective.stalled`, `.staleSignals`,
  `TeamStatusStaleSignal`, `TeamStatusMember.stalled`, `.stalledReasons`,
  `TeamStatusResponse.stalledAfterMs` (the window echoed back) · route `GET
  /team/status?stalled_ms=` *(ships as `stalledMs`; D44)* · CLI `csuite team status
  --stalled <duration>`, output `STALLED missing=…` · MCP `team_status` (`stalled`) ·
  docs [Running a team](/docs/guides/running-a-team)
- **Not** — Objective status `blocked` (a state the assignee declares, with a
  `blockReason`; a blocked objective that is being discussed is not stalled, and an
  active one nobody has touched is) · terminal work (stalledness is measured only on
  non-terminal objectives — `TeamStatusObjective.status` is narrowed to `active |
  blocked`) · the work-state TTL (a 30-second lapse of a report back to `idle`; nothing
  is called stalled there) · `sweepStaleSessionLogs()` (a seven-day sweep of runner
  artifacts on disk) · a leftover unix socket, and the other implementation uses of
  "stale" — there is no unified staleness concept to go looking for · Executor state
  `degraded` (an inability to act, and the other reason a member is stalled) ·
  Restart-pending (a member running superseded instructions)
- **Values** — `staleSignals`: `thread_post` (an `objective_discuss` post in the
  objective's thread) · `pr_link` (such a post carrying a canonical GitHub pull-request
  URL; a bare `#241` does not count) · `lifecycle` (the newest row in that objective's
  `objective_events` log) — `stalledReasons`: `objective_stale` `executor_degraded`
- **Verbs** — get (`GET /team/status`); the broker composes it and no client recomputes
  it
- One judgement is spelled two ways in one response shape — `stalled` beside
  `staleSignals`, and `stalledReasons: 'objective_stale'`. `stalled` is the user-facing
  stem, and the others should follow it.
- **Decided** — D44: query parameters are snake_case, over camelCase, so the current
  `?stalledMs=` spelling is legacy (2026-09-03)

## Execution runtime

What actually runs when an agent is on the team: a runner process, the agent
process it spawns, the adapter that tells the two apart, and the controls that
replace the agent without ending the runner. The distinction the whole family
turns on is **runner vs agent vs generation** — one runner process spawns a
succession of agent processes, and the `session_start`/`session_end` pair on the
wire brackets each one of those, not the runner.

### Runner

The parent process that owns one csuite session: it authenticates to the broker,
holds the instructions, the event forwarder, the objectives tracker, the IPC
socket and the capture host, and spawns one agent under itself. "The csuite
runner — the parent process that owns a csuite session."

- **Names** — SDK `RunnerIdentity` · `RunnerReport` · `ClientIdentity {kind:'runner'}` ·
  headers `X-CSUITE-Runner-Version`, `X-CSUITE-Runner-Identity` ·
  CLI `csuite claude` · `csuite codex` · `csuite stub` ·
  code `startRunner`, `RunnerHandle`, `RunnerOptions`, `RunnerStartupError` ·
  env `CSUITE_RUNNER_SOCKET` · UI "Runner cannot act" ·
  docs [Runners overview](/docs/runners/overview), [Glossary](/docs/concepts/glossary)
- **Not** — Agent (the framework child process the runner spawns; it exits with the
  runner) · Executor state (live-state: `ExecutorState`, the roll-up across every
  subscription this member holds of whether its runners are proving they can
  complete turns — a projection of the runner, not a second thing that runs
  agents) · Member (people-and-seats: the seat on the team, which persists across
  every runner that fills it) · Harness (not this concept anywhere — the word is
  taken by server test fixtures)
- **Verbs** — start (`csuite <runner>`) · install-service · cycle · shutdown
  (`RunnerHandle.shutdown`)

### Runner id

The stable symbol for which agent framework a runner runs. "Stable runner id:
`'claude'`, `'codex'`, ... Used as the session-log component, the run-summary
`runner` field, and the `csuite <id>:` banner prefix. Kebab-case, matches the CLI
verb." One producer, `AgentAdapterMeta.id`, populates every field that carries it.

- **Names** — code `AgentAdapterMeta.id` · SDK `RunnerIdentity.runner`,
  `ActivitySessionStart.runner`, `ActivitySessionEnd.runner` · code `RunSummary.runner` ·
  CLI verbs `claude` `codex` `stub` · UI `item.runner`, beside "session started"
- **Not** — `ActivityLlmExchange.agent` / `ActivityToolAction.agent` /
  `ActivityUserPrompt.agent` (the same value under a second name, because those
  event families were authored later; `runner` is canonical and `agent` is the
  alias) · Subagent name (`GenAiInference.agentName`, `gen_ai_inference.agent_name`
  — the named subagent that made one model call, `general-purpose` for the builtin
  one) · `claude-code` (a deprecated CLI verb alias only; nothing has emitted it
  as a runner id since the rename, though both SDK doc examples still show it)
- **Values** — `claude` · `codex` · `stub` — the stub is a test/CI instrument that
  proves the runner lifecycle with no model credential, never a member you deploy.
  `RunnerIdentity.runner` closes the union; the activity events type the same
  value as a free string so a third-party adapter can declare its own `meta.id`

### Agent

The LLM-driven child process the runner spawns and holds a handle to. It has no
broker identity of its own: everything the team sees — the member, the
permissions, the traces — belongs to the runner that spawned it.

- **Names** — code `AgentProcess` · `AgentAdapterMeta.displayName` ("Claude Code",
  "OpenAI Codex") · docs [Architecture](/docs/dev/architecture),
  [Runners overview](/docs/runners/overview)
- **Not** — Runner (the parent process; the agent runs under it and exits with it) ·
  Subagent name (`GenAiInference.agentName` — a named subagent inside one agent's
  own run, not a framework) · `csuite push --agent <id>` (the recipient **member**;
  the docs already render the flag as "Recipient member") · `Teammate.kind: 'agent'`
  (people-and-seats: whether a seat is held by a person or by an agent)
- **Verbs** — spawn · signal · shutdown

### Adapter

The formal interface between the shared agent-session driver and one agent
framework: "An adapter implements ONLY what is specific to one agent framework:
`locate()` / `runnerOptions` / `prepare()` / `spawn()` / `doctor()`." Everything
broker-side is shared and lives in the driver, so an adapter is what a new runner
author writes and nothing else.

- **Names** — code `AgentAdapter` · `AgentAdapterMeta` · `AgentAdapterError` ·
  `AgentSessionContext` · `AgentPrepared` · `RespawnPosture` · `CaptureTier` ·
  `TestedVersionRange` · `AgentDoctorCheck` · `RunnerOptions.channelSink` (what
  `runnerOptions()` supplies) ·
  docs [Runner conformance](/docs/dev/conformance)
- **Not** — Agent-session driver (`runAgentSession`, `AgentSessionInput` — the one
  lifecycle every adapter is handed to; the adapter is the interface, the driver
  is the sequence) · `csuite-core/conformance` (the broker's port contract suites
  for injected drivers — a different published surface that shares the word) ·
  "notification sink" (no such field; the contract's own header still uses the
  pre-rename name for the **channel sink**, and `channelSink` is the only spelling
  in the code)
- **Values** — `signals`: `forward` (the agent owns the terminal; SIGINT/SIGTERM
  are forwarded to it) · `teardown` (the runner owns the terminal; signals end the
  session) — `RespawnPosture`: `{resume:false}` is the only posture any driver path
  passes; `{resume:true, sessionId}` survives for the posture's own contract tests
  and is documented as the instruction-restart path it no longer is

### Runner session

Everything one `csuite <runner>` invocation does, from auth to teardown: one
runner process, one member identity, one broker subscription, one IPC socket. It
is the unit an operator starts and stops.

- **Names** — code `runAgentSession` · `RunSummary` · session log
  `~/.cache/commandsuite/session-<pid>.log` · CLI banner `csuite <id>: session ended` ·
  docs [Runners overview](/docs/runners/overview)
- **Not** — Generation (one agent-process lifetime *inside* this; a runner session
  usually contains several, and it is the generation that the wire brackets mark) ·
  Web session (auth-credentials: the cookie-backed web-UI login, table `sessions`,
  cookie `csuite_session` — unrelated and never joined to this) · Agent session id
  (the framework's own conversation id) · Unit cycle (`csuite <runner> cycle` — the
  supervisor replacing this whole process with a new one)

### Generation

One agent-process lifetime inside a runner session. This is what
`session_start`/`session_end` actually bracket: a restart, a `clear` and a
`reload` each close the current bracket and open a new one without the runner
process going anywhere. *(defined in code only)*

- **Names** — code `generationStartedAt`, `attachGenerationWatch`, `spawnGeneration` ·
  SDK `ActivitySessionStart` / `ActivitySessionEnd` · DB `member_activity.kind` ·
  UI "session started" / "session ended" · docs [Activity and
  traces](/docs/concepts/activity-and-traces) ("restart, clear, and reload
  generations")
- **Not** — Runner session (the whole invocation; counting `session_start` rows
  counts generations, not runner launches, and the newest one is not when the
  runner started) · Agent turn (a response cycle inside one generation)
- **Values** — `resumeReason` on a successor's `session_start`, the only wire
  evidence of why the predecessor ended: `instructions changed` · `environment
  changed` · `instructions and environment changed` (the two coalesced into one
  swap) · `context cleared` · `environment reloaded`. A first generation carries a
  `resumeReason` in exactly one case — codex under a bare `--resume`, which stamps
  `bare --resume: codex threads resume by id only` — and otherwise carries none

Every `session_end` reports its own generation's duration, and so does the
`run summary` log line at teardown — the `RunSummary` type doc calls itself an
account of "one runner session", but every `finishRun` after the first spawn is
handed `generationStartedAt` rather than the invocation's start. (The one
exception is the spawn-failure path, which closes the bracket before any
generation exists.) The code is authoritative: these numbers measure a
generation.

### Agent session id

The agent framework's own identity for its conversation — a Claude Code session
id, a codex thread id. "Agent-native session identity … for the run summary and
resume hints. Return `null` when unknown."

- **Names** — code `AgentProcess.sessionId()` · SDK `ActivitySessionEnd.agentSessionId` ·
  code `RunSummary.agentSessionId` · core `RawBodyEnvelope.sessionId` ·
  DB `raw_exchange.session_id` · CLI `--resume [<sessionId>]` (claude),
  `--resume [<threadId>]` (codex) · code codex `Thread.id`, `ThreadStartResponse` ·
  docs [Runners overview](/docs/runners/overview)
- **Not** — Runner session (the csuite invocation; no session id is carried on
  `session_start` at all, which is why capture health bounds its window on
  `created_at` instead) · Generation · Web session (auth-credentials: the cookie
  login row in `sessions`) — the bare `sessionId` / `session_id` spellings on the
  raw-body envelope and column are the ones most likely to be read as either
- **Verbs** — resume — an operator's lever only. No driver-initiated swap ever
  resumes it: the hook contract calls the id "diagnostics only — the successor
  never resumes it"

### Agent context

The model's live conversation window inside one generation: the fixed context the runner
pinned, plus every turn since. It is what `compact`, `clear` and `reload` act on, and the
only sense of `context` that is not durable prose. *(defined in code only)*

- **Names** — route `POST /members/:name/context` (`packages/sdk/src/protocol.ts:195`,
  handler `packages/core/src/app.ts:5735`) · permission leaf `members.context` — "Ask another
  member's runner to compact or clear its agent context"
  (`packages/sdk/src/types.ts:65`, leaf at `:79`) · MCP `context_control`, "Ask a runner
  to compact, clear, or reload its agent context"
  (`packages/cli/src/runtime/tools.ts:296`) · the phrase "never enters agent context",
  which is how four secret-and-credential tool descriptions
  (`packages/cli/src/runtime/tools.ts:959`, `:1247`, `:3530`, `:3991`) and
  [Secrets](/docs/concepts/secrets) (`docs/concepts/secrets.mdx:117`) say a value stays
  out of it
- **Not** — Team context (durable team configuration on the `team` row, and the other
  claimant on the `context` leaf split) · Fixed context (the standing bundle the runner
  pins into this at spawn) · Session transcript (Claude's on-disk record of this
  conversation; a codex member's equivalent is a rollout, which is why the record and the
  window need separate names) · Objective re-brief (a push *into* this window, not the
  window) · Context pack (`packages/sdk/src/context-packs.ts` — not shipped)
- **Verbs** — compact, clear, reload, all three through Context control. Nothing reads it
  back over the wire: the broker can shrink or replace a member's window and can never
  see inside it.

### Context control

The broker verb for making a live agent's context smaller or newer without
ending its runner. "NEITHER IS `shutdown`. The runner process — and with it the
broker subscription, the IPC socket the MCP bridge reconnects to, the objectives
tracker and the capture host — outlives both verbs."

- **Names** — SDK `ContextControlVerb`, `ContextControlOutcome`,
  `ContextControlRequest`, `ContextControlResponse`, `ActivityContextControl` ·
  route `POST /members/:name/context` · permission leaf `members.context` ·
  MCP `context_control` · code `createContextControlCoordinator`, `CompactAttempt` ·
  DB `member_activity.kind = 'context_control'` · UI "context `{verb}`" ·
  docs [Runners overview](/docs/runners/overview#context-control),
  [Permissions](/docs/concepts/permissions)
- **Not** — Agent restart (broker-fanned, triggered by an instructions or
  environment change, and it emits no `context_control` row at all — only the
  session bracket) · Unit cycle · `delivered` on `ContextControlResponse` (whether
  the target had ANY live subscriber at push time, not whether a runner acted; the
  ack row's `outcome` is the only evidence of that)
- **Values** — `verb`: `compact` — cooperative and in place, the agent does the
  summarizing and may refuse · `clear` — imposed cold swap, instructions refetched ·
  `reload` — imposed cold swap, instructions **and** environment refetched. All
  three ship, but the `ContextControlVerb` doc block defines only the first two,
  so `reload`'s definition is this line. `clear` and `reload` both respawn cold,
  and so does every restart; the broker's push text and the MCP tool description
  still say `reload` "resumes", and the code says otherwise. `outcome`: `applied` ·
  `declined` (asked and refused, with the framework's reason) · `unsupported`
  (this runner has no such op; retrying cannot help) · `failed` (the runner tried
  and something broke). A request with no outcome stays visibly outstanding
- **Verbs** — request (`POST`), acknowledge (the runner's `context_control` row)

### Agent restart

The drain-and-restart cycle a runner performs on itself when the broker says the
member's instructions or environment changed: wait for idle, detach ambient
input, stop the agent, refetch instructions and environment, respawn cold.
"DRAIN, DON'T KILL … The successor does not resume the predecessor's
conversation."

- **Names** — code `createRestartCoordinator`, `RestartReason`, `RestartHooks`,
  `describeRestartReasons` · core `restartPendingMembers` ·
  SDK `RosterResponse.restartPending` · docs [Runners
  overview](/docs/runners/overview)
- **Not** — Unit cycle (`csuite <runner> cycle` — systemd replaces the whole runner
  unit, so the member drops off the net and comes back; this replaces only the
  agent, and the runner keeps its broker subscription and MCP wiring) · `clear`
  (context control: a cold swap that refetches instructions only, requested per
  member over `/members/:name/context` and acknowledged with a `context_control`
  row — a restart refetches the environment too, so `reload` is the verb it
  mechanically matches) · Resume (no driver-initiated respawn resumes: "Resuming
  here was the bug")
- **Values** — `RestartReason`: `instructions` · `environment` — both run the same
  cycle, refetching both; the distinction survives only in the successor's
  `resumeReason`

### Agent turn

One framework-level response cycle: the unit codex makes a first-class object and
claude leaves implicit as a streaming-input round. It is what the runner counts
to prove a member can still act.

- **Names** — code codex `Turn`, `TurnStartParams`, `TurnSteerParams`
  (`expectedTurnId`), `TurnInterruptParams`; methods `turn/start`, `turn/steer`,
  `turn/interrupt` · SDK `RunnerTurnFrame` (`turnId`, `phase`, `outcome`) ·
  SDK `ExecutorState.activeTurns`, `lastTurnAt`, `lastTurnOutcome` ·
  SDK `MessageDispositionReasonCode` `turn_failed`
- **Not** — Turn (observation-capture's headword for the UI's rendering unit, one
  `llm_exchange` — 1:1 with an agent turn for claude, 1:N for codex, "whose turns
  genuinely aggregate several Responses-API calls") · Turn spine
  (observation-capture: the join hanging inference records under those markers) ·
  Generation (the process lifetime the turns happen inside)
- **Values** — `phase`: `started` · `completed` — `outcome`: `acted` · `no_action` ·
  `failed`. The frame deliberately omits the tool or effect name: "liveness proves
  that, not what"

### Doctor

The command that runs a runner's local checks and prints the report: "`csuite
<runner> --doctor` — preflight checks for any agent runner. The doctor is
adapter-generic: every runner gets the same shared checks, plus whatever the
adapter declares." It never contacts a broker and never spawns the agent proper,
and it retains nothing.

- **Names** — code `runAgentDoctor`, `DoctorReport`, `DoctorCheck`, `CheckStatus`,
  `formatReport` · adapter hook `AgentAdapter.doctor()` returning
  `AgentDoctorCheck[]` · `AgentAdapterMeta.testedVersions` / `TestedVersionRange`
  (the range the version check WARNs outside of) · CLI flags `--doctor`,
  `--skip-doctor` · docs [Runner conformance](/docs/dev/conformance) ("Doctor
  preflight")
- **Not** — Runner condition (live-state: `RunnerConditionCode` /
  `RunnerConditionFrame`, a running runner's own degraded classification reported
  to the broker — the doctor is local and pre-session) · Diagnostics
  (diagnostics-completeness: broker-side records that are retained; a doctor
  report is printed and dropped) · Runner conformance suite (contributor-side: the
  adapter test kit a new runner must pass, not a check an operator runs) · Port
  conformance suites (the broker's published port contracts, which prove a driver, not
  an environment)
- **Values** — `CheckStatus`: `PASS` · `WARN` · `FAIL`. Only a FAIL aborts a
  session or exits non-zero; a version outside the tested range is a WARN and
  never a FAIL, because agents ship faster than the ranges do
- **Verbs** — `--doctor` prints the full report and exits 0/1; the silent
  preflight before every session runs the same checks minus the version probe,
  and `--skip-doctor` opts out

### Preflight

The doctor's checks as they run *without* the command: every runner session runs them
silently before spawning the agent, and only a `FAIL` aborts. The same checks minus the
version probe, because spawning the agent binary to read a version adds latency to every
start (`packages/cli/src/commands/doctor.ts:63-66`). *(defined in code only)*

- **Names** — code `runAgentDoctor` — "Run the shared + adapter-specific preflight checks
  for one runner" (`packages/cli/src/commands/doctor.ts:94`) ·
  `AgentDoctorOptions.includeVersion: false` on the silent pass · CLI flag
  `--skip-doctor`, stderr "`csuite claude: preflight FAILED — fix the above or pass
  --skip-doctor to bypass`" (`packages/cli/src/index.ts:1054`, and the same line per
  runner at `:1221` and `:1379`) · docs
  [CLI reference](/docs/reference/cli)
- **Not** — Doctor (the command, the flag and the `DoctorReport` object; preflight is that
  same report run with no command and printed only when it fails) · Check status (one
  line's verdict inside it) · Port conformance suites (a published test contract for
  injected drivers, not a local environment check) · the `install-service` auth preflight
  (`packages/cli/src/commands/install-service.ts:342`), a different pre-check on a
  different command
- **Verbs** — run, abort on `FAIL`, bypass with `--skip-doctor`

## Standing context

The durable prose a member carries into every agent session — the team's context, the
member's role description, their personal instructions, and the team process — plus
the hash that versions it and the notice that says it moved. Two mistakes are built
into the current vocabulary. **Standing context is the whole bundle, not
`team.context`**: the SDK, the MCP tool text and the web UI all describe team context
as "the standing context every member inherits", and a reader who takes that literally
will think one field is the whole story. And **`instructions` is two different strings
on one wire**: the member's own private block, and the entire composed system prompt
that contains it.

### Instruction block

One operator-authored unit of standing prose composed into a member's fixed context,
identified on the wire by a pinned `kind` string and by the sha256 of its exact text.
`GET /instructions` returns a descriptor for each block actually present; an empty
block is omitted rather than descriptor'd, so a packet carries between zero and four.

- **Names** — SDK `InstructionBlockKind` (`packages/sdk/src/types.ts:515-519`),
  `InstructionBlockDescriptor` `{kind, sha256}` (`:527-530`),
  `InstructionsResponse.blocks` (`:571`) · core `instructionBlocks()` in
  `packages/core/src/instructions.ts:208` · docs headword "Instruction blocks"
  ([Glossary](/docs/concepts/glossary)), [REST API](/docs/dev/rest-api)
- **Not** — Composed instructions (three of the four kinds are composed *into* that
  string, and their membership test is a substring match on it; `team_process` never
  is, so its test is that the broker sent it) · Fixed context (the whole rendered
  bundle — the glossary headword "Instruction blocks" names that bundle while the wire
  enum names its parts, the same word at two grains) · Everything else that moves the
  composed hash (the team-name line, every teammate's roster line and the
  external-notification doctrine section are composed into the same prose and hashed
  with it, and none has a kind or a descriptor; a client that reads `blocks` as the
  full inventory of a session's standing text is short by three) ·
  `persistent_context kind="…"` and `context.block.kind` (the "wire and telemetry
  contract" the enum's own doc-comment gives as the reason the strings are pinned;
  neither attribute exists anywhere in the tree — see the context watchdog under Fixed
  context)
- **Values** — `team_context` · `role_description` · `personal_instructions` ·
  `team_process`

### Composed instructions
