The single prose string the broker renders for one member — the team line, `You:`,
"Your role here", the team context, the personal instructions, the teammate roster and
the broker-authored doctrine sections — which the runner pins into the agent's system
prompt and which is "FROZEN for the lifetime of the session"
(`packages/core/src/instructions.ts:8`). The composer's own comment claims the
response "also carries … raw `instructions` (just the member's own personal
directives)" (`instructions.ts:101-104`); it does not — the composed string overwrites
that field.

- **Names** — SDK `InstructionsResponse.instructions` · route `GET /instructions` ·
  core `composeInstructions()` (`packages/core/src/instructions.ts:106`),
  `composePrompt()` (`:238`) · runner `systemPrompt` `append` (claude-code,
  `packages/cli/src/runtime/agents/claude-agent.ts:478`), `developerInstructions`
  (codex, `packages/cli/src/runtime/agents/codex/adapter.ts:729-741`) ·
  docs [REST API](/docs/dev/rest-api)
- **Not** — Personal instructions (`Member.instructions`, the member's own private
  block and one input to this string; `InstructionsResponse extends Member`
  (`packages/sdk/src/types.ts:541`), which is why one field name carries both) · Fixed
  context (this string *plus* the rendered team-process block — what the agent
  actually receives) · Team process (rides in its own packet field and is never inside
  this string) · The web UI's `instructions` signal and its "Loading instructions…"
  state (`packages/web-ui/src/lib/instructions.ts:13`,
  `packages/web-ui/src/components/MemberProfile.tsx:108`) — both hold the whole packet,
  not this string · `composeInstructions` in `packages/sdk/src/context-packs.ts` (a
  different function of a different signature, never called, asserting a composition
  order the broker does not implement)

### Personal instructions

"Personal working directives + context for this member … Not visible to teammates —
this is private to the member and to `members.manage` holders"
(`packages/sdk/src/types.ts:220-223`). One of the four operator-authored instruction
block kinds.

- **Names** — SDK `Member.instructions` · DB `members.instructions` · wire kind
  `personal_instructions` · route `PATCH /members/:name` ·
  CLI `csuite member update --instructions` · MCP `members_update` argument
  `instructions` · UI "personal instructions" · docs [Members](/docs/concepts/members)
- **Not** — Composed instructions (the whole system prompt, returned under the same
  field name by `GET /instructions`) · Role description (the public per-member block
  every teammate sees) · Team context (team-wide, one copy, edited by `team.manage`) ·
  Instruction block (this is one of four kinds; [Members](/docs/concepts/members)
  heads this single block "Instructions", which reads as the whole and is not)

### Team context

The team-level standing prose: "what the team is here to do plus any background every
member should carry … durable team configuration"
(`packages/sdk/src/types.ts:156-160`). Rendered to the agent as one `Context: <text>`
line, omitted from the prose when blank, and overwritten in place — it has no version
and no history, and the `team` row's `updated_at` / `updated_by` columns reach no wire
type, so nothing a client sees names an author.

- **Names** — SDK `Team.context` · DB `team.context` · wire kind `team_context` ·
  route `GET`/`PATCH /team` · CLI `csuite team set --context` / `--context-file` · MCP
  `team_update` argument `context` · UI "Team context" · core `validateTeamContext()`
  in `packages/core/src/members-domain.ts:86`
- **Not** — Standing context (the whole bundle of blocks an agent starts a session
  with; the SDK (`packages/sdk/src/types.ts:153`), the `team_update` argument text and
  the UI's "Standing context every member inherits" all use the phrase for this one
  field, and that narrow use is the drift) · Agent context (the running conversation,
  acted on by `POST /members/:name/context`) · Objective re-brief (the runner's
  `context_refresh` push, which carries the open objective plate, not standing prose) ·
  Context pack (`packages/sdk/src/context-packs.ts` — not exported from the SDK index,
  no subpath export, no member field, no route, no caller outside its own test: not
  shipped, and not a concept)

### Role description

The public per-member prose — "shared public context: what this member does on the
team, visible to every teammate in the roster and instruction packet"
(`packages/sdk/src/types.ts:175-179`). It is composed into every teammate's prompt as
a roster line, so editing one member's description moves every other member's composed
hash.

- **Names** — SDK `Role.description` · DB `members.role_description` · wire kind
  `role_description` · route `PATCH /members/:name` ·
  CLI `csuite member update --description` · MCP `members_update` argument
  `description` · UI "role description", "Role description" ·
  docs [Glossary](/docs/concepts/glossary), [Members](/docs/concepts/members)
- **Not** — Personal instructions (the private block, delivered only to its own
  member) · `Role.title` (the short label — "engineer", "qa-lead"; the description is
  the prose beside it) · Team role (the seat's label-and-prose pair, defined elsewhere)

### Fixed context

Everything the runner hands the agent as its standing instructions: the composed
instructions string followed by the rendered team-process block. `composeFixedContext`
is "the one place that knows the packet is more than its composed `instructions`
string" (`packages/cli/src/runtime/fixed-context.ts:87-93`), and absence of a team
process renders as an explicit line — "unavailable" for a broker that sent no field,
"none has been set" for a broker that sent `null` — never as nothing.
*(defined in code only)*

- **Names** — runner `composeFixedContext()`, `renderTeamProcessBlock()` in
  `packages/cli/src/runtime/fixed-context.ts` · SDK "instruction packet",
  `InstructionsResponse` (`packages/sdk/src/types.ts:532-541`) — the wire form of the
  same bundle · web UI `instructions` signal
  (`packages/web-ui/src/lib/instructions.ts:13`), whose loading state reads
  "Loading instructions…" · MCP `team_process_get` description
  ("already in your fixed context") · prose "standing context"
  ([Glossary](/docs/concepts/glossary), [Team process](/docs/concepts/team-process)) ·
  docs [REST API](/docs/dev/rest-api), [MCP tools](/docs/dev/mcp-tools) —
  used in both, defined in neither
- **Not** — Composed instructions (the broker's string alone, which never contains the
  team process) · Instruction block (one composed unit; this is the bundle they add up
  to, plus the team-process text that no substring of the prose carries) · Briefing
  (the retired protocol-v1 name for this bundle; no route, client method, type or event
  kind uses the word — it survives only in scripts, test locals and comments) · Context
  watchdog (four modules across three packages describe a component that detects a
  block falling out of an agent's context mid-session and re-sends it —
  `packages/sdk/src/types.ts:506`, `packages/core/src/instructions.ts:51,205`,
  `fixed-context.ts:34-46`, `packages/cli/src/runtime/context-control.ts:6`; no such
  module, frame or `persistent_context` attribute exists in the tree, and the one
  surviving artifact, `instructionCaptureExemptions`, is scoped by its own doc to trace
  redaction)

### Team process

"The team's process, as one authored document. There is at most one per team"
(`packages/sdk/src/types.ts:1818-1822`) — a singleton row whose every write, the first
included, appends an edit record carrying an actor, a reason and a disposition, so
version 1 has a real author instead of appearing to have always existed. It rides in
its own packet field rather than inside the composed instructions because "a member
authors their own `instructions`; this is authored by whoever holds
`team_process.manage`. One string collapses two authorities into one field"
(`packages/core/src/team-process.ts:56-61`). It reaches humans only through the web-UI
editor and agents only through MCP: no `csuite team-process` command exists, and
nothing in the web UI reads the history the editor collects a `reason` for.

- **Names** — SDK `TeamProcess`, packet field `teamProcess`, ceiling
  `TEAM_PROCESS_MAX` (16384, `packages/sdk/src/schemas.ts:980`) · wire kind
  `team_process` · DB `team_process` (singleton, `CHECK (id = 1)`),
  `team_process_edits` (append-only) · route `GET`/`PUT /team-process`,
  `GET /team-process/history` · MCP `team_process_get`, `team_process_write`,
  `team_process_history` · permission `team_process.manage` · UI "Team process" ·
  docs [Team process](/docs/concepts/team-process)
- **Not** — Team context (`team.context`: what the team is here to do, overwritten in
  place with no version and no history) · "document" (the storage shape the rename set
  out to remove, still live as the wire field `GetTeamProcessResponse.document`
  (`packages/sdk/src/types.ts:1874-1877`) and inside the store as
  `assertDocumentInvariants` and the row type `DocumentRow`) · `process_document` /
  `process.manage` (the pre-rename names, alive only as a table migration
  (`LEGACY_TABLES`), a permission alias in `LEGACY_PERMISSION_EXPANSIONS`
  (`packages/sdk/src/types.ts:116`), and unadvertised MCP dispatch aliases marked for
  removal in the next minor)
- **Verbs** — `get` · `write` — one path for create and edit, replacing the text
  wholesale and appending an edit row with a reason and a disposition; it is outside
  the verb table by the conventions rule, not a drifted `update` · `history`

### Amendment disposition

Whether an edit to the team process was retroactive or forward-only. Required on every
write by the request schema, stored on the edit record, and the same field and meaning
as an objective contract amendment, "so 'does work started under the old process finish
under it' has one answer across both" (`packages/sdk/src/types.ts:1863-1870`).

- **Names** — SDK `AmendmentDisposition`, `EditTeamProcessRequest.disposition`,
  `TeamProcessEdit.disposition` · DB `team_process_edits.disposition`
  (`CHECK(disposition IN ('correction','scope_change'))`) · MCP `team_process_write`
  argument `disposition` ·
  UI "scope change — the process itself moved" /
  "correction — the text was wrong about the process"
- **Not** — Message disposition (a runner's terminal acknowledgement of a delivered
  message: `accepted`, `acted`, `handled`, `deferred`, `refused`, carried under
  protocol `disposition-v1` — a different enum under the same field name on the same
  wire, defined elsewhere)
- **Values** — `correction` — retroactive; the prior text was never validly binding ·
  `scope_change` — forward-only; work already underway finishes under the prior text

### Composed instructions hash

sha256 of the canonical composition — the composed prose with the transient
broker/runner version line normalized, then a NUL separator, then the team-process
text. Normalized, not stripped: the line stays in the hashed string with both versions
rendered as the literal `unknown`, so a runner upgrade never reads as an instruction
edit. It is the session's instruction-version identifier: the broker records it as
issued on every `GET /instructions` and compares it against the current composition to
decide restart-pending.

- **Names** — SDK `InstructionsResponse.composedSha256` · core
  `composedInstructionsSha256()` in `packages/core/src/instructions.ts:178` ·
  broker-internal `composedHashFor()` (`packages/core/src/app.ts:819`),
  `allComposedHashes()` (`:831`) · docs [REST API](/docs/dev/rest-api)
- **Not** — `InstructionBlockDescriptor.sha256` (the hash of one block's exact text,
  listed in the packet's `blocks`; there are up to four of those and none of them is
  this) · Instruction block (`InstructionBlockKind` names four operator-authored kinds,
  while this hash also covers the team name, every teammate's roster line and the
  external-notification doctrine section — none of which has a kind or a descriptor)

### Instruction-bearing edit

An edit that moves at least one member's composed instructions hash. What reaches the
canonical composition is wider than the four instruction blocks: the team name, every
teammate's roster line and the set of enabled external-notification endpoints that
reach the member are all in it, so creating a member, deleting one, renaming the team
or enabling an endpoint is instruction-bearing and none of the four fans out an
instructions-edited event. The REST reference defines the term by the fanout instead
(`docs/dev/rest-api.mdx:505,682`) — the code is the narrower authority here and the
code computes the broader set.

- **Names** — docs [REST API](/docs/dev/rest-api), [MCP tools](/docs/dev/mcp-tools) ·
  core `packages/core/src/app.ts:851,5536` · runner
  `packages/cli/src/runtime/runner.ts:179`, `packages/cli/src/runtime/forwarder.ts:164`
  · web UI `packages/web-ui/src/lib/live.ts:158`
- **Not** — Instructions-edited event (the announcement, published from three call
  sites only, so it names a strictly smaller set of edits) · Restart-pending (the state
  an instruction-bearing edit leaves a live session in — the two are the same predicate
  read from opposite ends, which is why the docs collapsed them)

### Restart-pending

A member whose live session was issued a composed instructions hash that differs from
the current composition. It is computed from the hash alone: "Unknown issued (broker
restarted since their fetch) is not pending — unknown is not a claim"
(`packages/core/src/app.ts:837-840`).

- **Names** — SDK `RosterResponse.restartPending` · route `GET /roster` · core
  `restartPendingMembers()` in `packages/core/src/app.ts:841` ·
  runner `RestartReason = 'instructions' | 'environment'` in
  `packages/cli/src/runtime/restart.ts:53` · UI "Restart pending" banner and badge ·
  docs [Glossary](/docs/concepts/glossary), [Members](/docs/concepts/members)
- **Not** — Instructions-edited event (the push that tells a runner to drain and
  restart; a strictly smaller set — `POST /members`, `DELETE /members/:name` and every
  notification-endpoint change move the hash and fan out nothing, so those members are
  listed here and never told) · `RestartReason` `'environment'` (a runner-environment
  change, which restarts an agent without any instruction edit)
- **Decided** — D21: restart-pending means *the composition changed*, over *we told
  them* — the broker derives the list from the hash and never from the fanout
  (2026-09-03)

### Instructions-edited event

The broker-authored notice pushed to exactly those members whose canonical composition
moved as a result of a team edit, a member patch or a team-process write — three call
sites, and `affected` is the diff of the hash map taken across the mutation. The
`changed` list beside it is declared by the call site rather than derived: a name-only
`PATCH /team` moves every member's hash and reports `changed: []`, and a role edit that
touches only the title reports `changed: ['role_description']`.

- **Names** — wire `data.kind: 'instructions'` with `data.event: 'edited'`,
  `data.changed: InstructionBlockKind[]`, `data.actor: string`,
  `data.affected: string[]` · core `publishInstructionsEvent()` in
  `packages/core/src/app.ts:858` · runner `onInstructionsEvent` in
  `packages/cli/src/runtime/forwarder.ts:391` · UI reload in
  `packages/web-ui/src/lib/live.ts:157`
- **Not** — Restart-pending (the roster's hash-derived list, the broader set) ·
  Instruction-bearing edit (the edit itself, which is instruction-bearing whether or not
  this event goes out) · Objective re-brief (the runner's `context_refresh` push, which
  re-asserts the open objective plate and carries no instruction text) · Context control
  (a request to an agent to compact, clear or reload its conversation, defined
  elsewhere)

### Objective re-brief

The runner's own push on session attach — which is also what a restart produces — and
after a compaction, re-asserting the member's open plate to an agent whose window was just
replaced. It carries objectives, not standing
prose, which is why it is not an instruction block and why the word `context` in its wire
kind is a misnomer. *(defined in code only)*

- **Names** — wire `data.kind: 'context_refresh'` · runner
  `packages/cli/src/runtime/objectives-tracker.ts:5-6` ("the runner's `context_refresh`
  re-brief (session attach / compaction)") · SDK `Objective`'s own doc-comment,
  which names the re-brief as one of the two places an outcome is re-asserted
  (`packages/sdk/src/types.ts:1731`)
- **Not** — Context control (a *broker* request that the agent compact, clear or reload;
  a re-brief is the runner's own answer to one having happened) · Instructions-edited
  event (the broker's notice that standing prose moved) · Fixed context (the standing
  bundle, which a re-brief does not resend) · Plate (what a re-brief carries, not the
  push itself) · Agent context (what it is pushed into)
- **Verbs** — emitted by the runner, never requested

## Authentication and credentials

This family covers everything that proves who a caller is, and everything stored
that would let someone impersonate them. The distinction that decides most of it
is **direction**: a bearer token, a web session and an authenticator secret prove a
caller *to* CommandSuite, a tool-source credential proves CommandSuite *to* a third
party, a notification signing secret proves a *sender* to CommandSuite without
resolving a member, and a secret or variable proves nothing at all — it is
environment material injected into an agent. Three unrelated operations are called
"enrollment", and two opposite-facing handshakes are both called "the device flow".

### Credential

Any stored value that proves identity or authorization. It has four subclasses —
bearer token, web session, authenticator secret and tool-source credential — and
the only one the code defines as a type is the last, which is why this entry's own
identifiers are that subclass's (defined under **Tool-source credential**).

- **Names** — SDK `BEARER_TOKEN_PATTERN` / `containsBearerToken`,
  `SetToolCredentialRequest`, `ToolCredentialKind`, `ToolSourceSummary.hasCredential` ·
  core `CredentialShapedBodyError`, `DecryptedCredential` ·
  DB `tool_source_credentials` · CLI `csuite tools cred`, `csuite tools cred-rm` ·
  MCP `tool_sources_set_credential` · UI "Credential" ·
  docs [Tool sources](/docs/concepts/tool-sources)
- **Not** — Secret and Variable (broker-held environment material injected at session
  start; they authenticate nothing, though a secret's *value* is often some third
  party's credential) · Notification signing secret (the `hmac-sha256` or
  `header-secret` material on `PUT /notifications/endpoints/:slug/secret` and
  `/notifications/profiles/:slug/secret`; it verifies an inbound sender at
  `/hooks/:slug` and resolves no member) · Model credential (the API key the agent
  binary needs to reach its model provider — `csuite stub` exists to prove the
  runner lifecycle "with no model credential"; CommandSuite never holds or issues
  one) · KEK (the key that wraps credentials at rest; it proves no identity) ·
  Tool source (the registered provider; the credential is a write-only subresource
  of it) · `credentialMode` on `POST /members`, which is about one subclass only —
  it selects whether creating a member mints a bearer token (`bootstrap`) or waits
  for a device enrollment (`pending`)
- **Values** — `ToolCredentialKind`: `bearer` — sent as `Authorization: Bearer` ·
  `header` — sent as a named header, with `headerName` required
- **Verbs** — set, delete. Read paths report only that a credential exists —
  `hasCredential`, `TokenInfo` — never what it is; plaintext appears exactly once,
  at issuance.

### Auth plane

Which of three request-authentication mechanisms resolved the caller. All three
attach the same `LoadedMember`, so downstream handlers "don't care which plane
authenticated the request — the identity surface is the member". The plane is not
itself a wire value: it is observable only as the `sessionId` / `tokenId` bindings
the middleware sets. *(defined in code only)*

- **Names** — core "Tri-auth middleware" `createAuthMiddleware`, `AuthDependencies`,
  `AuthBindings`, `LoadedMember`, `AuthBindings.Variables.sessionId` / `.tokenId` ·
  docs [REST API](/docs/dev/rest-api) "Three planes, all resolving to the same
  loaded member"
- **Not** — Permission leaf (what a member may do once a plane has resolved them;
  the plane never widens or narrows authority, including the JWT `role` claim) ·
  `dual`, the per-endpoint auth label in the REST reference, which predates the JWT
  plane and names only two of the three — it is stale, not a fourth plane. The test
  block `describe('dual auth (bearer OR cookie)')` is *not* stale: it covers exactly
  the two planes it names.
- **Values** — bearer — an opaque `csuite_` token · session — the `csuite_session`
  cookie · JWT — a federated RS256 token. The two bearer-shaped planes are separated
  by shape, not by header, and only when an issuer is configured: the JWT branch runs
  on `jwt && looksLikeJwt(raw)`, so with no verifier wired a JWT falls through to the
  opaque lookup and 401s as `unknown token`. Once the branch is taken, a structurally
  valid JWT that fails verification is a 401 rather than a fallthrough. Authority is
  identical across planes; ancillary behavior is not — the session-online notice and
  the offline-queue flush fire only for opaque-bearer callers (`tokenId !== null`).

### Bearer token

An opaque machine credential — `csuite_` followed by 43 base64url characters — held
as one row in `tokens`. "The plaintext token is NEVER exposed by this shape — it's
returned exactly once at issuance"; the row stores `sha256:<hex>`, so a lost token
is replaced, never recovered.

- **Names** — SDK `TokenInfo`, `TokenOrigin`, `ListTokensResponse`,
  `RotateTokenResponse`, `RevokeTokenResponse` · core `TokenStore`,
  `generateBearerToken`, `TOKEN_HASH_PREFIX`, `InternalTokenRow` ·
  DB `tokens.hash` · route `POST /members/:name/rotate-token`,
  `GET /members/:name/tokens`, `DELETE /members/:name/tokens/:id` ·
  CLI `csuite rotate`, `csuite roster --reveal-token` (a compatibility alias over
  `csuite rotate`; it refuses unless given `--all --token-file` and prints no
  plaintext) · UI "Bearer tokens" · docs [Members](/docs/concepts/members)
- **Not** — Federated JWT (arrives in the same `Authorization: Bearer` header, but is
  verified against a pinned JWKS instead of being looked up in `tokens`) · Web session
  (the cookie plane; a session row is not a token row and cannot be listed or revoked
  through these routes) · Tool-source credential (material the broker presents
  outward) · Secret (environment material; never an authenticator for the broker) ·
  `InternalTokenRow` (the same shape plus the on-disk `hash`, which never reaches
  the wire)
- **Values** — `origin`: `bootstrap` — issued outside the token lifecycle: carried
  across the first-boot config-file → SQLite migration, written explicitly by the
  first-run wizard, and written explicitly (with label `initial`) by `POST /members`
  under `credentialMode: 'bootstrap'`; it is also the column default and the fallback
  for a corrupt row · `rotate` — minted by `POST /members/:name/rotate-token` ·
  `enroll` — minted by an approved device enrollment. The web UI relabels these
  "config-file", "rotated" and "device-code" while the revoke dialog prints the raw
  value, so one row shows two vocabularies; the wire value is the canonical one.
- **Verbs** — list, rotate, revoke. There is no create route. A token is minted as a
  side effect of something else: a rotation, an approved device enrollment, or
  creating a member with `credentialMode: 'bootstrap'` — which is what the first-run
  wizard does at seed time.

### Web session

A cookie-backed human login. "A session is a server-issued capability: after TOTP
verification, the caller mints a session row binding a random `id` to a member
name." Sliding seven-day TTL; expired rows read as nonexistent.

- **Names** — SDK `SessionResponse`, `TotpLoginRequest` · core `SESSION_COOKIE_NAME`
  (`csuite_session`), `SESSION_TTL_MS`, `SessionRow`, `SessionStore` · DB `sessions` ·
  route `POST /session/totp`, `GET /session`, `POST /session/logout` ·
  docs [REST API](/docs/dev/rest-api)
- **Not** — Runner session (one agent process run, bracketed by `session_start` and
  `session_end` activity events) · Runner generation (one process incarnation inside
  a runner session, after a cold respawn) · Live subscription (what the roster counts
  in `Presence.connected` when it reports a member live in several places at once) ·
  Agent session id (`ActivitySessionStart.agentSessionId`, the id the underlying agent
  CLI assigns its own conversation). No two of these share a store, an id space or a
  lifetime with this one, or with each other.
- **Verbs** — log in (`POST /session/totp`), read (`GET /session`), log out

### Authenticator secret

A member's TOTP secret: twenty random bytes rendered base32, held on the member row
and wrapped `enc-v1:` when a KEK is active. Holding one is also what makes a member
render as `kind: 'person'` in the roster — a stored fact about the member, not a
property of how any caller authenticated.

- **Names** — SDK `EnrollTotpResponse { totpSecret, totpUri }` · core `generateSecret`,
  `otpauthUri`, `MemberStore.setTotpSecret` · DB `members.totp_secret` ·
  route `POST /members/:name/enroll-totp` · CLI `csuite enroll --member <name>` ·
  UI "Enter your authenticator code", "TOTP secret", "otpauth URI" ·
  docs [Members](/docs/concepts/members) "Web sign-in"
- **Not** — Device enrollment (joins a machine to the team and mints a bearer token;
  it shares the word "enroll" and nothing else, which is why the CLI's own help text
  has to disclaim the collision) · Bearer token (what a machine presents; an
  authenticator secret is never presented — it generates the six-digit code that
  mints a web session) · Member `kind` (the roster projection, whose doc comment says
  it is derived from the auth plane and which in fact reads this stored field, for
  every member, with no request context)
- **Verbs** — set, clear. Setting is destructive: the route generates a fresh secret
  on every call, so re-running it rotates and invalidates every authenticator app
  previously bound to that member.
- **Decided** — D15: rename the act to **authenticator setup**, `csuite totp set`,
  keeping `csuite enroll` as a warned alias for one release and `enroll-totp` /
  `EnrollTotpResponse` behind a deprecation window — over leaving the verb alone and
  adding a disambiguating sentence to the glossary, which leaves the highest-severity
  homonym in this family permanently in the CLI's own help text (2026-09-03)

### Device enrollment

The RFC 8628 device-authorization handshake that joins a new machine to the team:
the device posts `/enroll` and polls, a `members.manage` holder approves in the web
UI or the CLI, and the bearer token travels straight to the polling device — never
into the approver's browser. One in-flight handshake is a **pending enrollment**, one
row in `pending_enrollments`, single-use: "a replay always returns `expired_token`".

- **Names** — SDK `DeviceAuthorizationRequest` / `Response`, `DeviceTokenRequest` /
  `Response`, `DeviceTokenErrorCode`, `PendingEnrollment`,
  `ListPendingEnrollmentsResponse`, `ApproveEnrollmentRequest`,
  `RejectEnrollmentRequest` · core `EnrollmentStore`, `EnrollmentRow`,
  `EnrollmentStatus`, `LookupOutcome`, `PollOutcome` · DB `pending_enrollments` ·
  route `POST /enroll`, `/enroll/poll`, `/enroll/pending`, `/enroll/approve`,
  `/enroll/reject` · CLI `csuite connect`, `csuite connect pending`,
  `csuite connect approve` · MCP `connect_pending`, `connect_approve` ·
  UI "Pending enrollments" · docs [Device enrollment](/docs/enrollment)
- **Not** — Authenticator setup (`csuite enroll`, the TOTP secret; renamed under
  D15) · Saved auth entry (what the delivered token becomes on the device; `csuite
  auth list` calls those rows "enrolled", which is the third unrelated use of the
  word) · Platform registration (the other device flow, pointing the opposite way) ·
  `TokenOrigin: 'enroll'` (a token's provenance, not a row in this table) ·
  `enrolment`, the UK spelling that survives in the MCP tool descriptions, in two
  web-UI strings (the create banner and the unsupported-broker error), in a thrown
  core error, and across `docs/`, `compose.yaml` and `docker/entrypoint.sh` — the
  same concept, off house style under D34
- **Values** — three vocabularies describe one row, and they are not synonyms.
  Stored: `EnrollmentStatus` = `pending` `approved` `rejected`. Approver path:
  `LookupOutcome.kind` = `pending` `expired` `already_approved` `already_rejected`
  `not_found`. Device path: `PollOutcome.kind` = `authorization_pending` `slow_down`
  `expired_token` `access_denied` `approved`, re-declared on the wire as
  `DeviceTokenErrorCode` (the first four). Only `EnrollmentStatus` is stored; the
  other two add the derived facts *expired* (`expiresAt < now`) and *absent* (row
  deleted or never written), plus `slow_down`, which is a rate signal and not a row
  state at all. Two asymmetries are load-bearing and survive any renaming: the device
  path collapses *absent* into `expired_token` so a prober cannot tell "never existed"
  from "expired", and `PollOutcome` is RFC 8628's wire vocabulary, so it cannot be
  unified with the other two.
- **Verbs** — create (device), poll (device), list pending, approve, reject

### User code

The short, human-typeable half of a device enrollment: eight Crockford base32
characters displayed `XXXX-XXXX`. A person reads it off the enrolling machine and
types it into the web UI; it identifies a pending row and is not itself a secret.

- **Names** — SDK `DeviceAuthorizationResponse.userCode`, `PendingEnrollment.userCode`,
  `ApproveEnrollmentRequest.userCode`, `RejectEnrollmentRequest.userCode` ·
  core `formatUserCode`, `normalizeUserCode` · DB `pending_enrollments.user_code` ·
  route `/enroll?code=` (the SPA deep link, `enrollVerify`) ·
  CLI `csuite connect approve --code <XXXX-XXXX>` ·
  docs [Device enrollment](/docs/enrollment)
- **Not** — Device code (`csuite-dc_…`, the long secret half the CLI polls with; the
  broker stores only its sha256 and never displays it) · the authenticator code (the
  six digits the login form asks for, `TotpLoginRequest.code`) · the platform
  registration `code` (a single-use handle on `POST /platform-connect/bind`) ·
  `RunnerConditionCode` (a runner fault code, not a secret at all)

### Saved auth entry

A `(broker URL, workspace, bearer token)` triple in the client-side auth store,
written by `csuite connect` and read on every CLI invocation. "Resolution matches the
URL exactly, then picks the entry whose `workspace` is the longest path-prefix of the
cwd, then falls back to the unscoped entry."

- **Names** — CLI `AuthConfigEntry`, `authStorePath`, `findAuthEntry`,
  `listAuthEntries`, `migrateLegacyStore` · CLI `csuite auth list`,
  `csuite auth migrate` · file `~/.config/csuite/auth.json`
  (`$XDG_CONFIG_HOME/csuite/auth.json`) ·
  docs [CLI reference](/docs/reference/cli)
- **Not** — Device enrollment (the broker-side handshake that produced the token; a
  saved entry is a client-side file row and holds no enrollment state) ·
  `--auth <kind>` on `csuite notifications` (an inbound webhook signature scheme,
  `hmac-sha256` or `header-secret` — unrelated to signing in) · the legacy
  project-scoped `<dir>/.csuite/auth.json`, still read and folded in by
  `csuite auth migrate`
- **Verbs** — list, migrate. The store is user-global rather than in-tree on purpose:
  "a csuite bearer is not a narrow credential: `GET /secrets/resolve` on it returns
  every third-party secret bound to that member." Tokens are never printed.

### Auth blocked

The state a running agent enters when the broker rejects its saved bearer with a 401:
the runner stops uploading, queues what it captures, and re-reads the auth store on a
timer until a different token succeeds. The roster surfaces it as a count of live
subscriptions whose credential has been revoked, so an operator can see a revoked
runner still attached and still trying, and it is the one input that makes the broker
classify a runner `degraded` with reason `auth_blocked` without that runner having said
anything about itself. *(defined in code only)*

- **Names** — SDK `Presence.authBlocked`, `ActivityAuthState` (`kind: 'auth_state'`),
  `RUNNER_CONDITION_CODES` / `RunnerConditionCode` value `auth_blocked` ·
  CLI `createAuthRecoveryController`, `AuthRecoveryController`,
  `resolveReplacementToken` · MCP roster output `auth-blocked=<n>` ·
  UI "auth blocked:"
- **Not** — `RunnerConditionCode: 'no_credential'` ("runner credential is
  unavailable" — the *model* credential the agent binary needs, which CommandSuite
  never issues; `auth_blocked` is "runner credential is revoked", the csuite bearer) ·
  `Presence.connected` (live subscriptions of any kind; an auth-blocked subscription
  is still connected) · Work state `needs_input` (an agent waiting on a person, not on a
  credential; the wire still spells that value `blocked`) · Objective status `blocked` ·
  Runner condition (one subscription's own statement; this is the one condition the
  broker synthesizes on the runner's behalf)
- **Values** — `ActivityAuthState.state`: `blocked` — the first 401 on any outbound
  transport · `recovered` — a replacement token from the auth store authenticated.
  `status` is always `401`.

### Federated JWT

An RS256 token minted by an issuer the operator pinned in config and verified against
that issuer's JWKS. "csuite is issuer-agnostic … Scope: verification only. Minting
tokens is the issuer's job; the csuite server never holds a signing key."

- **Names** — core `JwtConfig { issuer, audience, jwksUrl }`, `JwtVerifier`,
  `VerifiedClaims`, `JwtClaimError`, `looksLikeJwt` ·
  docs [REST API](/docs/dev/rest-api)
- **Not** — Bearer token (the opaque credential sharing the same header; a JWT is
  three dot-separated base64url segments and an opaque token contains no dots) ·
  Platform registration (the flow that writes the `issuer` / `jwksUrl` / `audience`
  block; the JWT is what an issuer mints afterward)
- **Values** — claims: `iss` and `aud` must match config · `member` names a roster
  entry by name, and an unknown name is a hard 401 because "memberships are managed
  via the invite flow, never by JWT side channels" · `sub` is logged for audit and has
  no local meaning · `role` is informational and grants nothing — authorization runs
  off the resolved member's permission leaves

### KEK

The 32-byte key-encryption key the broker uses to wrap fields at rest. It is resolved
from `CSUITE_KEK` or from `<configDir>/csuite-kek.bin` (mode 0600, minted on first
resolve) and installed process-wide by a boot gate that exits rather than start
without it. Expand the acronym on first use on any operator-facing surface: the
repository expands it three times — in `apps/server/src/kek.ts`,
`packages/core/src/enrollments.ts` and `.env.example` — and never in the config
reference, the environment-variable table or the troubleshooting page, which is where
an operator meets it.

- **Names** — server `resolveKek`, `installKekOrExit`, `KekResolutionError`, `setKek`,
  `getKek` · core `type Kek = Buffer` · env `CSUITE_KEK` · file `csuite-kek.bin` ·
  docs [Config files](/docs/reference/config),
  [Environment variables](/docs/reference/env-vars)
- **Not** — Secret (a broker-held record injected into an agent's environment; the KEK
  wraps secret values and is never itself a secret record — it has no slug, no binding
  and no delivery) · Tool-source credential (wrapped by the KEK, not a key) · Variable
  value (plaintext by design and never wrapped, so a broker booted without one still
  delivers git identity) · Field cipher (the envelope format and the seam; the KEK is
  the key material handed to it)
- **Values** — `no_kek` is a 503 and not a 400: a write that needs the key fails closed
  rather than storing plaintext
- **Verbs** — resolve, install. Losing it does not lose the team: bearer tokens are
  stored as hashes and survive, while stored secret values and authenticator secrets
  become undecryptable.

### Field cipher

The seam a store receives from its host to read and write `enc-v1:` values, and the
envelope format itself — `enc-v1:<b64u(iv)>:<b64u(tag)>:<b64u(ct)>`, AES-256-GCM with
a 12-byte IV and a 16-byte tag. Two byte-compatible implementations exist:
synchronous over Node crypto in the server, asynchronous over Web Crypto in the
broker. They are held compatible by intent, not by a shared import — `ENCRYPTED_FIELD_PREFIX`
is declared in both packages, and D35 makes `csuite-sdk` the single home for wire
constants, so the duplication is scheduled to collapse rather than a pattern to copy.
*(defined in code only)*

- **Names** — core `FieldCipher`, `GetFieldCipher`, `ENCRYPTED_FIELD_PREFIX`
  (`enc-v1:`), `encryptFieldPortable` / `decryptFieldPortable`, `EncryptedFieldError` ·
  server `encryptField` / `decryptField`, `kekFieldCipher` ·
  conformance `fieldCipherConformance` in `csuite-core/conformance`
- **Not** — KEK (the key; the field cipher is the envelope and the seam that applies
  it) · Secret (a record whose value happens to be wrapped this way) · Variable
  (stored in plaintext deliberately, and never handed to this seam for its value —
  the cipher appears in `variables.ts` only on the migration path out of secrets)
- **Values** — two postures, and stores do not share one. **KEK-required**: the write
  is refused with `no_kek` — secrets, tool-source credentials, notification signing
  secrets. **KEK-optional**: the value is written as plaintext when no key is active —
  authenticator secrets, the issued enrollment token, the VAPID private key. "Fail
  closed" names only the first posture; the boot gate is what keeps the second
  unreachable in production. Whether the second posture stays is D16, a security
  question the vocabulary records rather than settles.

### Platform registration

The outbound device flow that binds a self-hosted broker to a hosted control plane.
The broker is the *client* here: it registers itself, prints a verification URL and
user code for a platform admin, polls until authorized, and writes the returned
issuer, JWKS URL and audience to `<config>.platform.json` — which is what turns the
federated JWT plane on.

- **Names** — binary `csuite-connect-platform` · route `GET /setup/connect-platform`,
  `POST /platform-connect/bind`, `GET /platform-connect/lookup` ·
  file `<config>.platform.json` ·
  docs [Connect a self-hosted server to a platform](/docs/self-hosted-connect)
- **Not** — Device enrollment (the inbound device flow, in which this broker is the
  identity provider approving a machine; both mint a `deviceCode` / `userCode` pair,
  their field names and statuses diverge, and no file references the other) ·
  `csuite connect` (the CLI verb for the inbound flow) · `Presence.connected` (the
  count of live subscriptions for a member, not a registration)
- **Values** — `status`: `pending` `expired` `authorized` — the hosted platform's own
  vocabulary, read by the broker while it polls, unrelated to `EnrollmentStatus`

## Provisioned environment

An admin registers a named value on the broker once; the runner resolves the set that
reaches its member and injects each one as an environment variable on the agent child at
spawn. Two things fragment the vocabulary here. The first is **kind**: a secret's value is
write-only and scrubbed from captured traces, a variable's is readable and recorded
verbatim, and the server holds that difference as two tables rather than a column. The
second is the word **environment**, which names the map the runner injects, a row in one of
the two registries, and — in a reader's head, wrongly — an OS process env var that nothing
here provisions.

### Runner environment

The merged, per-member `{envName → value}` map a runner requests from the broker
immediately before spawning its agent child: *"Two stores write into one environment map"*
(`packages/core/src/env-namespace.ts:4`). One permission gates all of it — `secrets.manage`,
whose name says secrets and whose scope is both registries and the reading of variable
values.

- **Names** — SDK `EnvironmentEvent`, `EnvironmentEventSchema` · core `env-namespace.ts`,
  `ensureEnvNamespaceSchema`, `publishEnvironmentEvent`, event `data.kind: 'environment'` ·
  CLI `--no-env-reload`, restart reason `'environment'` · UI "Environment" nav item,
  `EnvironmentPanel`, `/environment` · docs [Variables](/docs/concepts/variables)
  "One environment, two stores"
- **Not** — environment variable (an OS process env var an operator sets on the broker or
  the runner host, such as `CSUITE_KEK` or `CSUITE_TOKEN`; the broker never delivers those) ·
  runner-managed variables (the `CSUITE_`/`OTEL_`/`CLAUDE_CODE_`/`CODEX_` names the runner
  layers on after the broker's map — both adapters merge broker values first, so the runner
  wins any collision) · Resolved runner environment (the broker-provisioned half of this map,
  and only that half) · Environment entry (one row, not the map)
- **Values** — `EnvironmentEvent.action`: `value_set` `bound` `unbound` — the only three
  changes that reach a running member. A `PATCH`, a `DELETE /:slug` and a
  `DELETE /:slug/value` emit no event and take effect at the next runner start.
- **Verbs** — resolve, refresh (a live change lands at the member's next idle boundary; at
  the next runner start for a runner started with `--no-env-reload` or a broker older than
  `EnvironmentEvent`)
- **Decided** — D18: adopt "runner environment" (the map) and "environment entry" (a row) as
  the superordinate, over staying two-entity like the server, which would leave the nav
  label, the `/environment` route prefix, `EnvironmentEvent`, `--no-env-reload` and half the
  concept prose with no name (2026-09-03)

### Environment entry

One row in one of the two registries: an immutable slug, a target `envName`, a description,
`enabled`, `allMembers`, a creator and timestamps, at most one stored value, and zero or
more delivery bindings. The metadata is byte-identical between the kinds; `kind` decides
whether the value can be read back and whether it is scrubbed from traces.
*(defined in code only)*

- **Names** — UI `EnvKind`, `EnvEntry`, `EnvOps` (`packages/web-ui/src/components/env/shared.tsx`),
  form "New environment entry" with a "Kind" choice of "Secret" and "Variable — not a secret"
- **Not** — Secret, Variable (the two kinds; the server has no supertype over them, and
  `env-namespace.ts:10-18` states why: on one table the read-path filter is a conditional
  every caller must remember, on two it is held by the type) · filesystem entry (a path in
  the shared filesystem, a different family)
- **Values** — `kind`: `secret` `variable` — the create form has no default, deliberately
- **Verbs** — create, update, delete, set-value, delete-value, bind, unbind

### Secret

> *"A secret is a named value an admin drops on the broker once; the runner resolves the set
> bound to its member immediately before spawning the agent and injects each as an
> environment variable on the agent child. The value never appears in composed instructions,
> prompts, or MCP traffic — the agent just finds the variable set."*
> — `packages/core/src/secrets.ts:5-9`

Its value is write-only over the wire, KEK-encrypted at rest, and registered with the trace
redactor.

- **Names** — SDK `Secret`, `SecretSummary`, `SetSecretValueRequest` · DB `secrets`,
  `secret_values.value_enc`, `secret_bindings` · route `GET /secrets`, `PUT /secrets/:slug/value` ·
  CLI `csuite secrets list|view|add|update|set-value|delete-value|bind|unbind|rm` ·
  MCP `secrets_list`, `secrets_view`, `secrets_create`, `secrets_update`, `secrets_delete`,
  `secrets_set_value`, `secrets_delete_value`, `secrets_bindings` · UI "Secrets" section,
  tag "contained" · docs [Secrets](/docs/concepts/secrets)
- **Not** — Variable (the same record with a readable value that is never redacted) ·
  tool-source credential (auth material for one registered tool source, in
  `tool_source_credentials`, gated on `tools.manage`, read only by the broker's invoke path) ·
  signing secret (the HMAC or header secret that verifies an inbound `POST /hooks/:slug`,
  set with `csuite notifications set-secret` and projected as `hasSecret`; the broker reads
  it back in cleartext, which a secret's value never is) · TOTP secret (`totpSecret`, a
  member's second-factor seed) · bearer token (a proof the broker accepts, not a value it
  hands out)
- **Values** — `hasValue`: whether a value is stored. No endpoint returns the value. Two
  store methods decrypt one: `resolveFor`, per member, for the runner's own resolve; and
  `allDecryptedValues`, which hands every decryptable value to the in-process trace redactor
  at boot and is marked *"NOT an API surface; values never leave the process"*
  (`packages/core/src/secrets.ts:156-164`).
- **Verbs** — list, get, create, update, delete, set-value, delete-value, bind, unbind

### Variable

> *"A variable is everything a secret is … except for the two things that matter: 1. Its
> value is READABLE by a caller holding `secrets.manage` … 2. It is NEVER registered with the
> core trace redactor, so it appears verbatim in captured traces."*
> — `packages/core/src/variables.ts:5-13`

Stored in plaintext on purpose: *"a git author name appears in every commit the member has
ever written. Encrypting it would buy nothing and would make identity depend on an active
KEK"* (`packages/core/src/env-namespace.ts:89-94`).

- **Names** — SDK `Variable`, `VariableSummary` (carries `value` for a `secrets.manage`
  holder) · DB `variables`, `variable_values.value`, `variable_bindings` · route
  `GET /variables`, `PUT /variables/:slug/value` · CLI `csuite variables …` (the undocumented
  alias `vars` is accepted by the dispatcher and absent from `--help`; `csuite variables`
  itself is listed) · MCP `variables_list`, `variables_view`, `variables_create`,
  `variables_update`, `variables_delete`, `variables_set_value`, `variables_delete_value`,
  `variables_bindings` · UI "Variables" section, tag "recorded", kind card
  "Variable — not a secret" · docs [Variables](/docs/concepts/variables)
- **Not** — Secret (write-only, redacted) · environment variable (the OS process env var an
  operator exports; a variable is a broker record that *targets* one) · `envName` (the name
  the value lands under, not the record) · a laxer slug grammar (`variables_create` tells
  agents "max 64" and `validateVariableSlug` allows 64; the route parses with the shared
  32-character schema first, so the wire rule is 32 for both stores and the store's extra
  latitude is unreachable over HTTP — see Slug) · `VariablesError` (not a class —
  `variables.ts:56-61` re-exports `SecretsError` under that name, so a variables 404 arrives
  with `name: 'SecretsError'`, deliberately, so one mapper covers both route families)
- **Values** — `hasValue` and `value` are independent: `hasValue` says a value is stored,
  `value` is present only for a caller holding `secrets.manage`
- **Verbs** — list, get, create, update, delete, set-value, delete-value, bind, unbind

### envName

The uppercase POSIX environment variable name the runner sets on the agent child —
*"Target environment variable name (validated, reserved names rejected)"*
(`packages/sdk/src/types.ts:940-941`). It is the entry's *destination*; the slug is its
address.

- **Names** — SDK `SecretEnvNameSchema`, `RESERVED_ENV_NAMES`, `RESERVED_ENV_PREFIXES`,
  `isReservedEnvName` · core `validateEnvName` · DB `secrets.env_name`, `variables.env_name` ·
  CLI `--env <ENV_NAME>` · MCP `envName` · UI "Env var name" · docs
  [Secrets](/docs/concepts/secrets) "Reserved names"
- **Not** — slug (the immutable address of an entry inside its store; unique per store and
  never across the pair, so a secret and a variable may both be `git-token`) · Variable (the
  record; an `envName` is a field on it) · environment namespace (the space these names
  compete in)
- **Values** — `/^[A-Z][A-Z0-9_]*$/`, 1–128 characters, checked case-insensitively against
  six reserved prefixes — `CSUITE_` `OTEL_` `CLAUDE_CODE_` `CODEX_` `LD_` `DYLD_` — and
  twenty-two reserved names: `PATH` `HOME` `SHELL` `TERM` `TMPDIR` `USER` `LOGNAME` `IFS`
  `ENV` `BASH_ENV` `PS4` `PROMPT_COMMAND` `NODE_OPTIONS` `NODE_PATH` `NODE_EXTRA_CA_CERTS`
  `PYTHONPATH` `PYTHONSTARTUP` `PERL5LIB` `PERL5OPT` `RUBYOPT` `GIT_ASKPASS` `SSH_ASKPASS`.
  Reserved names are runner-managed or interpreter/loader controls: allowing them would let a
  `secrets.manage` holder break trace capture or gain code execution on every runner machine.
  Validated four times — zod at the wire, `validateEnvName` in the store,
  `SecretEnvNameSchema` in the browser form, and again in the runner before injection.

### Environment namespace

The single per-member space of `envName`s that both registries write into. Env names are
**not** globally unique — that index was dropped on 2026-07-30 so `cora-github-token` and
`rune-github-token` can both target `GITHUB_TOKEN` — and the invariant that replaced it is
per-member: *"a member's resolved env map can never carry two values for one variable"*
(`packages/core/src/secrets.ts:25-33`).

- **Names** — core `ensureEnvNamespaceSchema`, `findEnvRivalForMember`, `findEnvRivalAnyone`,
  `envRivalMessage`, `EnvRival` · error code `env_taken` → HTTP 409 · message
  `"{member} already resolves {ENV} from {kind} \"{slug}\""` · docs
  [Variables](/docs/concepts/variables) "One environment, two stores"
- **Not** — reserved env name (a static blocklist checked on the value of `envName` itself,
  rejected with 400 and never with 409) · `slug_taken` (a collision of addresses inside one
  store, not of destinations across both) · precedence (there is none: *"A collision is an
  ERROR, never a precedence rule"* — `env-namespace.ts:27-30`; `GET /secrets/resolve` throws
  rather than pick a winner)
- **Values** — checked at `bind()` on both stores, at `create()`/`update()` for all-members
  rows, and again at resolve time. The rival queries select on `env_name`, `all_members` and
  the bindings table only — they never filter `enabled`, so a disabled entry still blocks a
  binding. Conservative, and undocumented.
- **Verbs** — the rule is enforced, never queried; "rival" is an implementation word and is
  not product vocabulary

### Member binding

An administrator-issued, individually revocable row `(<resource>_id, member_name,
created_at)` that makes one resource reach one named member. One relation with three
subjects — secrets, variables and tool sources share the DDL, the method quartet, the
predicate and the route shape. Reach is `enabled && (allMembers || bound)`, stated once
for all three at `packages/sdk/src/types.ts:935-936`; for a tool source that same
predicate is also the invoke check, with no `tools.manage` bypass, so an administrator
binds itself like anyone else.

- **Names** — SDK `BindSecretRequest`, `BindVariableRequest`, `BindToolSourceRequest`,
  `bound`, `boundMembers`, `allMembers`, `ToolSourceSummary.bound`,
  `GetToolSourceResponse.boundMembers` · DB `secret_bindings`, `variable_bindings`,
  `tool_source_bindings` · route `POST /secrets/:slug/bindings`,
  `DELETE /secrets/:slug/bindings/:name`, and the `/variables` and `/tool-sources`
  mirrors · core `bind`, `unbind`, `isBound`, `listBindings` · CLI `csuite secrets bind`,
  `csuite variables unbind`, `csuite tools bind` · MCP `secrets_bindings`,
  `variables_bindings`, `tool_sources_bindings` — D42 renames all three to
  `<noun>_bind` / `<noun>_unbind`: `secrets_bind`/`secrets_unbind`,
  `variables_bind`/`variables_unbind`, `tool_sources_bind`/`tool_sources_unbind`, because
  a bare plural noun gives a model scanning `tools/list` no signal that the tool writes · UI "Access" section, "Bind
  member", "Deliver to all members (including future ones)" · docs
  [Secrets](/docs/concepts/secrets), [Tool sources](/docs/concepts/tool-sources)
- **Not** — Request template (the method, URL, headers and body the broker executes for a
  custom tool: `CustomToolBinding`, `custom_tools.binding_json`, `validateBinding` — the
  same word, in the same subsystem, for a completely different thing) · `tools.manage`
  (the leaf that administers the registry — create, update, delete, define tools, set
  credentials, bind members; it confers no invoke) · fs grant (the automatic, path-keyed
  read grant in `fs_grants`, created as a side effect of an attachment reference,
  carrying provenance and having no per-viewer revoke) · device attachment (the
  enrollment flow's `bind` approve mode, attaching an enrolling device to a member) ·
  grant (the verb for handing a member a permission leaf; `secrets_bindings` and
  `tool_sources_bindings` both open their agent-facing descriptions with *"Grant or
  revoke members' access"* while `variables_bindings` says "bindings" — two strays, and
  the SDK verb is `bind`)
- **Values** — `allMembers`: `true` is the wildcard form of the same relation, and
  reaches future members too
- **Verbs** — bind, unbind (SDK `bindSecret`/`unbindSecret`; the MCP tools take `add` and
  `remove` arrays)
- **Decided** — D10: "binding" survives for the member↔resource relation across all three
  subjects, verb *bind*/*unbind*, over reserving the word for the custom-tool HTTP shape
  and renaming this relation "grant" — `bind` is in the SDK client's arbitrating verb
  grammar, it dominates the DDL, the stores, the routes, the CLI and the MCP names, and
  "grant" is already the verb for permission leaves and the noun for `fs_grants`
  (2026-09-03)

### Resolved runner environment

The broker-provisioned half of a member's runner environment: the merged `{envName → value}`
delta returned by `GET /secrets/resolve`, computed from every enabled secret **and** every
enabled variable that reaches the calling member. The path names one store and returns both.
*(defined in code only)*

- **Names** — route `GET /secrets/resolve` (`PATHS.secretsResolve`) · SDK
  `ResolveSecretsResponse` with `env` and `secretEnvNames`, client `resolveSecrets()` · core
  `SecretsStore.resolveFor`, `VariablesStore.resolveFor` · CLI runtime `RunnerHandle.secretsEnv`,
  `refreshSecrets()`, flag `--no-secrets` · broker log `secrets resolved` · docs
  [REST API](/docs/dev/rest-api)
- **Not** — Secret (the endpoint, the response type, the client method, the runner field and
  the flag all say "secrets" and all act on both stores; `/secrets/resolve` is a legacy path
  name, kept because `secretEnvNames` already encodes a version-skew contract) · runner
  environment (this delta plus the runner-managed variables layered on top, which always win)
- **Values** — `secretEnvNames`: which keys of `env` came from the secrets store, and so the
  only ones the runner registers with the trace redactor. Absent from a broker that predates
  the field, and the runner then registers everything — the fail-closed direction.
- **Verbs** — resolve, refresh
- **Decided** — D17: rename the flag to `--no-broker-env`, keeping `--no-secrets` as a
  deprecated alias, over keeping the name and fixing only `docs/runners/claude.mdx` and
  `docs/runners/codex.mdx`, and over splitting it into `--no-secrets` and `--no-variables`.
  The flag short-circuits the whole resolve, so today an operator who passes it to debug a
  token also silently loses git identity; only the rename says so at the point of use
  (2026-09-03)

### Registered secret value

A literal string the broker or the runner has loaded into its in-process redaction set:
every stored secret value of six characters or more that decrypted successfully at boot, plus
every value written since, for the life of that process. It is what makes the write APIs
attach a warning when an instruction block or a role description contains one.
*(defined in code only)*

- **Names** — core `registerSecretValues`, `containsRegisteredSecretValue`, `REDACTED`, and
  the module-private `MIN_REGISTERED_VALUE_LENGTH` and `registeredValues`
  (`packages/core/src/trace/redact.ts`) · wire `warning` field and the `X-CSuite-Warning`
  header on `PATCH /members/:name` and `PATCH /team` · UI "scrubbed from captured traces" ·
  docs [REST API](/docs/dev/rest-api) (which uses the term twice without defining it)
- **Not** — `SECRET_PATTERNS` (module-private: five hardcoded regexes for well-known
  credential shapes, plus a six-name header strip-list, that run on every captured string with
  no registry at all) · Variable (never registered, by design — registering identity is what
  made members' own names vanish from their own traces) · the secrets registry (a row can
  exist with no registered value, and a registered value can outlive its row)
- **Values** — three silent edges, all fail-safe in one direction and lossy in the other: a
  value under six characters is never registered and never warned about; the set is never
  pruned outside tests, so deleting a secret keeps its old value scrubbed until the process
  restarts; and boot registration is best-effort, so after a KEK mismatch some live secrets
  are absent.
- **Verbs** — register (there is no unregister outside tests)

### Identity migration

The boot-time, one-way move of the four git identity env names out of the secrets store and
into variables, so a member's own name stops being redacted from the member's own traces.
Automatic and not operator-driven, all-or-nothing per row inside one transaction, and
idempotent.

- **Names** — core `IDENTITY_ENV_NAMES`, `migrateIdentityToVariables`,
  `IdentityMigrationResult` · boot logs `identity migrated from secrets to variables`,
  `identity rows left in the secrets store`, `identity migration rolled back; identity remains
  registered for redaction` · docs [Variables](/docs/concepts/variables) "Git identity
  migrates automatically"
- **Not** — a convert action (there is none, in any surface: a secret's value is write-only,
  so nothing can read it back to carry it across; moving one to a variable means deleting it
  and registering a new one with the original value to hand) · a heuristic (a fixed list of
  four names, because *"a wrong guess in the permissive direction publishes a secret"*)
- **Values** — `GIT_AUTHOR_NAME` `GIT_AUTHOR_EMAIL` `GIT_COMMITTER_NAME` `GIT_COMMITTER_EMAIL` ·
  `IdentityMigrationResult`: `migrated` (slugs moved), `skipped` (identity rows that could not
  move, each with a reason — commonly that no KEK is active, so they stay in `secrets`, and
  stay redacted), `noop` (nothing needed doing)
- **Verbs** — runs at broker start; it must run before `registerSecretValues`, or the identity
  values stay registered for the life of the process

## Tool sources, custom tools and MCP

Everything an agent can call that csuite did not write itself hangs off one row: a
**tool source**, which the broker holds a credential for and executes on the agent's
behalf. Three words fragment at this family's edges. **Binding** is a member's access to a
source and nothing else; the HTTP call a custom tool declares is a **request
template**. "MCP server" is always qualified, because three unrelated servers answer
to it. "Credential" is always qualified, because four unrelated secrets answer to it.

### Tool source

A platform-registered provider of external tools, "distributed to bound members via
the instruction packet and invoked through the broker (the broker holds the
third-party credential; the agent never sees it)" (`ToolSource`, SDK). The `slug` is
immutable — the change-event thread key `tool:<slug>` rides on it.

- **Names** — SDK `ToolSource`, `ToolSourceSummary`, `ToolSourceSchema`,
  `ListToolSourcesResponse` · DB `tool_sources` · route `GET /tool-sources`
  (tri-auth; the REST reference still labels this family "dual-auth", the older word —
  owned by the auth family) · CLI `csuite tools list` · MCP `tool_sources_list`,
  `tool_sources_view`, `tool_sources_create`, `tool_sources_update`,
  `tool_sources_delete` · UI "Tools" (nav, panel title; URL `/tools`) · docs
  [Tool sources](/docs/concepts/tool-sources)
- **Not** — Secret and Variable (broker-held values injected as environment
  variables on the agent child; a tool-source credential never leaves the broker) ·
  notification endpoint (routed by `targets`, with no `all_members` column and no
  bindings table, so it is a sibling of this shape and not an instance of it) ·
  local MCP server (a process on a member's workstation, registered nowhere) ·
  "Tools" (the web UI's label for this same registry — one surface's noun, not a
  second concept; the panel's own empty state says "No tool sources yet")
- **Values** — `kind`: `custom` — tools declared on the platform and executed
  broker-side against a third-party HTTP API · `mcp` — a remote MCP server the
  broker connects to as an MCP client
- **Verbs** — list · get · create · update · delete · bind · refresh · invoke. The
  CLI adds `enable` / `disable`, which are sugar over `update` with `{enabled}`, and
  accepts undocumented aliases: `create` for `add`, `remove`/`delete` for `rm`,
  `credential` for `cred`, `define` for `def`; `cred-rm` works but is missing from
  the top-level `csuite` help line.

### Request template

The HTTP call a custom tool declares and the broker executes: method, URL template,
optional headers, body template, content type and result path. Placeholders are
`{{args.<name>}}`, the URL origin is fixed at save time and re-checked at execute, and
the credential is injected by the executor — never through a template. The type, the
column and the MCP argument all still spell it `binding`; that is the legacy spelling,
and it is the collision D10 resolves.

- **Names** — SDK `CustomToolBinding`, `SetCustomToolRequest.binding` · DB
  `custom_tools.binding_json` · core `validateBinding`, `expandBinding`,
  `BindingValidationError` · MCP `tool_sources_define_tool` ("an HTTP `binding` the
  broker executes … Binding shape: `{…}`") · UI `TOOL_JSON_TEMPLATE` · docs
  [Tool sources](/docs/concepts/tool-sources)
- **Not** — Member binding (a member's access to a source, which is the sense that keeps
  the word: `TOOL_SOURCE_PATHS.binding(slug, name)` is a member-binding path while
  `SetCustomToolRequest.binding` is this) · Tool source (the UI empty state offers "a
  custom API binding", meaning a whole `kind=custom` source; a request template is one
  field on one tool) · endpoint template (`NotificationEndpoint.template`, which shapes
  the text of an inbound delivery and executes nothing)
- **Values** — `method`: `GET` · `POST` · `PUT` · `PATCH` · `DELETE`. `bodyTemplate`
  takes a string (raw text, placeholders interpolated) or a JSON value (structural
  template: a string that is exactly one placeholder passes the arg's raw JSON value
  through, and a missing arg omits the containing key)
- **Decided** — D10: the member↔resource relation keeps "binding" and this sense becomes
  the **request template**, over keeping bare `binding` here and renaming that relation
  "grant" — the relation carries the word across three tables, three stores, the routes,
  the CLI and the SDK's `bind` verb, while this sense holds one field on one type
  (2026-09-03)

### Custom tool definition

One tool declared on a `kind=custom` source: a name, a description, a JSON Schema for
its arguments passed to agents verbatim, and a request template. "A custom tool is
configuration, not code."

- **Names** — SDK `CustomToolDef`, `SetCustomToolRequest`, `ToolNameSchema` · DB
  `custom_tools` · route `PUT /tool-sources/:slug/tools/:name`,
  `DELETE /tool-sources/:slug/tools/:name` · CLI `csuite tools def`,
  `csuite tools def-rm` · MCP `tool_sources_define_tool`,
  `tool_sources_delete_tool` · UI "+ Define tool" · docs
  [Tool sources](/docs/concepts/tool-sources)
- **Not** — Request template (one field on this) · Resolved tool (this same tool minus
  its request template, as the agent receives it) · cached MCP tool (`kind=mcp` sources have no
  defs at all — `GetToolSourceResponse.tools` is an untagged union, and `source.kind`
  is its only discriminator)
- **Values** — a tool name is `[A-Za-z0-9_-]{1,64}`, so it may contain `_` and even
  `__`; a source slug may not, which is what makes the first `__` an unambiguous
  separator
- **Verbs** — define · delete. The SDK client spells the write `setCustomTool`.

### Resolved tool

"One tool as resolved for a member's instruction packet — the projection the runner
turns into an MCP tool named `<source>__<name>`" (`ResolvedTool`, SDK). It is a
custom tool definition minus its request template, or a cached MCP tool minus its
annotations; the template and the credential stay on the broker.

- **Names** — SDK `ResolvedTool`, `ResolvedToolSource`,
  `InstructionsResponse.toolSources` · core `resolveFor` · CLI runtime
  `externalTools`, `externalToolName`, `parseExternalToolName` · UI
  `{source.slug}__{t.name}` · docs [MCP tools](/docs/dev/mcp-tools) "External tools"
- **Not** — Custom tool definition (the stored def, with its request template) · cached MCP
  tool (`McpCachedTool`, with its `annotations`) · built-in tools (the runner's own,
  which no source supplies)
- **Values** — the agent-visible name is `<source-slug>__<tool-name>`, and only that
  spelling names both halves by their real field; `<source>__<name>` in comments and
  docs and `<slug>__<name>` in MCP tool descriptions are the same convention.
  `ResolvedToolSource.source` holds a slug, not a display name.

### Tool-source credential

The one secret a source authenticates with upstream: a bearer token or a named header
value. "Write-only: the secret is stored KEK-encrypted and never returned" — writes
fail closed with `no_kek` when no KEK is active, and only the invoke path decrypts.

- **Names** — SDK `SetToolCredentialRequest`, `ToolCredentialKind`,
  `ToolSourceSummary.hasCredential` · core `DecryptedCredential` · DB
  `tool_source_credentials` · route `PUT /tool-sources/:slug/credential`,
  `DELETE /tool-sources/:slug/credential` · CLI `csuite tools cred`,
  `csuite tools cred-rm` · MCP `tool_sources_set_credential`,
  `tool_sources_delete_credential` · UI "Credential", "Set credential" · docs
  [Tool sources](/docs/concepts/tool-sources)
- **Not** — Secret (an environment value the runner injects on the agent child; this
  one never leaves the broker process) · Member binding (which members may invoke the
  source; the credential is what the broker presents upstream once one of them does) ·
  member token (the `csuite_`-prefixed bearer
  a member authenticates to the broker with) · model credential (the agent's own
  provider key, absent as the `no_credential` runner condition) · `credentialMode`
  (how a member enrolls) · a per-member credential, which does not exist —
  `tool_source_credentials.member_name` is always NULL and is a
  forward-compatibility seam
- **Values** — `kind`: `bearer` — sent as `Authorization: Bearer <secret>` ·
  `header` — sent as `<headerName>: <secret>`
- **Verbs** — set · delete. There is no read: `hasCredential` reports existence only.

### Invoke

A broker-executed call to one tool on one source. The broker checks the caller is
bound, expands the request template or relays a `tools/call` upstream, applies the stored
credential, caps the result, and records the call; "tool-level failures are
successful calls with `isError: true` (MCP convention)".

- **Names** — SDK `InvokeToolRequest`, `InvokeToolResponse`, `Client.invokeTool` ·
  route `POST /tool-sources/:slug/tools/:name/invoke` · core `executeCustomTool`,
  `ToolCallResult`, `McpToolManager.invoke` · CLI runtime `handleExternalToolCall`,
  dispatching `<source-slug>__<tool-name>` · diagnostics
  `toolinvoke.audit_append_failed` · docs
  [Tool sources](/docs/concepts/tool-sources)
- **Not** — Tool action (the record written after the call, not the call) · source
  unavailable (`McpUnavailableError` → 502: the upstream is unreachable, an operator
  problem the model cannot self-correct, deliberately not an `isError` result) ·
  `no_kek` on a registry write (503) versus `no_kek` on a decrypt during invoke (500
  "credential unavailable") — one error code, two contracts
- **Values** — the authorization order is fixed and leaks nothing to unbound probers:
  404 unknown slug → 403 unbound → 409 disabled → 413 payload over 1 MiB → 400 bad
  payload → 404 unknown tool. Result caps: `TOOL_RESULT_MAX_BYTES` = 64 KiB, applied
  to each text block (and to the streamed raw body of a custom call) ·
  `RELAY_TOTAL_MAX_BYTES` = 256 KiB, applied to the whole serialized relay. Neither
  name states its granularity, and only the first is documented.
- **Verbs** — invoke

### Upstream MCP server

The remote Streamable-HTTP MCP endpoint a `kind=mcp` tool source points at. The
broker holds "one lazily-connected, single-flight `Client` per source … shared across
member invokes", discovers that server's tools into a cache on `refresh`, and relays
`tools/call` to it.

- **Names** — SDK `ToolSourceConfig.url`, `ToolSourceKind` value `mcp`,
  `RefreshToolSourceResponse` · core `McpToolManager`, `McpUnavailableError`
  ("Upstream MCP server unreachable after the single reconnect retry"),
  `McpCachedTool` · server `McpClientManager`, `createMcpClientManager` · DB
  `mcp_tools_cache` · route `POST /tool-sources/:slug/refresh` · CLI
  `csuite tools refresh` · MCP `tool_sources_refresh` · UI "Refresh from upstream" ·
  docs [Tool sources](/docs/concepts/tool-sources)
- **Not** — csuite MCP bridge (the stdio server the runner hands the agent) · local
  MCP server (a stdio process on a member's workstation) · tool source (the registry
  row; the upstream server is what one kind of row points at)
- **Values** — a cached tool's `name`, `description` and `inputSchema` reach the
  agent; `annotations` does not — it is discovered, stored in `annotations_json`,
  read by nothing, and excluded from the `changed` fingerprint, so `readOnlyHint` and
  `destructiveHint` never reach the model and an annotations-only upstream change
  reports `changed: false` *(defined in code only)*
- **Verbs** — refresh · invoke · invalidate

### csuite MCP bridge

The stdio MCP server an agent spawns and speaks MCP to — "a thin relay that owns
nothing except its two endpoints", stdio to the agent and IPC to the runner. It holds
no tools and never talks to the broker.

- **Names** — CLI `csuite mcp-bridge` · codex config `[mcp_servers.csuite]` · docs
  [CLI reference](/docs/reference/cli#mcp-bridge) — "Internal — spawned by agents,
  never by you"
- **Not** — Upstream MCP server (the third-party endpoint a `kind=mcp` source
  proxies) · local MCP server (a workstation process listed in a codex config) ·
  Toolbox (what the bridge carries; the bridge itself defines none of it)

### Local MCP server

A stdio MCP process on a member's own workstation, declared in
`~/.config/csuite/codex/mcp-servers.json` and written into the ephemeral codex home
beside the bridge. Codex-only, and outside the broker entirely: no registration, no
credential, no grant, no audit.

- **Names** — CLI `LocalMcpServer`, `LocalMcpConfig`, `LocalMcpConfigError` · doctor
  check "codex local MCP servers" · docs
  [Codex runner](/docs/runners/codex#local-stdio-mcp-servers) — "Tool sources are for
  URL-reachable servers that the broker can call"
- **Not** — Tool source (the generated codex TOML calls this file a "Local stdio MCP
  source"; nothing about it is registered, and its tools appear in no instruction
  packet) · upstream MCP server (reachable by URL from the broker, which is the whole
  difference) · csuite MCP bridge

### Toolbox

The full set of MCP tools one member's agent sees in `tools/list`: the runner's
built-in tools, filtered by that member's permissions, plus that member's resolved
external tools. The built-in half is fixed for the session; the external half is
live, and a registry change pushes `tools/list_changed` mid-session.

- **Names** — CLI runtime `defineTools`, `buildExternalTools` · MCP
  `tool_sources_bindings` ("Bound members' agents see the tools in their toolbox") ·
  docs [MCP tools](/docs/dev/mcp-tools) ("a fixed toolbox"),
  [Tool sources](/docs/concepts/tool-sources) ("their toolbox")
- **Not** — Tool source (a registry row; its resolved tools are one slice of one
  toolbox) · built-in tools (the fixed half only — "fixed toolbox" in the dev
  reference names that half and is wrong about the whole). The built-in half has no
  settled name in the tree: [Files](/docs/concepts/files) says "built-in tools", the
  [IPC protocol](/docs/dev/ipc-protocol) reference says "builtin tools", and "core
  tools" appears nowhere. Use **built-in tools**.

### Tool action

The record of one tool run on a member's activity stream. Three producers write it —
the Claude transcript reader, the codex rollout parser, and the broker's invoke route
— and the SDK names all three, splitting them into **agent-native** and
**broker-audit** and telling a reader to branch on `source`
(`packages/sdk/src/types.ts:2422-2445`).

- **Names** — SDK `ActivityToolAction`, kind `tool_action` · CLI runtime
  `transcript-reader.ts` (Claude), `agents/codex/rollout-parser.ts` (codex) · core
  the invoke route's audit append (`packages/core/src/app.ts:2918`) · UI timeline filter chip "tools"
- **Not** — Invoke (the call; a tool action is what survives it) · activity stream
  (the durable per-member record this is one entry kind in) · tool source (the
  `source` field is a **capture-source** tag naming the reader that produced the row;
  its `'tool_source'` value is the one that names a registry concept, and it means
  "the broker wrote this", not "this tool came from a source")
- **Values** — `agent` and `source` are open `string` fields on the wire, not unions;
  the doc comment enumerates the shipped values and the code writes exactly those.
  What is written, and the provenance you read off
  it: `agent: 'claude'`, `source: 'transcript'` — `result`, `isError`, `toolUseId`,
  and **no `input`** (the tool's arguments ride the matching `llm_exchange` turn, and
  `toolUseId` is what folds the two together) · `agent: 'codex'`,
  `source: 'codex_rollout'` — redacted `input` and `result`, plus a `toolUseId` from
  the Responses API `call_id`, which the SDK comment now names · `agent: 'broker'`,
  `source: 'tool_source'` — `toolName` is
  `<source-slug>__<tool-name>`, `input` is the args or `{truncated: true}` past 8 KiB
  of serialized JSON, `result` is reduced to `{isError, contentBlocks}`, and there is
  never a `toolUseId`. Comparing `input` across the three compares three different
  things. *(defined in code only)*

## External notifications and web push

Two subsystems share the word "notification" and share no code. **External
notifications** run inbound: a webhook reaches `POST /hooks/<slug>`, is verified, and
becomes a message inside the team. **Web push** runs outbound: a message sent through
`POST /push` is fanned out to a member's browsers and shown by the service worker. They
meet nowhere — a hook delivery never reaches a device. The bare word "notification" is
retired: every occurrence resolves to external notification, web push, toast, MCP
notification, or an ambient event. The first three are defined here; **ambient event** is
defined with the messaging vocabulary, and **MCP notification** is JSON-RPC protocol
vocabulary that names no product concept. Both appear below only as exclusions.

### External notification

An event from outside the team — a GitHub webhook, a monitoring alert, a `curl` from a
deploy script — received on the unauthenticated-but-verified ingress `POST /hooks/:slug`,
normalized, and routed to members or channels as ambient `<channel>` input. Strictly
inbound: no outbound webhook sender exists anywhere in the tree.

- **Names** — SDK section banner "External Notifications"
  (`packages/sdk/src/types.ts:1100`) · permission
  `notifications.manage` · route `POST /hooks/:slug`, admin surface `/notifications/*` ·
  CLI `csuite notifications` (alias `csuite hooks`) · the `notifications_*` MCP tools,
  `notifications_list` through `notifications_profile_set_secret` · UI nav "Notifications", aria "Manage external
  notifications" · event kind `external_notification` · agent instruction block
  "── External notifications ──" · docs
  [External notifications](/docs/concepts/external-notifications)
- **Not** — Web push (outbound to a browser; unrelated code path, and
  `notifications.manage` does not gate it) · Toast (in-app transient alert) · MCP
  notification (`kind: 'mcp_notification'`, a JSON-RPC frame the runner asks the bridge to
  emit on stdio for `tools/list_changed`) · Ambient event (a broker message rendered into an
  agent's `<channel>` through the runner's `channelSink`) · Notification-endpoint registry
  event (`kind: 'notification_endpoint'`, a config change, not a delivered payload)
- **Decided** — D26: **external notification** survives, always two words and always
  documented as inbound, over renaming the product concept to *inbound hook* /
  *webhook endpoint* and demoting `/notifications/*` to legacy spelling (2026-09-03)

The plain-English reading of the term is the opposite of the truth, which is why the
qualifier and the word *inbound* are both mandatory. [Events](/docs/concepts/events) says
"For delivery **to** external systems — Slack, webhooks, pagers — see external
notifications"; that sentence is wrong about the code and is scheduled for deletion.

### Notification endpoint

The configured receiver for one sender: a slug-addressed hook at `POST /hooks/<slug>` that
verifies an inbound request, filters and templates it, and routes it to member or channel
targets under a delivery policy. Slugs are immutable — the ingress URL and the
`hook:<slug>` sender identity ride on them; `displayName` is the mutable label.

- **Names** — SDK `NotificationEndpoint`, projection `NotificationEndpointSummary` (adds
  `hasSecret`) · core `CreateEndpointInput` / `UpdateEndpointInput`, row `EndpointRow` · DB
  `notification_endpoints` · route `/notifications/endpoints/:slug`, ingress `/hooks/:slug` ·
  CLI `csuite notifications add|view|update|rm <slug>` · MCP `notifications_create`,
  `notifications_view`, `notifications_update`, `notifications_delete` · UI "+ New endpoint" ·
  docs "Endpoint — the thing you configure"
- **Not** — Push subscription (its `endpoint` field is the browser's push-service URL, a
  capability token, not a hook receiver) · Auth profile (verification config plus secret,
  shared across endpoints; an endpoint references one by slug) · Route (an HTTP path — docs
  that call any path an "endpoint" mean route) · Hook, elsewhere in the tree, means an
  in-process callback ("Push fanout hook", `apps/server/src/run.ts:707`); only `csuite hooks`
  and `/hooks/<slug>` mean a receiver
- **Verbs** — `list` · `get` · `create` · `update` · `delete` · set/delete signing secret.
  The CLI spells create and delete `add` and `rm`; MCP spells get `notifications_view`.
  Delete cascades the endpoint's delivery receipts and pending rows in one transaction.

### `hook:<slug>`

One string with two roles. As the `from` on a delivered message it is the **sender
identity**: member names cannot contain `:`, so external content is collision-free and
visibly foreign. As a **thread tag** it names the endpoint's registry-change history —
