`notification_endpoint` events about the config, never the payloads that arrived.

- **Names** — core sender `from: 'hook:<slug>'`
  (`packages/core/src/notifications/dispatcher.ts:181`) · thread prefix
  `NOTIFICATION_THREAD_PREFIX = 'hook:'` (`packages/core/src/event-log.ts:79`,
  module-private) · registry-event `data.thread: 'hook:<slug>'`
  (`packages/core/src/app.ts:3804`) · docs [REST API](/docs/dev/rest-api)
- **Not** — Delivered content does not carry a `hook:` thread. A member target gets an
  untagged DM and a channel target gets `chan:<id>`, so the `hook:ci-alerts` thread holds
  who edited the endpoint, not what CI said. The constant's name says `NOTIFICATION_`
  while its value says `hook:`; the value is canonical.

### Verification

How an inbound request proves it came from the configured sender. Two schemes, both
compared in constant time, both fail closed: an endpoint with no secret accepts nothing,
and an endpoint whose secret exists with no active KEK throws rather than verify against
garbage. The HTTP answer to a failure is a bare 401 — the reason lands on the receipt and
never on the wire.

- **Names** — SDK `NotificationAuthKind`, `NotificationAuthConfig` (`headerName`, `prefix`) ·
  core `verifyInbound`, `VerifyResult`, `ResolvedVerification`, `IngressVerification`,
  defaults `DEFAULT_HMAC_HEADER` (`x-hub-signature-256`), `DEFAULT_HMAC_PREFIX` (`sha256=`),
  `DEFAULT_HEADER_SECRET_HEADER` (`x-hook-secret`) · SDK/CLI field `auth` · CLI
  `--auth <kind>`, `--auth-header`, `--auth-prefix` · UI column "Scheme" · docs
  [External notifications](/docs/concepts/external-notifications) § Verification
- **Not** — Auth profile (the reusable record holding one of these configs plus its secret) ·
  Signing secret (the material — write-only over the wire, KEK-encrypted at rest, same
  posture as tool-source credentials) · Member authentication (`notifications.manage` gates
  the admin surface; the ingress is unauthenticated at the middleware layer and verified
  per endpoint)
- **Values** — `NotificationAuthKind`: `hmac-sha256` — hex HMAC of the raw request body
  carried in a header, GitHub-compatible by default · `header-secret` — the shared secret
  carried verbatim in a header, for senders that cannot sign. Two values, no third.

Three layers, three spellings of one thing: `auth` is the wire field, `--auth` is the flag,
*verification* is the prose. Keep all three and never let a fourth in.

### Auth profile

One verification config plus one secret, reused by several endpoints, so rotating a
sender's secret is one write. An endpoint references a profile by slug in `authProfile`;
when it is set, the endpoint's inline `auth` is ignored.

- **Names** — SDK `NotificationProfile`, projection `NotificationProfileSummary` (adds
  `hasSecret`, `endpointCount`) · SDK field `NotificationEndpoint.authProfile` · core row
  `ProfileRow` · DB `notification_profiles` · route `/notifications/profiles/:slug`,
  `…/secret` · CLI `csuite notifications profiles`, flag `--profile <slug>`, rendered
  `profile:<slug>` in the auth column · MCP `notifications_profiles`,
  `notifications_profile_create`, `notifications_profile_delete`,
  `notifications_profile_set_secret` · UI "Auth profiles" · error code `profile_in_use`
- **Not** — "Public profile", the member-identity section of the same web UI's Account
  panel · Delivery policy (targets, debounce, offline and busy handling live on the
  endpoint, never on the profile — a profile carries verification only)
- **Verbs** — `list` · `create` · `update` · `delete` · set/delete shared secret. Only REST
  carries the full set: `PATCH /notifications/profiles/:slug` and
  `DELETE /notifications/profiles/:slug/secret` have no CLI subcommand and no MCP tool.
  Deleting a profile an endpoint still references is a 409 `profile_in_use`; an endpoint
  silently losing its verifier is an outage.

Always two words. Bare "profile" collides with member profile in the same UI, and the
concepts page declares "Profile" canonical and then never uses it again.

### Notification delivery

"The receipt for one inbound request — the audit/debug/replay unit. The full raw body is
retained server-side (capped) for replay; the wire shape carries only a preview."
**Receipt** is the accepted prose synonym for exactly this row; it has no identifier
anywhere in code, and there is no second entity behind it.

- **Names** — SDK `NotificationDelivery` · core `DeliveryRecord` (the same row plus
  dispatch state: `body`, `rendered`; projected by `toWireDelivery`) · DB
  `notification_deliveries`, row `DeliveryDbRow` · route
  `GET /notifications/endpoints/:slug/deliveries`,
  `POST /notifications/deliveries/:id/replay` · ingress response `HookIngressResponse`
  (`id`, `status`) · CLI `csuite notifications deliveries <slug>`,
  `csuite notifications replay <deliveryId>` · MCP `notifications_deliveries`,
  `notifications_replay` · UI "Deliveries" · docs "Every delivery gets a receipt"
- **Not** — Message delivery (the `message_deliveries` ledger row for one pushed message
  and one recipient; a different table, a different status vocabulary) · Queued delivery (a
  `notification_pending` hold that *references* delivery ids; the delivery row itself is
  updated in place) · Message (what a delivery becomes; `messageIds` lists one per member
  target or channel post) · Endpoint (the config; a delivery is one arrival at it)
- **Verbs** — `list` (newest first; `limit` defaults to 50 and clamps to 500, cursor `before`
  is epoch-ms — D44 respells it `before_ts`, not yet on the wire) · `replay`. Retention is a
  separate bound: `DELIVERY_RETENTION_PER_ENDPOINT` keeps the newest 1000 receipts per
  endpoint. A replay creates a **new** delivery whose `replayOf` points at the source and
  re-runs filters, template and policy; verification, dedupe and rate limiting are skipped. A
  `rejected` delivery cannot be replayed — it never passed verification and its body was not
  retained.

### Notification delivery status

The state of one delivery: nine values on the wire, written by the dispatcher, rendered as
a badge per value in the endpoint's Deliveries list.

- **Names** — SDK `NotificationDeliveryStatus` · DB `notification_deliveries.status` and
  `status_reason` · UI badge map `STATUS_BADGE` · docs [REST API](/docs/dev/rest-api)
- **Not** — `message_deliveries.state` (`pending` `accepted` `acted` `handled` `refused`
  `unreported`), the ledger row for one pushed message and one recipient · `PushSendOutcome.kind`
  (`ok` / `gone` / `failed`), which is what one push service said about one web-push
  subscription · `level` / `severity`, which grade a message rather than record its fate
- **Values** — `delivered` — a recipient runner **acknowledged** it, either synchronously
  (`result.delivery.acknowledgement.status === 'settled'`) or later, through a message
  disposition of `acted` or `handled`; not merely that it was sent · `pending` — five
  causes: debouncing, offline-queued, busy-waiting, pushed but not yet acknowledged, and a
  `deferred` disposition left deliberately open · `expired` — offline-queued past
  `queueTtlMs` without a wake · `dropped` — every member target was offline under
  `if_offline: drop`, or a subscriber refused the message · `rejected` — signature
  verification failed, or no secret was configured; body not retained, replay refused ·
  `filtered` — a filter rule failed, so nothing reached the targets · `duplicate` — the
  dedupe header matched an earlier delivery · `coalesced` — merged into another delivery's
  message · `failed` — reserved; no code path writes it
- **Decided** — D25: `delivered` means **acknowledged by a recipient runner**, and `failed`
  is **reserved and never emitted**, over the SDK doc comment's "pushed to at least one
  target" and over wiring the push-failure path to write `failed` (2026-09-03)

The code is the arbiter here and the doc comments are the thing that is wrong: an
un-acknowledged but successfully pushed delivery is written `pending`, and the
push-failure path records a note and lands on `dropped` or `pending`. An operator debugging
receipts will never see `failed`, and `delivered` is a statement about the agent, not about
the network.

### Queued delivery

A hold on one (endpoint, member) pair, created when policy says queue an offline member or
wait out a busy one. The hold names the delivery ids it is holding and a deadline; the
deliveries themselves stay `pending` in place until it flushes, expires, or is force-
delivered by the starvation guard.

- **Names** — DB `notification_pending` (`reason` is `offline` or `busy`, plus
  `deadline_at` and `delivery_ids`) · core `PendingRecord` · policy fields
  `NotificationDeliveryPolicy.ifOffline`, `ifBusy`, `queueTtlMs`, `maxWaitMs` ·
  per-delivery overrides `?if_offline=`, `?if_busy=`, `?level=` parsed into
  `NotificationOverrides` · CLI `--if-offline`, `--if-busy`, `--queue-ttl-ms`,
  `--max-wait-ms` · docs "**Queue** holds deliveries for an offline member"
- **Not** — Notification delivery (the receipt row; deleting an endpoint removes "delivery
  receipts + pending rows" because they are two tables) · Debounce buffer (an in-process
  coalescing window, not a persisted hold)

No wire type exists for the hold. It surfaces to a user as the delivery's `pending` status
and as the "queued 47m while you were offline" fact inside the provenance wrap.
*(defined in code only)*

### Provenance wrap

The broker-authored frame around delivered external content. "The provenance WRAP is
non-templatable by design: whatever an endpoint's template produces sits inside a
broker-authored frame that names the endpoint, marks the content as originating outside
the team… The frame always outranks the content — no endpoint config can make external
bytes read as a teammate speaking."

- **Names** — core `composeBody` in `packages/core/src/notifications/render.ts` · wire tag
  `<external_content endpoint="…" delivery="…" received="…">` · header text "External
  notification from endpoint …" · agent instructions call the header the **preamble** and
  the tags a **fence** · docs "wrapped in a frame"
- **Not** — Template (`NotificationEndpoint.template`, which controls only what appears
  *inside* the frame) · Instruction blocks (the standing brief; the wrap is per-message)

### Web push

Outbound delivery of a chat message to a member's registered browsers through a push
service, shown by the service worker as an operating-system notification. It covers
messages sent through `POST /push` — direct messages and broadcasts — and nothing else.

- **Names** — core `dispatchPush` ("Push fanout"), policy `shouldPush` ("Push notification
  policy"), `HIGH_PRIORITY_LEVELS` · payload `PushPayload` in
  `packages/core/src/push-dispatch.ts`, mirrored in the service worker as
  `CsuitePushPayload` · seam `PushSender`, `PushSendOutcome` · server
  `createWebPushSender()` · app hook `onPushed` · route `GET /push/vapid-public-key` ·
  config `vapidPublicKey`, `vapidPrivateKey`, `vapidSubject` · UI Account section eyebrow
  "Notifications", row "Push on this device", component `NotificationToggle`, signal
  `pushState` · docs [Events](/docs/concepts/events), "Notifications when you're away"
- **Not** — External notification (inbound; a hook delivery never fires a push). The web UI
  labels both "Notifications" one screen apart — the nav item is the inbound registry, gated
  on `notifications.manage`; the Account section is web push, gated on nothing — so a member
  who cannot see the nav item can still toggle push · `POST /push`, the chat-send route,
  whose body type is a **different** exported `PushPayload` in `packages/sdk/src/types.ts`
  (`to`, `title`, `body`, `level`, `data`, `attachments`) — same name, unrelated shape ·
  Toast (the in-app alert shown instead when the tab is visible) · Ambient event
- **Values** — `PushPayload.severity`: `normal` `high` — derived from the message `level`
  (`warning`, `error`, `critical` map to `high`), and `high` sets `requireInteraction` so
  the banner stays until dismissed. It is not a sixth log level.

Scope is narrower than the code's own comment claims. `onPushed` is documented as firing
"once per successful `/push` (or broker-level push)", but it is invoked from the `POST /push`
handler alone — one of twelve `broker.push` call sites in `app.ts`. Channel posts,
objective events, registry events and every external-notification delivery reach no device.
Push also skips a recipient who has a live subscriber attached, and never echoes to the
sender.

### Push subscription

"A push subscription is a capability URL + crypto keys the browser hands the server after
`pushManager.subscribe()`. Treat the endpoint like a session token: anyone holding it can
push to the device." One row per (member name, subscription endpoint URL) pair, so a member
can have many devices enrolled.

- **Names** — SDK `PushSubscriptionPayload` (`endpoint`, `keys.p256dh`, `keys.auth`),
  `PushSubscriptionResponse` · core `PushSubscriptionRow`, `PushSubscriptionInput`,
  `PushSubscriptionStore` · DB `push_subscriptions` (`member_name`, `endpoint`, `p256dh`,
  `auth`, `last_error_code`) · route `POST /push/subscriptions`,
  `DELETE /push/subscriptions/:id` · UI "Push on this device", `pushState`
- **Not** — Notification endpoint (the inbound hook receiver). The field name `endpoint`
  here is fixed by the W3C Push API; in prose call this one the **subscription endpoint
  URL**, and log it only through `redactEndpoint` because the full URL is the capability ·
  Session (`DispatchDeps.sessions` is typed `PushSubscriptionStore`, a leaked name — a
  subscription is not a session, and it belongs to a member, not to a login)
- **Verbs** — `create` (idempotent on the subscription endpoint URL: a repeat replaces the
  crypto keys and clears error state rather than duplicating) · `delete`, scoped by member
  name so a guessed id cannot reach another member's row. A 404 or 410 from the push service
  deletes the row as dead. There is no list route: `listForMember` is a store method the
  fanout calls, and a member sees only the current device's `pushState`.

### Toast

"The canonical in-app transient notification primitive" — a short message the web UI shows
inside the app, with an optional action. Dwell is 6s, 8s when it carries an action, and
sticky for errors; at most three on screen.

- **Names** — web UI `toast.info` / `toast.success` / `toast.warn` / `toast.error`,
  `toasts` signal, `ToastKind`, `<ToastContainer/>` in
  `packages/web-ui/src/components/ui/ToastContainer.tsx` ·
  `packages/web-ui/src/lib/notify.ts`, which fires a toast for an incoming live message
  when the tab is visible
- **Not** — Web push (the OS-level notification the service worker shows when the tab is in
  the background; `notify.ts` fires a toast precisely so a visible tab does not produce
  both) · External notification

*(defined in code only)*

## Files and blobs

The broker holds one path tree with two top-level scopes — member homes and
objective folders — over a content-addressed store of bytes. The distinction that
governs everything else: a **path** is the identity of a file and a **blob** is the
identity of bytes, so two paths can share one blob and neither knows about the
other. "Blob" unqualified is the trap: a second, unrelated content-addressed store
holds captured API bodies in a different database.

### Virtual filesystem

The path tree the broker serves at `/fs/*`: "Paths are absolute, Unix-like, with `/`
as separator… Two top-level scopes share the path tree" — member homes at
`/<member-name>/` and objective namespaces at `/objectives/<id>/`. Root `/` is
implicit and has no row. Listing `/` is not a listing of both scopes: it returns the
top-level directories the viewer owns, so a member sees their own home and nothing
else, and `members.manage` sees every home. `/objectives` is reachable by path and
never enumerated at the root.

- **Names** — SDK `FS_PATHS.read` · route `GET /fs/ls` `GET /fs/stat`
  `GET /fs/read/*` `POST /fs/write` `POST /fs/mkdir` `DELETE /fs/rm` `POST /fs/mv`
  `GET /fs/shared` `GET /fs/all` · SDK `fsList` `fsStat` `fsRead` `fsReadStream`
  `fsWrite` `fsMkdir` `fsRm` `fsMv` `fsShared` `fsAll` · CLI `csuite fs put` ·
  `csuite fs get` · MCP `fs_ls` `fs_stat` `fs_read` `fs_write` `fs_upload`
  `fs_download` `fs_mkdir` `fs_rm` `fs_mv` `fs_shared` · UI the "Files" panel and
  its three modes, "Browse tree", "Shared with me", "All files" · docs
  [Files](/docs/concepts/files), which calls the first mode "Tree"
- **Not** — the runner's own disk (`fs_upload`'s `localPath` is an OS path on the
  runner machine, resolved against the runner cwd, never a csuite path; the codex
  adapter's ephemeral `CODEX_HOME` lives there too) · file blob (the bytes an entry
  points at, addressed by hash rather than by path) · filesystem entry (one row in
  this tree)
- **Verbs** — list, stat, read, write, mkdir, rm, mv (files only — a directory move
  is refused), upload, download. Upload and download are one operation pair under
  two spellings: `csuite fs put` / `csuite fs get` on the command line, `fs_upload` /
  `fs_download` in the toolbox. They stream bytes past the agent's context rather
  than through it, which is why they stay outside the `create`/`get` verb grammar.
- **Decided** — D27: record the register pair — code says *namespace*, product prose
  says *folder*, one referent — over collapsing both registers onto one word
  (2026-09-03)

### Filesystem entry

One row in the path tree, "either a file or a directory… For directories: `size`,
`mimeType`, and `hash` are null. For files: all three are populated." Path is the
primary key, so a path names at most one entry. `owner` is derived from the path by
`ownerOf()` and never supplied by a caller.

- **Names** — SDK `FsEntry`, `FsEntrySchema`, `FsEntryKindSchema`,
  `FsListResponse`, `FsEntryResponse`, `FsWriteResponse` · DB `fs_entries` · core
  `ownerOf()` · MCP `FsEntry` in the return line of `fs_write`, `fs_upload`,
  `fs_mkdir` and `fs_mv` · UI the `FilesPanel` rows · docs
  `{ entries: FsEntry[] }` [REST API](/docs/dev/rest-api)
- **Not** — file blob (the bytes; many entries may point at one hash) · attachment
  (a frozen `{path, name, size, mimeType}` snapshot embedded in a message or an
  objective, which the entry outranks — the entry holds the authoritative metadata)
  · the DB row's own column names (`content_hash` on disk is `hash` on the wire,
  `mime_type` is `mimeType`; that is a mapping, not a second concept)
- **Values** — `kind`: `file` · `directory`
- **Verbs** — stat, list

### Member home

The per-member scope at `/<member-name>/`: "The first path segment is the member
name; that name is also the row's `owner` value, and the read/write ACL is
'owner-only + members.manage + grant-holders'". Created for every roster member at
boot and idempotently by `ensureHome`, so an empty own home lists as `[]`, not 404.

- **Names** — core `ensureHome(memberName)` · DB `fs_entries.owner` = the member name
  · route `GET /fs/ls?path=/` (every visible home root) · MCP `fs_ls` — "Your home is
  `/<name>`" · UI "Home" button, "All member files" · docs "home folder"
  [Files](/docs/concepts/files)
- **Not** — objective folder (owned by an objective's member set, `owner` = `obj:<id>`,
  never by a person) · "slot home" (a legacy spelling of this, still in the Files
  panel comment and in `fs_shared`'s agent-facing text; **member** is the term —
  see Member) · the runner's OS home directory, including the codex adapter's
  ephemeral `CODEX_HOME`
- **Verbs** — list, stat, read, write, mkdir, rm, mv

### Objective folder

The per-objective scope at `/objectives/<id>/`: "a per-objective scope owned not by
an individual but by the objective's member set (originator + assignee + watchers).
Rows under this prefix have `owner = 'obj:<id>'`; the ACL gate is 'member of the
objective + members.manage'". Membership is asked live on every check, so adding a
watcher grants access immediately and removing one revokes it with no row to clean
up. Access is read **and** write — the MCP text an agent reads says so: "every thread
member … gets read/write access via the namespace ACL." Code calls this an
**objective namespace**; product prose calls it an objective folder. Same thing.
Neither developer reference is authoritative for this scope: the REST reference's
filesystem overview names only per-member homes, and the MCP reference lists a grant
or `members.manage` as the two ways to read outside your home, omitting objective
membership entirely.

- **Names** — core `OBJECTIVE_NAMESPACE_SEGMENT` (`'objectives'`),
  `OBJECTIVE_OWNER_PREFIX` (`'obj:'`), `objectiveNamespacePath()`,
  `parseObjectiveNamespacePath()`, `ObjectiveAclProvider.isMember` · SDK
  `FsOwnerSchema` · DB `fs_entries.owner = 'obj:<id>'` · MCP `objectives_create`
  `attachments` — "mirrored into the objective's namespace" · docs "objective folder",
  "shared folder" [Files](/docs/concepts/files)
- **Not** — file share (a `fs_grants` row for one exact path, read-only;
  objective-folder access writes no grant row, confers write as well as read, and its
  files never appear in `GET /fs/shared`) · member home · objective (the unit of work
  these files hang off — see Objective)
- **Values** — `fs_entries.owner`: a member name for a home entry · `obj:<id>` for an
  objective-folder entry · the bare string `objectives` for the `/objectives`
  directory itself, which belongs to no objective. Only the first is a member name,
  and access must not be inferred from any of them.
- **Verbs** — list, stat, read, write, mkdir, rm, mv

### File share

An `fs_grants` row: "(path, viewer, granted_via) rows. Populated when a message or
objective references an attachment, granting the recipient read access to that exact
path even though the tree otherwise belongs to someone else." Exact-path, never a
prefix; read-only — "`canRead` honours grants, `canWrite` does not". An owner
self-grant is dropped. The primary key is the whole `(path, viewer, granted_via)`
triple, so a repeat of one reference is a no-op while two different messages
referencing one path leave two rows; `GET /fs/shared` selects distinct entries, so
the duplication is invisible in the listing. Removing the path deletes the rows.
Moving the path carries them across to the destination, so the share survives a
rename that leaves the attachment which created it unresolvable.

Two of the three attachment paths write a share: `POST /push` grants each recipient
read on each attached path, and `POST /objectives/:id/discuss` grants every thread
member read on each attachment of that post. Objective **create-time** attachments
write no share — they are mirrored, and access comes from objective membership
instead. Four surfaces still describe the retired behavior — `Objective.attachments`
("Thread members … all receive read grants for each attachment"),
`CreateObjectiveRequest.attachments`, `fsShared()`, and the REST reference's
"message / objective attachments" — and the code wins: `GET /fs/shared` is an inner
join on `fs_grants`, so an objective's create-time attachments are never in it.

- **Names** — DB `fs_grants (path, viewer, granted_at, granted_via)` · core `grant()`,
  `hasGrant()`, `listShared()`, `grantAttachmentsTo()` · route `GET /fs/shared` · SDK
  `fsShared()` · MCP `fs_shared` · UI "Shared with me" toggle, "Shared with you"
  heading · docs "Message shares are read-only by design"
  [Files](/docs/concepts/files)
- **Not** — permission leaf (what "grant" means as a verb in permissions prose and in
  admin errors like "grant it to another member first" — see Permission leaf) ·
  member binding (`secret_bindings` / `variable_bindings`: the sole delivery path
  for a value, refused on an env-name conflict, where a share is an additive
  exception that never fails on conflict — see Member binding) · device authorization grant
  (enrollment) · objective folder (the other read path, and the one that carries
  create-time objective attachments)
- **Values** — `granted_via`: the id of the message the share came from, unprefixed.
  The column comment promises "message id, objective id, etc."; both live call sites
  pass a message id.
- **Verbs** — grant, list

### Attachment

"Lightweight file reference embedded in a `Message` or an objective" —
`{path, name, size, mimeType}` and nothing else. It carries no hash, so it identifies
a row in `fs_entries` by path, not content: move or delete the target and the
attachment stops resolving, even though a move carries the share it created across to
the new path. The server re-derives `name`, `size` and `mimeType` from the stored
entry at attach time, so a caller cannot lie about them; a claim whose path is
missing, is a directory, or is unreadable to the sender is refused.

- **Names** — SDK `Attachment`, `AttachmentSchema` · core `canonicalizeAttachments()`
  · DB `objectives.attachments` (JSON) · route `POST /push`, `POST /objectives`,
  `POST /objectives/:id/discuss` · MCP `attachments` on `broadcast`, `send`,
  `channels_post`, `objectives_discuss`, `objectives_create` · UI
  `MessageAttachments`, "Attachments (optional)", "Attach files" · docs
  [Files](/docs/concepts/files)
- **Not** — `Content-Disposition: attachment` (an HTTP response header `GET /fs/read/*`
  emits for anything not inline-renderable; always write it with its header name) ·
  file blob (an attachment names no hash and copies no bytes) · filesystem entry (the
  row an attachment points at, which owns the authoritative metadata)
- **Verbs** — attach, resolve

### Attachment mirroring

What happens to an attachment listed at objective **create**: a new `fs_entries` row
at `/objectives/<id>/<basename>` pointing at the same blob, after which the
objective's attachment list is rewritten to the mirrored paths. "The originator's
home copy stays put — `copyByBlobRef` shares a single underlying blob, so bytes
aren't duplicated, but each entry is independently deletable." Collision strategy is
fixed to `suffix`, and a failed copy fails the whole create.

- **Names** — core `copyByBlobRef()`, `objectives.setAttachments()` · MCP
  `objectives_create` — "Each is mirrored into the objective's namespace at
  `/objectives/<id>/<basename>`" · docs "copied into the objective's folder"
  [Files](/docs/concepts/files)
- **Not** — file share (what message attachments and objective *discussion*
  attachments do instead — a grant row, no copy, no mirroring) · a copy of the bytes
  (only the metadata row is copied; the blob refcount goes up by one)
- **Verbs** — mirror

### File blob

The bytes behind a file entry: "Content-addressed blob storage seam for the team
filesystem… bytes live behind this interface, addressed by sha256." Metadata carries
a real `refcount` column, incremented on every create, overwrite and copy and
decremented on delete, and the on-disk file is dropped when the count reaches zero.

- **Names** — core `BlobStore` (`putFromStream`, `putFromBuffer`, `openReadStream`,
  `exists`, `delete`), `PutResult {hash, size}`, `PutOptions {maxSize}` · server
  `LocalBlobStore` under `filesRoot` (default `./data/files`), sharded
  `<first-2-of-hash>/<rest>` · DB `fs_blobs (hash, size, refcount, created_at)`,
  `fs_entries.content_hash` · SDK `FsEntry.hash` — "SHA-256 hex of the blob content
  and doubles as the dedup key" · docs "Identical files are stored once under the
  hood" [Files](/docs/concepts/files)
- **Not** — raw body blob (captured API bodies, a different table in a different
  database with no refcount column) · filesystem entry (the path row; many entries
  may point at one blob, and deleting one leaves the others intact) · bare "blob",
  which names both stores and settles nothing
- **Verbs** — put, open, exists, delete

### File size limit

The per-upload ceiling the broker enforces while streaming bytes into the blob store,
before any metadata row is written. An operator sets it in the server config's
`files` block alongside the blob root; the broker defaults to 25 MiB and clamps any
configured value to a 1 GiB hard cap. Exceeding it aborts the upload with the
`too_large` fault code and HTTP 413, and nothing is stored. *(defined in code only)*

- **Names** — server config `files.maxFileSize`, `files.root` · core
  `DEFAULT_MAX_FILE_SIZE` (25 MiB), `HARD_CAP_MAX_FILE_SIZE` (1 GiB) · core
  `PutOptions.maxSize`, `WriteFileInput.maxSize` · fault code `too_large` → 413
- **Not** — the attachment count cap (at most 64 attachments per message or objective
  payload, enforced by the schema, unrelated to byte size) · a per-member or per-team
  quota (nothing totals bytes across entries; the limit applies to one upload) ·
  `MAX_PATH_LENGTH` / `MAX_SEGMENT_LENGTH` (1024 and 255 characters, limits on the
  path, not the file)
- **Verbs** — write, upload

### Raw body blob

A captured request or response body in the activity database: "one row per DISTINCT
body, keyed by sha256 of the original bytes. Bytes are gzipped at rest." It shares
only the sha256 idea with a file blob — no table, no database file, no directory, no
reclamation rule. Nothing deletes one outside `csuite prune-traces`, and that command
reclaims it by reachability: after the age-based deletes, a `NOT EXISTS` sweep drops
every row no `raw_exchange.hash` and no retained `gen_ai_inference.request_sha256` or
`response_sha256` still points at.

- **Names** — DB `raw_blob (hash, bytes, byte_length, stored_length, first_seen_at)`
  and its companion `raw_exchange`, both on the activity DB · core
  `PruneActivityResult.rawBlob` · CLI `csuite prune-traces` — "raw bodies  N
  exchange(s), M blob(s) collected" · docs [Trace pipeline](/docs/dev/trace-pipeline)
- **Not** — file blob (the virtual filesystem's bytes, refcounted and deleted eagerly)
  · reference-counted in the literal sense: `raw_blob` has no refcount column, and the
  module's "reference-counted, not time-bounded" means reclaimed by reachability
  rather than by age — one body backs many exchanges across many turns, and
  `first_seen_at` records when the bytes were first stored, not when they were last
  needed
- **Verbs** — append, get, prune

### Write collision strategy

What `POST /fs/write` does when the target path already exists: "`'error'` (default)
rejects, `'overwrite'` replaces in place, `'suffix'` writes to a deduped" path.
Resolution happens inside the metadata transaction, so two concurrent writers at one
path do not both succeed. The wire spelling is `collide` on every surface the server
reads; the `collision` parameter named in the REST reference is not read, and a
caller who sends it gets the `error` default and a 409 on an occupied path.

- **Names** — SDK `FsWriteCollisionStrategy`, `FsWriteCollisionSchema`, client field
  `collision` · core `WriteCollisionStrategy`, `WriteFileInput.collision`,
  `CopyByBlobRefInput.collision`, `dedupeBasename()` · route query param `collide` on
  `POST /fs/write` · CLI `--collide` · MCP `collide` on `fs_write`, `fs_upload`, and
  the local-file attachment object · SDK `FsWriteResponse.renamed`
- **Not** — env-name conflict (two configured rows resolving to one `envName` for one
  member — "an ERROR, never a precedence rule", surfaced as `env_taken`; it has no
  strategy and no default — see Environment namespace)
- **Values** — `collide`: `error` — reject · `overwrite` — replace the entry in place
  and drop the previous blob's refcount · `suffix` — write to a deduped basename and
  return `renamed: true`. `dedupeBasename` appends `-1`, `-2`, … before the extension:
  `foo.txt` → `foo-1.txt`. The `name (1).ext` example in `FsWriteCollisionStrategy`'s
  doc-comment is not what ships. The default is `error` on `fs_write`, `fs_upload`,
  `POST /fs/write` and `csuite fs put`, and `suffix` on the local-file attachment
  object `broadcast`, `send`, `channels_post` and `objectives_discuss` accept — one
  field name, two defaults, and the second is the one an agent hits without asking.
- **Verbs** — write, upload

### `canWrite`

The server's own answer to "may this viewer mutate this entry", sent on the entry so
a client does not rebuild the rule: "for objective namespace entries it *cannot*: the
owner is `obj:<id>` and the rule includes objective membership." Today it is attached
by the read projections only — `stat`, `list`, `listShared`, `listAllFiles` — so the
entries returned by `POST /fs/write`, `POST /fs/mkdir` and `POST /fs/mv` omit it, and
the store's own header claiming "every entry returned to a caller carries `canWrite`"
overstates what ships. *(defined in code only)*

- **Names** — SDK `FsEntry.canWrite`, `FsEntrySchema.canWrite` · core
  `withCapability()`, `canWrite()` · route responses of `GET /fs/ls`, `GET /fs/stat`,
  `GET /fs/shared`, `GET /fs/all`
- **Not** — `owner` (an objective-folder entry is owned by `obj:<id>` and writable by
  every member of that objective; the inference from `owner` is wrong there) ·
  permission leaf (`members.manage` is one input to the predicate, not the flag) ·
  file share (a share confers read and never write, so an entry a viewer reaches
  only through one carries `canWrite: false`) · `false` — `undefined` means "unknown,
  ask", which is exactly what the write routes return today

### Filesystem fault code

The `code` a `/fs/*` route returns beside its `error` message, and the value a client
branches on. Nine codes, each with a fixed HTTP status. The module comment listing
the mapping is a code short: it sends `exists`, `not_a_directory` and `is_a_directory`
to 409 and everything unnamed to 400, while the router also sends `not_empty` to 409.
*(defined in code only)*

- **Names** — core `FsErrorCode`, `FsError` · route body `{ error, code }` on every
  `/fs/*` failure
- **Not** — the REST reference's "common error codes" table, which is a different
  vocabulary for the same field: it spells the same conditions `bad_request`,
  `conflict` and `payload_too_large` where `/fs/*` sends `invalid_input`, `exists`
  and `too_large`, and it does not name this enum · `error` (the human-readable
  message on the same body; a caller never parses it — see Fault code) · an HTTP
  status alone (403 covers `forbidden` while 409 covers four different codes, so the
  status does not identify the failure) · `SecretsError`'s codes (`slug_taken`,
  `env_taken`, `no_kek`: a different store's vocabulary, sharing only the `code`
  field name)
- **Values** — `not_found` → 404 · `forbidden` → 403 · `too_large` → 413 · `exists`,
  `not_a_directory`, `is_a_directory`, `not_empty` → 409 · `invalid_input`,
  `corrupt` → 400
- **Verbs** — raise, map

## Topology and product surfaces

The parts a deployment is made of: the process that holds a team's state, the team
itself, the applications that wrap a UI around it, and the regions of that UI a
reader can point at. The distinction that matters most is that **broker**, **host**
and **platform** are three separate programs, and each of those three words gets
applied to one of the other two somewhere in this tree — the self-hosted guide calls
the platform "an identity broker", the web-ui README counts the platform as one of
two hosts, and the architecture page calls `csuite-core` "broker logic".

### CommandSuite

The product. `csuite` is its command, its npm scope-less package prefix, and the
name of the meta-package that installs the whole stack — `npm install -g csuite`
puts both `csuite` and `csuite-server` on `$PATH`. Capitalization carries the
distinction and never drifts: **CommandSuite** is the product, `csuite` is the
binary and the package prefix.

- **Names** — meta-package `csuite` (bins `csuite`, `csuite-server`) · `csuite-cli`
  (bin `csuite`) · `csuite-server` (bins `csuite-server`, `csuite-connect-platform`)
  · `csuite-sdk` (the wire contract) · `csuite-core` (runtime-neutral broker logic)
  · `csuite-web-ui` · `csuite-web-host` · docs
  [Getting started](/docs/getting-started) · [Architecture](/docs/dev/architecture)
- **Not** — Broker (the process `csuite-server` runs, not the package) · the `csuite`
  binary (installing the package `csuite` gets you the binary `csuite`; installing
  `csuite-cli` gets you the same binary and no server) · `csuite:` prefixes on
  stored keys and paths, which are namespaces rather than the product name

### Broker

The server process that holds one team's state — messages, objectives, files,
secrets and traces — and that every member connects to. One broker is authoritative
for exactly one team, and it refuses to boot when that team's row is missing: the
database outranks the config file, so a populated config pointed at a fresh database
is an error rather than a re-seed.

- **Names** — package and binary `csuite-server` · CLI `csuite serve` · library
  entry `runServer()`, handle `RunningServer` · docs
  [Glossary](/docs/concepts/glossary) · [Architecture](/docs/dev/architecture)
- **Not** — Presence broker (the class `Broker`, one component *inside* this
  process) · `csuite-core` (the runtime-neutral library it is built from, which the
  architecture page calls "broker logic") · Platform (a separate hosted service the
  self-hosted guide also calls "an identity broker") · Host (the application that
  serves the team UI in front of it) · bind address (`--host`, `CSUITE_HOST`,
  `RunningServer.host` — an interface this process listens on)
- **Verbs** — serve
- **Decided** — D31: keep `Broker` as the class identifier and name its concept
  "presence broker" in prose, over renaming the class to `PresenceBroker`
  (2026-09-03)

### Presence broker

"The runtime-agnostic core of csuite. Ties the presence registry to an event log and
handles the push fanout. Knows nothing about HTTP, MCP, or persistence; runtime
adapters layer those on top." One component the broker process constructs at boot,
beside the stores, the listeners and the route table. *(defined in code only)*

- **Names** — core class `Broker`, options `BrokerOptions`, both exported from
  `csuite-core`
- **Not** — Broker (the process that instantiates it — in prose "the broker" always
  means the process, never this class) · presence registry (`PresenceRegistry`, the
  live-connection table this class ties to the event log)

### Team

The top-level unit a broker controls: a name plus the standing context every member
inherits, stored as a single `CHECK (id = 1)` row.

- **Names** — SDK `Team` · DB `team` (singleton `id = 1`) · route `GET /team`,
  `PATCH /team` · CLI `csuite team get`, `csuite team set` · MCP `team_get`,
  `team_update` · UI eyebrow "Team" above the team name on Team Home
- **Not** — Team slug (a URL token the host supplies; it is not this row and is not
  derived from it) · team process (the team's authored working document, behind
  `team_process_get`, `team_process_history`, `team_process_write`) · team status
  (the read-only `members.manage` operability report at `GET /team/status`, a
  projection over presence and objectives) · `Team.permissionPresets` (a preset-era
  compatibility field current servers omit, whatever `docs/dev/rest-api.mdx` still
  promises) · `TeamStore` in `csuite-web-ui` (an unexported placeholder contract
  whose `createTeamStore` throws; the real store is `TeamStore` in `csuite-core`) ·
  session context (`Team.context` is durable team configuration, not an agent's
  conversation)
- **Verbs** — get · update

### Team slug

An opaque URL-scope token a host hands to `<TeamShell>`, which then prefixes every
in-shell URL with `/t/<slug>/`. It originates on the host side: the platform mints
it, returns it in the registration response, and `csuite-connect-platform` prints
`<platform>/t/<slug>` as where to open the team. Single-team deployments leave it
unset and URLs live at the origin root. *(defined in code only)*

- **Names** — web-ui prop `teamSlug` · `setRouterTeamSlug`, `getRouterTeamSlug` ·
  route field `Route.team` · URL prefix `/t/:team` · `teamSlug` on the platform's
  `authorized` registration response
- **Not** — Team (the broker's single row — "one deployment = one team" scopes a
  broker, while `/t/:team` scopes a host fronting several) · `TeamIdentity.slug` (a
  slug the UI computes locally with `slugify(team.name)`; nothing wires it into
  `setRouterTeamSlug`, so it is not the string in the URL, whatever its "Used for URL
  prefixes" comment claims) · slug (the entity identifier used for channels, tool
  sources, secrets and variables)

### Host

An application that authenticates a viewer and mounts `<TeamShell>`. "The host owns
auth and any chrome *around* the shell; the shell owns everything *inside*."

- **Names** — package `csuite-web-host` — the reference host, a TOTP auth gate and
  PWA served by the broker · `TeamShellProps` (`client`, `identity`, `onSignOut`,
  `onUnauthorized`, `teamSlug`, `leftRail`, `onTeamSettings`) — the whole contract a
  host implements · docs [Architecture](/docs/dev/architecture) ·
  `packages/web-ui/README.md`
- **Not** — Platform (the commercial hosted control plane; it is a host, but call it
  the platform) · Broker (the process the host authenticates against) · capture host
  (`CaptureHost`, the runner-side capture sink) · bind address (`--host`,
  `CSUITE_HOST`, `RunningServer.host`) · a JavaScript runtime (the runtime-neutral
  charter's "every non-Node host") · self-hosted (an adjective about who operates the
  broker, not a noun for this application)

### Platform

An external hosted control plane a self-hosted broker can optionally pair with. It
issues a device-flow pairing code and receives a member binding confirmed on the
broker, in exchange for a JWT verification block; the broker stays authoritative for
team state and member identity.

- **Names** — binary `csuite-connect-platform` · flags `--url <hosted-url>`,
  `--member-name <name>`, `--platform <url>` · routes `GET /setup/connect-platform`,
  `POST /platform-connect/bind`, `GET /platform-connect/lookup` · config overlay
  `<config>.platform.json` (`platformOverlayPathFor`) · docs
  [Connect a self-hosted server to a platform](/docs/self-hosted-connect)
- **Not** — Host (the general contract of mounting the shell; the platform is one
  host among others) · Broker (your server; the guide's phrase "identity broker" is
  the single place that word means something other than your own process) · Platform
  registration (the device flow that binds the two — the act, not the actor) ·
  "platform-defined external tools" in tool-source prose, which means the
  deployment's admin · "the platform" in contributor prose about merges, which means
  GitHub
- **Verbs** — connect · bind

### Server config file (`csuite.json`)

The slim boot-time file holding only the infrastructure knobs that must exist before
SQLite can open; team identity, members and credentials live in the database and
change through the API, CLI and MCP. Its path resolves as `$CSUITE_CONFIG_PATH`, then
`./csuite.json` when that file already exists, then `./csuite/csuite.json` — so a
fresh setup in a clean directory writes the third.

- **Names** — file `csuite.json` · type `ServerConfig` · schema `ServerConfigSchema`
  · resolver `defaultConfigPath` · env `CSUITE_CONFIG_PATH` · CLI `csuite setup`,
  `csuite serve --config-path` · docs [Config files](/docs/reference/config)
- **Not** — Team (name and context are rows in the `team` table) · member (roster
  rows are in the database; `tokenHash` and `totpSecret` never appear in this file) ·
  platform overlay (`<config>.platform.json`, a sibling file holding only the JWT
  block the platform minted) · "team config file" (a retired label for this same
  path, surviving in a few code comments and the architecture page's package table,
  that describes a superseded format)
- **Values** — `dbPath` `activityDbPath` `filesRoot` `https` `webPush` `jwt` `files`,
  plus the `_comment` the file writes about itself — the schema is non-strict, so a
  `team` or `members` block written into this file is stripped without an error

### Main database

The SQLite file a broker opens first and treats as authoritative: team and members,
tokens, sessions, enrollments, the event log, message deliveries, objectives,
channels, the team process, tool sources, secrets, variables, push subscriptions,
notifications and file metadata. Everything a member changes through the API lands
here, on one connection.

- **Names** — config `dbPath` · env `CSUITE_DB_PATH` · CLI `csuite serve --db` ·
  default `csuite.db` beside the config file · docs
  [Environment variables](/docs/reference/env-vars)
- **Not** — Activity database (the second file, dedicated to capture, so the heaviest
  write path never contends on this file's write lock) · `filesRoot` (blob bytes on
  disk; this database holds only their metadata) · server config file (it names this
  path and holds none of this state)

### Route

An HTTP path the broker serves, and the word for one wherever prose reaches for
"endpoint". The registry is authored once in `csuite-sdk` and shared by the broker and
every client, so a path has a single spelling. *(defined in code only)*

- **Names** — SDK `PATHS` (`packages/sdk/src/protocol.ts`), `TOOL_SOURCE_PATHS`,
  `FS_PATHS` · core the route table in `packages/core/src/app.ts` · docs
  [REST API](/docs/dev/rest-api)
- **Not** — Notification endpoint (the configured receiver behind `POST /hooks/:slug`:
  the REST reference's "Every HTTP endpoint the broker exposes" means routes, and its
  "Management endpoints are dual-auth" two pages later means those records) · Client
  route (the web UI's typed view state, which diverges from these paths on purpose) ·
  Push subscription (whose `endpoint` field is a browser push-service URL)

### TeamShell

The single public entry point of `csuite-web-ui` and the whole in-team surface: nav
column, header, the chat, roster, objectives, files and members panels, the command
palette, and the live-subscription lifecycle.

- **Names** — exports `TeamShell`, `TeamShellProps` from `csuite-web-ui` · docs
  `packages/web-ui/README.md`
- **Not** — `AppShell` (the internal four-slot layout — `leftRail`, `nav`, `main`,
  `drawer` under a `header`; call it the app layout in prose) · the SPA document
  (`index.html`, what the broker serves when a request's `Accept` prefers HTML over
  JSON) · `embeddedShell` (a mode flag, not a shell: it is true exactly when the host
  passes `leftRail`, which is also when the nav footer's Account button gives way to
  a host-owned "Team settings" button) · `RouteModal` (the overlay frame around modal
  routes) · Host (what wraps this)

### Client route

The typed description of what is on screen, derived from the URL — the URL is the
single source of truth for view state, and every in-app navigation goes through
`navigate(route)`. *(defined in code only)*

- **Names** — web-ui `Route` (20 kinds) · `parseRoute` · `formatRoute` · `navigate` ·
  `currentRoute`
- **Not** — `View` (19 kinds, a lossy projection of `Route` kept so older components
  need not parse URLs: it folds `thread-channel` and `thread-dm` into one `thread`
  kind and renames `home` to `overview` — a deprecated shim, not a second vocabulary)
  · Route (the broker's HTTP path, registered in `csuite-sdk`'s `PATHS`; the two
  diverge on purpose — the client route is `/tools` where the API path is
  `/tool-sources`)

### Team Home

The landing screen at `/`: the team's name and context, the team process, four stat
tiles (MEMBERS, ONLINE, ACTIVE OBJECTIVES, BLOCKED), and the roster.

- **Names** — route kind `home` · component `TeamHome` · UI nav label "Home" · UI
  eyebrow "Team"
- **Not** — `View.kind = 'overview'` (the deprecated `View` shim's name for this same
  screen; it goes when `View` goes) · `ProfileTab` `'overview'` (the default tab on a
  *member's* profile, an unrelated screen) · `RosterPanel` (a dead name; the section
  it owned is now the "Roster" eyebrow inside `TeamHome`)

### Nav column

The one left column: team header, the Home, Inbox, Objectives and Files items, the
permission-gated Members, Tools, Environment and Notifications items, the chat list,
and a footer holding the Account button — or, in embedded mode, the host's "Team
settings" button. Below 900px the same column slides in over the content.

- **Names** — component `NavColumn` · `AppShell` slot `nav` · CSS class `.nav-drawer`
  · signals `isSidebarOpen`, `openSidebar`, `closeSidebar` — public API of
  `csuite-web-ui`
- **Not** — "sidebar" (the same column under an older name that survives only in
  those exported signal names and three test headings) · nav drawer (this column in
  its below-900px slide-in state, not a second component) · Inspector (the right-hand
  panel, which `AppShell` also calls `drawer`) · `ContextDrawer` (a right-side
  primitive that ships unused) · user chip (the avatar-and-name footer this column
  used to carry; the gear-iconed Account button replaced it)

### Inspector

The right-hand activity panel beside a DM thread — a fixed member of the layout row
at 1100px and wider, an overlay that opens on demand below that. It owns the
activity-stream subscription for the agent in focus, which is why it mounts on DM
threads only: a channel spans many members and has no single agent to inspect.

- **Names** — component `ActivityInspector` · `AppShell` prop `drawer` · signals
  `isInspectorOpen`, `openInspector`, `closeInspector`, `toggleInspector` — public
  API of `csuite-web-ui` · UI "Activity inspector" (the thread-head toggle's tooltip)
  and `Activity for <member>` (the panel's label)
- **Not** — nav drawer (the left column's below-900px state; `AppShell` uses "drawer"
  for both, which is why the prop name misleads) · `ContextDrawer` (an unused
  right-side primitive, never this panel) · `AgentTimeline` (the same feed body drawn
  as a card on a member's profile) · activity stream (the durable record this panel
  renders)

### Command palette

The ⌘K launcher over members, objectives, channels and DMs, plus a short list of
named actions. Activating a row calls the same `select*` navigation wrappers the nav
column uses, so the palette adds no routing of its own.

- **Names** — component `CommandPalette` (internal to `csuite-web-ui`, not exported)
  · signals `paletteOpen`, `paletteQuery` · shortcut ⌘K / Ctrl-K · UI placeholder
  "Jump to member, objective, thread…"
- **Not** — "the Helm JUMP" (the design system's name for this component, cited once
  in the source and defined nowhere in this tree; Helm is the external design system
  from `@the-efficacious/brand`) · Inspector (a panel, not a launcher) · Client route
  (the palette navigates through the same `navigate`; it is not a second view
  vocabulary)
- **Values** — `PaletteItem.kind`: `member` `objective` `thread-channel` `thread-dm`
  `action`

### Port conformance suites

The broker's port-contract suites for injected drivers, published as the subpath export
`csuite-core/conformance`. A host that supplies its own SQL driver, blob store or field
cipher runs these to prove the port is honored — they are a published API surface, not a
contributor convenience.

- **Names** — subpath export `csuite-core/conformance`
  (`packages/core/package.json:32-35`) · `sqlDriverConformance`, `blobStoreConformance`,
  `fieldCipherConformance`, `brokerAppConformance`
  (`packages/core/src/conformance/index.ts:18-21`) · docs
  [Conformance](/docs/dev/conformance)
- **Not** — Runner conformance suite (contributor-side: the adapter test kit a new runner
  author must pass, `packages/cli/test/runtime/conformance/kit.ts`, which ships in no
  package) · Doctor and Preflight (an operator's local environment checks, not a test
  contract) · Protocol capability (what a peer advertises at runtime, not what a driver
  is proved to honor) · Host (the process that injects the drivers these suites test)

### Quickstart

The demo path: seeds one objective against a chosen assignee and opens the web UI. It
creates real rows on the running broker, not a sandbox, and health-checks the broker
first.

- **Names** — CLI `csuite quickstart [--skip-browser] [--assignee <name>]`
  (`packages/cli/src/index.ts:79`, dispatched at `:282`), `runQuickstartCommand`,
  `QuickstartError` (`packages/cli/src/commands/quickstart.ts`) · docs
  [CLI reference](/docs/reference/cli)
- **Not** — `csuite setup` (the first-run wizard that creates the team config, the first
  member and its TOTP enrollment; quickstart needs a team to already exist) · Objective
  (the demo objective is an ordinary one, with an ordinary assignee and outcome, and
  survives the demo) · Team Home (the page it opens)
- **Verbs** — seed, open

## Identifiers and naming

Every record here is addressed twice: by something a machine minted and by something a
person typed. The two are never interchangeable, and the rule that keeps them apart is the
one this family exists to state — an entity's **own** identifier is the bare field `id` or
`slug`, and a reference to a **different** entity is always qualified (`objectiveId`,
`endpointSlug`, `member_name`, `identity_id`). The type layer follows that rule without
exception; the MCP tool-argument layer, where every argument is foreign by definition,
abandons it, which is why an agent has to learn four spellings for "which one" — `id` on
objectives, `slug` on the four registries, `channel` on channels, `deliveryId` on delivery
receipts. The third identifier grammar, the member name, is defined below under
[Member name](#member-name); it is not a slug, and the docs are the only surface that
calls it one.

### Slug

The human-typed, URL-facing identifier of a record inside one store: lowercase kebab, 1–32
characters, unique per store and never across stores. *"Identified by an opaque immutable
`id`; addressed in URLs and the UI by a mutable `slug`."* Six subjects carry one — channel,
tool source, secret, variable, notification endpoint, notification profile — and only a
channel's may be changed, because on every other subject a durable reference rides on it:
the `secret:<slug>` thread tag, the `/hooks/<slug>` ingress URL, the `hook:<slug>` sender
identity, the `<slug>__<tool>` MCP tool name.

- **Names** — SDK `ChannelSlugSchema`, `ToolSourceSlugSchema`, `SecretSlugSchema`
  (`= ToolSourceSlugSchema`), `NotificationSlugSchema` (`= ToolSourceSlugSchema`) · core
  `validateSlug` (`channels.ts`), `validateSourceSlug` (`tool-sources/store.ts`),
  `validateVariableSlug` (`variables.ts`) · routes `/channels/:slug`, `/tool-sources/:slug`,
  `/secrets/:slug`, `/variables/:slug`, `/notifications/endpoints/:slug`, `/hooks/:slug` ·
  foreign reference SDK `NotificationDelivery.endpointSlug` · CLI `slug` positional
  (`requireSlug`) on `csuite tools`, `csuite secrets`, `csuite variables`,
  `csuite notifications` · MCP argument `slug`, except on channels, where the same thing is
  called `channel` · web UI `slug` form field on the four create panels · docs
  [Channels](/docs/concepts/channels), [Tool sources](/docs/concepts/tool-sources),
  [Secrets](/docs/concepts/secrets), [Variables](/docs/concepts/variables)
- **Not** — Id (opaque and machine-minted; a channel message pins `data.thread = 'chan:<id>'`
  rather than the slug precisely so a rename never orphans history) · Member name (a
  different grammar — `^[a-zA-Z0-9._-]+$`, 1–128 characters, mixed case, dots and
  underscores allowed — and the one place the two words collide is
  [Channels](/docs/concepts/channels), which calls a channel's slug its "name") · envName
  (the entry's *destination*, unique per member across secrets and variables together, where
  a slug is unique only inside its own store) · Team slug (`slugify(team.name)` computed in
  the browser for the `/t/:team` URL prefix; nothing stores it) · Foreign identity and
  Format prefix, which are shapes a *value* takes, not identifiers of a record
- **Values** — `^[a-z0-9](?:[a-z0-9]|-(?!-))*[a-z0-9]$|^[a-z0-9]$`, 1–32 characters. That
  grammar is written out four times: twice in zod (`ChannelSlugSchema`,
  `ToolSourceSlugSchema`) and twice on the server (`validateSlug`, `validateSourceSlug`),
  differing only in the error class thrown. `validateVariableSlug` is a fifth copy that
  disagrees — 64 characters and `^[a-z0-9][a-z0-9-]*$`, which admits `foo--bar` and `foo-` —
  under a doc-comment claiming the opposite: *"Slug grammar, matching the secrets store's"*.
  The secrets store calls `validateSourceSlug`, the strict one, and `CreateVariableRequest`
  validates against `SecretSlugSchema`, also the strict one, so the lenient validator governs
  only writers that reach the store without the wire layer
- **Verbs** — `get`, `update`, `delete`, `bind` / `unbind` address a record by it. Only a
  channel's is itself updatable (`RenameChannelRequestSchema`, `ChannelAuditAction`'s
  `rename`); the REST reference says "Slug is immutable" on each of the other four `PATCH`
  rows

### Id

The opaque identifier a store mints for a record. Never typed by a person, never reused,
never rewritten. *"A timestamp is not an identity — create emits two events in the same
millisecond — so anything naming a specific event (a correction) uses this."*

- **Names** — SDK `Message.id` · `ObjectiveEvent.id` · `TokenInfo.id` ·
  `NotificationDelivery.id` · route `POST /notifications/deliveries/:id/replay` ·
  MCP `objectives_view` argument `id` · CLI `csuite notifications replay <deliveryId>`
- **Not** — Slug (the human-typed, URL-facing identifier, unique per store and not across
  stores) · `identityId` (what survives a member name being reused, not a record's key) ·
  Source id (`ActivityEvent.sourceId`, an idempotency key naming a *foreign* record the
  runner read) · the correlation ids `requestId`, `promptId`, `turnId`, `responseId`, which
  name a moment inside one exchange rather than a stored row
- **Values** — bare `crypto.randomUUID()` for channels, secrets, tool sources, notification
  endpoints, profiles, deliveries and messages · `obj-<ms36>-<counter36>` for objectives ·
  `ev-<uuid>` for objective events · `var_<ms36>_<rand36>` for variables — three prefix
  styles and two separators across one concept, with `var_` the odd one out against its
  structural twin `secrets`, which uses a bare UUID
- **Verbs** — `get`, `update`, `delete` and the named lifecycle verbs (`replay`, `complete`,
  `cancel`) address a record by it. Nothing ever rewrites the id itself; `list` takes a
  cursor, not an id

### Member name

The member's handle: `^[a-zA-Z0-9._-]+$`, 1 to 128 characters, mixed case, chosen by
whoever creates the seat. It is the addressing key everywhere — on the wire, in routes,
in the JWT `member` claim, as a message's `from` and `to`, as the `member_name` column on
every table that references a member, and as the member's filesystem home segment. A slug
is not a name: different grammar, no case folding, and a different uniqueness scope.

- **Names** — SDK `NameSchema` (`packages/sdk/src/schemas.ts:47-51`), `Member.name`,
  `Teammate.name`, `Message.from` / `.to` · DB `members.name`, and `member_name` on every
  referencing table · route `/members/:name`, `/members/:name/activity`, `?with=<name>` ·
  JWT claim `member` · CLI `csuite push --agent <name>`, `csuite enroll --member <name>` ·
  MCP arguments `member`, `to`, `assignee` · UI the roster handle · docs
  [Members](/docs/concepts/members)
- **Not** — Slug (lowercase kebab, 1 to 32 characters, one store's address for one record;
  [Channels](/docs/concepts/channels) calls a channel's slug its "name" and is the one
  place the two words collide) · Id (opaque and machine-minted) · Identity id
  (`Teammate.identityId` — "Names are display/lookup handles and may be explicitly reused
  after departure; this id never is"; declared optional and emitted by nothing) · Role
  title (a display label, not a handle) · Label (a token's nickname) · Foreign identity
  (`hook:<slug>`, `csuite` — values in a `from` field shaped so they cannot be one of
  these)
- **Values** — `^[a-zA-Z0-9._-]+$`, 1 to 128 characters. The colon is excluded, which is
  the whole mechanism behind the foreign-identity forms. Nothing reserves `csuite` or
  `__system__`, so both are legal member names today.
- **Verbs** — a member is addressed by it; no surface offers a rename

### Thread tag

The value on `Message.data.thread` that says which conversation a message belongs to — the
wire half of the [thread key](#thread-key), and the only half the broker writes. On a
durable row written before the `recipients` column existed, `isScopedThreadTag` reads it to
decide whether the row's audience was narrower than the team. Three of its six prefixes are
exported constants and three are private, and every writing site in `app.ts` builds the tag
from an inline string literal instead of importing any of them.

- **Names** — SDK wire `Message.data.thread` · core `CHANNEL_THREAD_PREFIX`,
  `SECRET_THREAD_PREFIX`, `OBJECTIVE_THREAD_PREFIX`, `channelThreadTag()`,
  `isScopedThreadTag()`, and the private `TOOL_THREAD_PREFIX`, `VARIABLE_THREAD_PREFIX`,
  `NOTIFICATION_THREAD_PREFIX` · raw SQL `json_extract(data,'$.thread') NOT LIKE 'hook:%'`
  in `sqlite-event-log.ts`, a fourth copy of the vocabulary · docs
  [MCP tools](/docs/dev/mcp-tools), which carries the best sentence in the tree on the
  slug/id split: *"The channel is addressed by slug (mutable, URL-facing), but the runner
  resolves slug → id and pins `data.thread = 'chan:<id>'`"*
- **Not** — the thread tag is not the only pre-`recipients` scope signal: `isLegacyScopedEvent`
  also treats `data.kind` of `instructions` or `context_control` as scoped, because those
  pushes carry a recipient list and no tag · Thread key (the browser's grouping space, which
  contains every value here plus `dm:<name>` and `primary`, neither of which the wire ever
  sends, and in which `general` is spelled `primary`) · Foreign identity (the same
  `hook:<slug>` and `obj:<id>` spellings in a *sender* or *owner* field, where they say who
  acted rather than which conversation)
- **Values** — `chan:<id>` · `obj:<id>` · `secret:<slug>` · `tool:<slug>` ·
  `variable:<slug>` · `hook:<slug>` — every one except `chan:general` marks the row as
  scoped, because the general channel's membership is implicit

### Foreign identity

A value in a field that otherwise holds a member name, shaped so it cannot be one.
*"Member names can't contain `:`, so the sender identity is collision-free and visibly
foreign. That, plus the non-templatable wrap composed in render.ts, is the injection
boundary: external content never arrives looking like a teammate."*

- **Names** — SDK wire `Message.from` · core `hook:<slug>` on `PushContext.from`
  (`notifications/dispatcher.ts`) · `OBJECTIVE_OWNER_PREFIX` = `obj:` on
  `fs_entries.owner` · `legacy:<name>` on the delivery-ledger owner (`broker.ts`)
- **Not** — Thread tag (`hook:<slug>` and `obj:<id>` again, on `data.thread`, naming a
  conversation) · Format prefix (`enc-v1:`, `sha256:` — an encoding tag on a value, not a
  reference to an entity) · System actor, the one identity in the product that denotes an
  actor and does *not* use this form, which is what D33 changes · the colon inside enum
  values such as `'self-signed:fresh'` and `agent:builtin:general-purpose`, which is neither

### Format prefix

A literal that says how the rest of a string is encoded or which version produced it. It
qualifies the value it is glued to; it does not point at another record.
`ENCRYPTED_FIELD_PREFIX` is currently declared twice, in `csuite-core/field-crypto.ts` and
again in `apps/server/src/kek.ts`, each beside its own `encryptField` / `decryptField` pair
described as byte-compatible with the other, and no test asserts the two constants agree —
both `kek.test.ts` and `vapid-at-rest.test.ts` import the server copy.

- **Names** — core `ENCRYPTED_FIELD_PREFIX` = `enc-v1:` · core `TOKEN_HASH_PREFIX` =
  `sha256:` · SDK `BEARER_TOKEN_PATTERN` for `csuite_<43 base64url>`, minted server-side as
  `TOKEN_PREFIX` · core `DEVICE_CODE_PREFIX` = `csuite-dc_`
- **Not** — Foreign identity (`hook:<slug>`, `obj:<id>` — a reference to an entity, in a
  field that would otherwise hold a member name) · Thread tag (a conversation key) · Slug
  or Id, neither of which carries a version marker
- **Values** — `enc-v1:<iv>:<tag>:<ciphertext>`, all base64url · `sha256:<hex digest>` ·
  `csuite_` on a bearer token · `csuite-dc_` on a device code

### Kind

The reserved, layer-local name for a discriminator field. `kind` means nothing on its own:
its vocabulary is named by the type that encloses it, and that named type — `ActivityKind`,
`ToolSourceKind` — is the term to use in prose, in a review comment and in a bug report.
Nothing is gained by renaming thirty fields, and the discriminated-union ergonomics of a
bare `kind` are real; what is gained is that no one ever writes "the kind field" and expects
to be understood.

- **Names** — SDK `ActivityKind` · `InstructionBlockKind` · `ToolSourceKind` ·
  `ToolCredentialKind` · `NotificationAuthKind` · `ObjectiveEventKind` ·
  `ClientIdentity.kind` · `FsEntry.kind` · `Teammate.kind` · web-ui `Route.kind`,
  `View.kind`, `InboxItem.kind`, `PaletteItem.kind` · query `?kind=` on
  `GET /members/:name/activity`, repeatable and validated against `ActivityKindSchema`
- **Not** — Status (a lifecycle or outcome value, not a type) · `TelemetryRecordRow.signal`,
  `ApproveEnrollmentRequest.mode`, `TokenInfo.origin`, `ChannelAuditEntry.action`,
  `ExecutorState.state` and `MessageDisposition` — `kind` is the most common discriminator
  name, not the only one, and none of these should be renamed to it ·
  `?kind=request|response` on `GET /members/:name/genai/:id/raw`, which is not a
  discriminator at all: it selects which half of one captured exchange to return, and it
  shares a query-string surface with the activity route's `?kind=`, where the same parameter
  name carries `ActivityKind`
- **Values** — one closed set per enclosing type; the casing of the values says which layer
  the vocabulary belongs to (see Casing convention)

### Status

A lifecycle or outcome value, always a string from a closed set. Where an HTTP response
code is meant the field holds a number, and under the `kind`-versus-`status` rule that field
may not be called `status` — `AnthropicMessagesEntry.response.status` (`number | null`) and
`ActivityAuthState.status` (the literal `401`) are the two places the word names an integer
today, and both are to become `httpStatus`.

- **Names** — SDK `ObjectiveStatus` · `NotificationDeliveryStatus` ·
  `DeliveryReport.acknowledgement.status` · core `EnrollmentStatus` · CLI `CheckStatus` ·
  docs [REST API](/docs/dev/rest-api)
- **Not** — Work state, the live `idle` / `working` / `blocked` signal beside a member on
  the roster, which is a momentary reading and not a lifecycle · the delivery ledger's
  `state` (`pending` `accepted` `acted` `handled` `refused` `unreported`) and
  `ExecutorState.state` (`ready` `degraded` `unreported`), deliberately not called `status`
  so a row's lifecycle and its executor's condition never collide · Kind (which type of
  thing it is)
- **Values** — `ObjectiveStatus`: `active` `blocked` `done` `cancelled` ·
  acknowledgement: `pending` `settled` `refused` `unreported` · `EnrollmentStatus`:
  `pending` `approved` `rejected` · `CheckStatus`: `PASS` `WARN` `FAIL`, uppercase because
  it is CLI operator output and nothing else

### Label

A human-written display string, and a homonym: on the wire it is a bearer token's nickname —
*"each with a label that names what it's for ('laptop', 'ci-runner', 'prod-vm')"* — while in
the web UI it is the caption on a permission leaf or a create-form preset. The repository
already has a word for the second sense and uses it elsewhere.

- **Names** — SDK `TokenInfo.label` · `DeviceAuthorizationRequest.labelHint` ·
  `PendingEnrollment.labelHint` · `ApproveEnrollmentRequest.label` · web-host `Enroll.tsx`
  `FormState.label` · web-ui `PermissionMeta.label`, `PermissionTemplate.label`
- **Not** — `displayName`, the repository's existing word for a human-facing name
  (`ToolSource.displayName`, `NotificationEndpoint.displayName`), which is what the two
  web-ui fields should be called · Member name (an identity handle, not a caption) · Slug
  (typed once, addressed in URLs, and on every subject but a channel immutable) ·
  `label: 'legacy'` on a migrated token row, where the word is a stored value rather than a
  nickname · the TLS store's `label`, a short provenance tag for logs

### Casing convention

Which case an identifier is written in tells you which layer owns it. The rule holds across
every enum, column and parameter in the tree and is written down nowhere else, which is why
each new surface has had to guess.

- **Names** — DB `raw_exchange(member_name, request_id, prompt_id, session_id, query_source,
  agent_name, event_ts)` ↔ core `RawBodyEnvelope{requestId, promptId, sessionId, querySource,
  agentName, eventTs}` and `AppendBodyInput.memberName` · query `if_offline` ↔ SDK
  `NotificationOverrides.ifOffline` ↔ CLI `--if-offline` · SDK `PERMISSIONS` ·
  web-ui `Route.kind` · CLI `CheckStatus`
- **Not** — the OTEL attribute keys the capture layer ingests (`gen_ai.response.id`,
  `agent.name`): externally imposed spellings passing through, not csuite names, and not
  evidence of drift · Query parameter (the rule that a parameter's *name* is snake_case,
  which is one clause of this and owned by wire conventions) · the values a field carries,
  whose casing is a separate rule with the same layer signal
- **Values** — `snake_case`: enum values and query parameters that cross the wire, and every
  DB column · `camelCase`: TypeScript fields and JSON request bodies · `kebab-case`:
  client-only view state · `UPPERCASE`: CLI operator output only · `dotted.lower`:
  permission leaves · `--kebab-flag`: CLI flags. Four camelCase query parameters predate the
  rule and are the exceptions to sweep — `parentOrigin`, `stalledMs`, `clientKind` and
  `clientVersion`

## Completeness diagnostics

The broker detects, retains and counts failures in data it claims to be complete about —
a body it could not read, an append that did not land, a record it skipped. This family is
that subsystem and nothing else: three other things in the repository are called
"diagnostics", and the runner's "diagnostics only" means the opposite — a value logged for
debugging that nobody keeps.

### Completeness diagnostics

A store of failures the product detected in data it claims completeness over: data that
was lost, truncated, or never stored. An operation that failed under no completeness claim
— a push that did not send, a websocket that dropped, an auth rejection — is logged and
not retained. *(defined in code only)*

- **Names** — core `DiagnosticStore` (`packages/core/src/diagnostics.ts:547`), `createDiagnosticStore` (`:651`), module title "Retained completeness diagnostics" (`:2`) · broker option `createApp({ diagnostics })` (`packages/core/src/app.ts:340-344`), constructed at `apps/server/src/run.ts:526` over the activity database (`run.ts:516`) · DB `diagnostic_event` `diagnostic_bucket` `diagnostic_state` `diagnostic_meta` (`diagnostics.ts:596-630`) · UI "the diagnostics store" (`packages/web-ui/src/components/MemberProfile.tsx:193`)
- **Not** — the doctor preflight (`csuite <runner> --doctor`, spelled `csuite claude --doctor` for the back-compat entry point; `runAgentDoctor` at `packages/cli/src/commands/doctor.ts:95`: local environment checks reporting `PASS`/`WARN`/`FAIL`, retained nowhere) · the runner's "diagnostics only" (a marker for a value logged for debugging and deliberately not kept — `packages/cli/src/runtime/agents/claude-sink.ts:67`, `packages/cli/src/runtime/restart.ts:71`) · trace retention, `csuite prune-traces` (an operator-driven age cutoff over `member_activity`, `gen_ai_inference`, `telemetry`, `raw_exchange` and `raw_blob`, which touches none of these four tables)
- **Verbs** — `emit` writes · `unresolved`, `query`, `health`, `coverageFloor` read · `sweep` folds

### Completeness failure site

A place in broker code where the product detects that data it claims completeness over was
lost, truncated, or never stored. Every such site is registered as a diagnostic cause. An
operational log site — a push that did not send, a websocket that dropped, an auth
rejection — writes its stderr line and stops there. This split is the membership rule for
the whole family. *(defined in code only)*

- **Names** — core prose "SCOPE, from the reconciled census" and the rule itself (`packages/core/src/diagnostics.ts:14-24`) · the guard `apps/server/test/diagnostics-census.test.ts`, with `CAPTURE_MODULES` (`:49`, modules whose every diagnostic is a completeness claim), `REGISTERED` (`:57`), `APP_IN_SCOPE` (`:75`), `countAllSites()` (`:281`) and `TOTAL_SITES` (`:254`)
- **Not** — a runner-side loss (`otlp-relay`'s uncaptured bytes and the runner's queue evictions happen on the agent's machine, where this store cannot reach; the module names the cost — capture health then sees fewer markers and reads healthier, `diagnostics.ts:26-33`) · the module's own counts, which are a hand census that has drifted: "All 50 broker log sites" (`:6`), "50 / 21 / 29" (`:16-18`), "The 21 in-scope sites" on the emitter interface (`:463`) and "The 21 typed methods" on the store interface (`:549`). The guard measures 79 sites (`TOTAL_SITES`) and 25 causes, and the emitter carries 22 failure methods and 8 recovery methods. Cite the guard, never the prose numbers.
- **Verbs** — a site is *registered* (it earns a cause); a log line is *classified* — completeness claim, or operational

### Diagnostic emitter

The write surface: one typed method per completeness-failure site, plus one per observed
recovery. Each takes raw inputs — a path, a thrown value, a hash — and owns the conversion
to safe fields, so a call site never decides what is retainable. *(defined in code only)*

- **Names** — core `DiagnosticEmitter` (`packages/core/src/diagnostics.ts:493-545`), reached as `DiagnosticStore.emit` (`:553`) · capture-store option `diagnostics?: DiagnosticEmitter` (`packages/core/src/genai-store.ts:112`, `packages/core/src/telemetry-store.ts:124`, `packages/core/src/genai-capture.ts:57`, `apps/server/src/raw-body-store.ts:123`), held as the field `diag` (`genai-store.ts:143`, `telemetry-store.ts:149`, `raw-body-store.ts:156`)
- **Not** — the completeness diagnostics store (`createApp`'s option is spelled `diagnostics` too and is the whole store — `unresolved`, `query`, `sweep`, `health`, `coverageFloor`; the capture stores' option of that name is write-only) · generic `record` and `resolve`, which are off `DiagnosticStore` by construction — "THERE WERE THREE DOORS TO THE SAME ROOM" (`:482-486`), compile-checked at `apps/server/test/diagnostics-boundary.test-d.ts`
- **Verbs** — one method per site, named for what failed or for the success that heals it

### Diagnostic cause

The closed vocabulary of what failed: 25 dotted codes, one per registered site, namespaced
by the module that emits them. A cause owns its policy — health mode, attribution and the
fields it may carry — so the call site cannot pick them, and cause ids are persisted and
never renamed. *(defined in code only)*

- **Names** — core `DIAGNOSTIC_CAUSES` (`packages/core/src/diagnostics.ts:81-115`), `DiagnosticCause` (`:121`), `CAUSE_SPEC` (`:188-254`), `causeSpec()` (`:256`) · DB `diagnostic_event.cause`, `diagnostic_bucket.cause`, `diagnostic_state.cause`
- **Not** — a fault code shown to a caller (a cause never crosses the wire; the wire carries a count) · `errorCode` inside safe fields (a whitelist classification of a thrown value: `ENOENT`, `EACCES`, else `unclassified`) · a live enum on read — historical rows keep retired causes, because "they are the record of what happened" (`:666-668`)
- **Values** — namespaces `correlator.*` (9) `rawstore.*` (2) `genaistore.*` (2) `telemetrystore.*` (2) `otlp.*` (3) `codex.*` (1) `activity.*` (1) `toolinvoke.*` (1) `enrollment.*` (1) — and `retention.*` (3), the store's own losses rather than another subsystem's: `retention.overflow` `retention.fanout_truncated` `retention.unavailable`, each `unattributed`, each folded straight to a bucket because the code calls them queryable facts, not counters (`:745-750,728`)

### Health mode

Whether a cause describes a one-off loss or an ongoing condition. Only an incident cause
creates unresolved state; without the split, "wiring all 21 sites would mark every member
permanently sick from their first malformed record onward". *(defined in code only)*

- **Names** — core `HealthMode` (`packages/core/src/diagnostics.ts:164`), `CauseSpec.mode` (`:170`), `healthMode()` (`:264`)
- **Not** — diagnostics-store health (whether the store can record at all) · capture health (whether a member's request and response bodies reach the broker) — a mode is a fixed property of a cause, not a state of a member or of the store
- **Values** — `mode`: `point` — a one-off loss, history the moment it happens, no unresolved state and no recovery method · `incident` — an ongoing condition with a named observed recovery

### Attribution

How a member relates to a recorded failure. The cause fixes it: a caller cannot pair an
empty member list with `producer`, and blob-corruption causes resolve their affected set
from `raw_exchange` by hash rather than accepting one. *(defined in code only)*

- **Names** — core `Attribution` (`packages/core/src/diagnostics.ts:136`), `CauseSpec.attribution` (`:172`), `UNATTRIBUTED` (`:148`), `membersForHash()` (`:909`) · DB `diagnostic_event.attribution`, `diagnostic_event.member_name`
- **Not** — the calling member (`getBlob(hash)` has no member, and charging whoever happened to read a corrupt blob would be false attribution inside the store built to prevent false absence)
- **Values** — `producer` — the member whose work produced the failure · `affected` — members whose records are impacted, possibly several · `observer` — declared and documented, and no cause in `CAUSE_SPEC` uses it, so no row can carry it · `unattributed` — no member resolvable; stored as the empty string, because SQLite treats two NULLs as unequal inside a composite primary key and a nullable column would defeat `ON CONFLICT` (`:134-144`)

### Safe fields

The bounded context a diagnostic may retain. Paths are never stored — a 16-hex sha256
prefix and a byte length are — and thrown errors are never stored as text, only as a
classified code. The type is branded by a module-private symbol, so a caller cannot write
a literal, and the store re-checks the shape at runtime because a cast defeats the type.
*(defined in code only)*

- **Names** — core `SafeFields` (`packages/core/src/diagnostics.ts:291`), `FieldPolicy` (`:167`), `SAFE_BRAND` (`:288`), `digestPath()` (`:354`), `safeHash()` (`:362`), `safeCount()` (`:367`), `safeError()` (`:372`), `safeFields()` (`:377`), `classifyError()` (`:340`); module-private `validShape()` (`:403`) and `sanitize()` (`:424`) · server `digestPathSync()` (`apps/server/src/path-digest.ts:11`) · field names `pathDigest`, `pathLength`, `hash`, `kind`, `errorCode`, `count` · DB `diagnostic_event.fields`, JSON of the sanitized object
- **Not** — the path or the error message (never retained; a digest and a length are what recurrence detection needs) · `digestPath` as "the ONLY way to obtain a `pathDigest`" (`:352-355`) — `digestPathSync` is its sanctioned twin, held to byte-identical output, and the production correlator uses it because it hashes inside a synchronous `try` (`apps/server/src/genai-correlator.ts:230,289`)
- **Values** — `fields`: `path` `hash` `count` `none` — the policy a cause is permitted, enforced before storage; anything else is dropped
