- **Verbs** — `digestPath` / `digestPathSync`, `safeHash`, `safeCount`, `safeError`, merged by `safeFields`

### Diagnostic record

One stored failure: a cause, a member name, the cause's attribution, a timestamp and the
sanitized fields. A single emit writes one record per affected member. *(defined in code
only)*

- **Names** — DB `diagnostic_event` (`packages/core/src/diagnostics.ts:592-601`) · core `DiagnosticInput` (`:438-446`), and `resolution: 'event'` on `DiagnosticWindowResult` (`:452`) when the answer came from unfolded detail
- **Not** — activity event (`member_activity` / `ActivityEvent`, the durable record of what a member did, `packages/core/src/activity-store.ts:30`) · the `events` table (stored messages, `packages/core/src/sqlite-event-log.ts:44`) · an OTLP log record (a captured telemetry line). Four things carry the word "event"; only this one is a diagnostic, which is why the row-level concept is called a record in prose.

### Compaction ladder

How diagnostics age: detail rows fold into hour buckets at seven days, hour buckets into
day buckets at thirty days, day buckets drop at about 400 days. Each fold deletes the rows
it consumed, so it is idempotent, and the broker runs it once at startup and hourly after.

- **Names** — core `DiagnosticStore.sweep()` (`packages/core/src/diagnostics.ts:562-563`, impl `:1289`), `foldBucket` (`:705-714`), `DiagnosticOptions.detailMs`/`hourMs`/`dayMs` (`:571-576`) · DB `diagnostic_bucket`, column `resolution` (`hour`, `day`) · broker `DIAGNOSTICS_SWEEP_MS` (`packages/core/src/app.ts:556`), `sweepDiagnostics()` (`:711`), `diagnosticsSweepInterval` (`:724`) · docs [Trace pipeline](/docs/dev/trace-pipeline), "Broker-side diagnostics fold on their own hourly timer"
- **Not** — trace retention (`csuite prune-traces`, the operator's age cutoff over `member_activity`, `gen_ai_inference`, `telemetry`, `raw_exchange` and `raw_blob`; it prunes none of the diagnostic tables, so an operator who pruned the traces has not pruned diagnostics) · the correlator's `sweep()` (evicts stale pending exchanges, `apps/server/src/genai-correlator.ts:489`) · the retired-cause delete at open (drops `diagnostic_state` rows whose cause this build no longer emits, `diagnostics.ts:664-674`, and folds nothing)
- **Verbs** — fold; entry point `sweep`

### Coverage floor

The oldest instant the store still fully covers, raised whenever eviction destroys rows. A
query reaching below it answers `indeterminate` rather than zero, so a satisfied disk
bound never becomes a confident absence. `query()` has no route, tool or command; the only
callers are the broker's own tests. *(defined in code only)*

- **Names** — core `Coverage` (`packages/core/src/diagnostics.ts:268`), `DiagnosticStore.coverageFloor()` (`:562`, impl `:1393`), `raiseFloor()` (`:722-726`), `DiagnosticWindowResult.coverage` (`:459-467`) · DB `diagnostic_meta` key `coverage_floor`
- **Not** — a zero count (`indeterminate` is the store declining to answer, not an answer of none) · `resolution` on the same result (`event` `hour` `day` `none` — which rung of the ladder answered, not how complete the answer is)
- **Values** — `coverage`: `exact` — every matching record survives · `bucket` — answered at a bucket's resolution, wider than the window asked for · `indeterminate` — the window reaches below the floor (`:1166-1174`), or a member filter crosses a fanout refusal (`:1223-1242`)

### Unresolved incident

An incident cause recorded for a member that no observed recovery has cleared; `Presence`
carries the count, and the store keeps the cause and its `since`. Two web-UI surfaces
render it — the roster row on Team Home and the member profile — and no agent-facing
surface does, which is what the SDK's "the surface an agent reads about ITSELF" asks for
and does not get (D45 records the gap; register #240 is where it is argued).

- **Names** — SDK `Presence.diagnosticsUnresolved?: number` (`packages/sdk/src/types.ts:303`, doc `:289-302`), schema `diagnosticsUnresolved` (`packages/sdk/src/schemas.ts:287`) · route `GET /roster` (`packages/core/src/app.ts:1430`, path const `packages/sdk/src/protocol.ts:25`) · DB `diagnostic_state` (`cause`, `member_name`, `since`) · core `DiagnosticStore.unresolved()` (`packages/core/src/diagnostics.ts:555`) · UI `presenceDiagnostics(p).unresolved` (`packages/web-ui/src/lib/roster.ts:86-93`), badge rendering `3 UNRESOLVED` on the profile (`packages/web-ui/src/components/MemberProfile.tsx:182-189`) and on the Team Home roster row (`packages/web-ui/src/components/TeamHome.tsx:199-207`)
- **Not** — unexpanded permissions (`rawPermissions`, preset names and permission leaves as written on disk before expansion, `packages/core/src/members-domain.ts:21`) · a body the runner could not resolve and "forwarded unresolved" — oversized, not a regular file, or not UTF-8; that is unread, not unhealed, and it is what produces `correlator.body_ref_unreadable` · capture health, which renders beside this on both surfaces as `NO CAPTURE` and `CAPTURE UNCHECKED` (`packages/web-ui/src/components/TeamHome.tsx:196`, `:223`) and answers a different question — whether bodies reach the broker, not whether a recorded failure has healed
- **Values** — absent means the broker retains no diagnostics and has no opinion, never "this member is clean"; `0` is the positive statement that it looked and found none outstanding
- **Verbs** — read on `GET /roster`; cleared only by an observed recovery

### Diagnostics-store health

A broker-wide statement of whether the diagnostics store can currently record and describe
itself, stamped identically onto every member's presence row. `unknown` means it cannot
say — a write threw, its metadata is unreadable, or unresolved state was evicted;
`degraded` means an overflow inside the detail window is actively shedding evidence.

- **Names** — SDK `Presence.diagnosticsRetention?: 'healthy' | 'degraded' | 'unknown'` (`packages/sdk/src/types.ts:311`, doc `:304-310`), schema `diagnosticsRetention` (`packages/sdk/src/schemas.ts:288`) · core `RetentionHealth` (`packages/core/src/diagnostics.ts:272`), `DiagnosticStore.health()` (`:564`, impl `:1384`) · route `GET /roster` (`packages/core/src/app.ts:1431`) · DB `diagnostic_meta` keys `overflow_last`, `state_evicted` · UI `presenceDiagnostics(p).retention`, badge rendering `DIAGNOSTICS DEGRADED` / `DIAGNOSTICS UNKNOWN` (`packages/web-ui/src/components/MemberProfile.tsx:190-197`, `packages/web-ui/src/components/TeamHome.tsx:208-216`)
- **Not** — capture health (`captureHealth`, genuinely per member — whether that member's bodies reach the broker — and its good value is `ok`, not `healthy`; `packages/sdk/src/types.ts:46`) · trace retention and `csuite prune-traces` (this reports on neither) · the three other SDK `degraded`s rendered near the same badge row: executor state (`types.ts:364`), a runner-condition frame (`:346`), and a message-disposition reason (`:470`) · the "durable latch" its own cause comment points at (`packages/core/src/diagnostics.ts:113-114`) — three distinct things wear that word: the in-memory availability latch `writeFailed` (`:938-947`, "IN MEMORY, deliberately"), the `retention.unavailable` bucket row written on the next successful write (`:1101-1119`), and the genuinely durable eviction latch `diagnostic_meta.state_evicted` (`:854-860`)
- **Values** — `healthy` · `degraded` — evidence is being dropped to satisfy a cap · `unknown` — reachable and load-bearing: "a store that cannot say 'I do not know' would make this signal the thing it exists to prevent, one layer down"
- **Decided** — D54, **proposed**: broker-wide, a property of the broker repeated on every presence row, over reading `diagnosticsRetention` as a per-member fact; and `degraded` becomes `shedding` with `RetentionHealth` becoming `DiagnosticsStoreHealth`, over keeping "retention health" and a fourth claimant on `degraded` (2026-09-03). The wire, the schema and the badge still spell it `diagnosticsRetention` / `degraded`; those are the legacy spellings until the rename ships.

### Observed recovery

The only thing that clears an unresolved incident: a named success at the same site, with
one emitter method per incident cause. Detail expiry never heals — that would satisfy a
disk bound by erasing evidence — and a point cause has no recovery at all. *(defined in
code only)*

- **Names** — core recovery methods on `DiagnosticEmitter` — `correlatorBodyRefRead`, `correlatorRawCaptureSucceeded`, `otlpLogsStored`, `otlpGenaiIngested`, `otlpMetricsStored`, `codexGenaiIngestEntrySucceeded`, `activityAppended`, `toolinvokeAuditAppended` (`packages/core/src/diagnostics.ts:544-551`) · private `resolve()`, which deletes the `diagnostic_state` row (`:1152-1154`)
- **Not** — the retired-cause delete at open (removes state whose cause this build no longer emits, because "its clearing emit is gone with the mechanism") · detail expiry and the compaction ladder, which age evidence and clear nothing · a generic `recovered(cause, member)`, rejected because a site that succeeded at writing activity must not be able to clear a correlator incident
- **Verbs** — emitted by the succeeding site, never requested; an empty successful batch must not clear, guarded at `packages/core/src/app.ts:1960,1987,5132`

## Capture source

Everything a member did is stored as an activity stream and sliced into objective
traces. This family names the other half: the files and listeners a runner reads to
fill that stream. The distinction that matters is **source vs sink** — a capture source
is the agent's own record on disk, the capture host is the runner-owned thing that reads
it and ships the result. Nothing here intercepts network traffic, and the word
"transcript" names one specific source, never the genus.

### Capture source

The agent's own durable record on disk that a runner tails to produce a member's
activity stream. Every runner that captures content has exactly one, and the runner
reads it — there is no proxy, no interception, no second copy. A runner may have none:
`stub` declares capture tier 1 and produces no content at all.

- **Names** — runtime `TranscriptReader` / `attachTranscriptReader`
  (`packages/cli/src/runtime/trace/transcript-reader.ts:2-3`, "the transcript-primary
  capture source for Claude runners") · `RolloutReader` / `attachRolloutReader`
  (`packages/cli/src/runtime/agents/codex/rollout-reader.ts:2`, "the rollout-primary
  capture source") · CLI `csuite claude --no-trace` / `csuite codex --no-trace`
  (starts no capture host, so nothing is tailed) · docs
  [Trace pipeline](/docs/dev/trace-pipeline)
- **Not** — Capture host (the runner-owned sink every source feeds, not a source) ·
  Activity stream (the durable broker-side record the sources fill) · Hook server
  (presence-only; it hands over the transcript path and emits no content) ·
  Rollout-trace bundle (feeds the gen_ai / raw-body layer, not the activity stream) ·
  Claude Code hook callbacks (an earlier design for `tool_action`; the hook server is
  presence-only and emits no content, which the SDK header now says —
  `packages/sdk/src/types.ts:2433-2436`) · the SSE reassembler exported from `csuite-core`
  (`reassembleAnthropicSse`, `parseSseEvents`, `looksLikeSseStream`) — published API with
  no in-tree caller, and not evidence of a streaming-capture path
- **Values** — the two content sources at HEAD: **session transcript** (Claude; every
  event carries the line `uuid` as `sourceId`, so the broker refuses duplicates) ·
  **rollout** (codex; emits no `sourceId`, so nothing dedups it). Idempotence is a
  property of the source, not of the activity stream.
- **Verbs** — tail, parse, map to `ActivityEvent`, enqueue on the capture host
- **Decided** — D43: the Traces page names its two capture sources per runner, over
  leaving "each agent's own instrumentation" as the whole answer (2026-09-03)
- **Decided** — D43: capture tier is product vocabulary — the UI renders `capture: 3` to
  an operator with no legend — over keeping it in the contributor standard only; the
  entry lives in the observation-and-capture family (2026-09-03)

### Session transcript

"Claude Code appends every turn to a newline-delimited JSON transcript at
`~/.claude/projects/<slug>/<sessionId>.jsonl`"
(`packages/core/src/trace/transcript.ts:4-5`). Claude Code writes it; the runner learns
its path from the `transcript_path` on every hook body and tails it, re-pinning whenever
the agent process is swapped under a live capture host.

- **Names** — `csuite-core` `TranscriptEntry`, `parseTranscriptLine`,
  `parseAnthropicMessage` · runtime `TranscriptReader`, `attachTranscriptReader` ·
  hook body field `transcript_path` · DB `member_activity.source_id` (the transcript
  line `uuid`) · CLI `csuite claude --no-trace` · docs
  [Trace pipeline](/docs/dev/trace-pipeline)
- **Not** — Rollout (codex's equivalent file; different format, different parser, no
  `sourceId`) · the web UI `Transcript` component ("scrolling message list for the
  current thread" — the human-visible chat log of a thread, which touches none of
  this) · agent context (what the MCP tool descriptions mean when they promise a
  value "never enters agent context", and what `telemetry-store.ts:8` calls "the
  conversation transcript": the conversation record the agent writes into, which for a
  codex member is a rollout, not a transcript)
- **Verbs** — tail, parse, dedup on `sourceId`
- **Decided** — D55: rename the UI component `Transcript.tsx` to `ThreadView.tsx` —
  "Transcript" is never a rendered label, only an identifier — over keeping the name
  and forbidding bare "transcript" in every capture context (2026-09-03)

### Rollout

"Codex appends every turn of a thread to a newline-delimited JSON rollout at
`<CODEX_HOME>/sessions/YYYY/MM/DD/rollout-<ts>-<id>.jsonl`"
(`packages/cli/src/runtime/agents/codex/rollout-reader.ts:4-5`). One file per thread —
the root thread and every subagent — and one parser per file, because codex threads run
concurrently and interleaving two threads into one parser corrupts turn accumulation.
Rollouts are durable: the runner symlinks codex's `sessions/` into a directory outside
the ephemeral `CODEX_HOME`, so files present when the reader attaches are prior runs'
history and are ignored (`codexHome.sessionsLinked`); when the symlink cannot be made
the reader falls back to tracking everything in a genuinely per-run directory
(`packages/cli/src/runtime/agents/codex/adapter.ts:591-597`). The reader's own header
still describes the pre-symlink world, where teardown deleted the rollouts
(`rollout-reader.ts:6-8,33-37`) — the adapter is authoritative.

- **Names** — runtime `RolloutReader` / `attachRolloutReader`, `RolloutParser` /
  `createRolloutParser` (`packages/cli/src/runtime/agents/codex/`) · SDK
  `ActivityToolAction.source` value `codex_rollout` · docs
  [Trace pipeline](/docs/dev/trace-pipeline) "Codex: rollout-primary, layered"
- **Not** — Session transcript (the Claude analogue; `rollout-parser.ts:4-5` calls the
  rollout "codex's own durable, untruncated transcript of a thread", which is the one
  place the two words merge — they are separate sources) · Rollout-trace bundle (a
  different codex artifact that shares the prefix) · query source (which interleaved lane
  of the member's work produced a line, not which file it came from)
- **Verbs** — tail, parse

### Rollout-trace bundle

"With `CODEX_ROLLOUT_TRACE_ROOT=<dir>` set on the child, codex writes a per-session
bundle `<dir>/trace-<traceId>-<rolloutId>/`" holding `manifest.json`, an ordered
`trace.jsonl` spine, and `payloads/N.json` — the verbatim Responses API request and
response bodies (`packages/cli/src/runtime/agents/codex/bundle-reader.ts:5-14`). It
feeds the gen_ai / raw-body fidelity layer, never the activity stream.
*(defined in code only)*

- **Names** — runtime `BundleReader`, `attachBundleReader` · env
  `CODEX_ROLLOUT_TRACE_ROOT` · docs [Trace pipeline](/docs/dev/trace-pipeline),
  [Runner conformance](/docs/dev/conformance) ("rollout-trace bundles")
- **Not** — Rollout (the activity-stream content source; this is the raw-body layer
  beside it) · Objective trace (one objective's slice of a member's activity stream) ·
  Capture source. Write the name in full — "bundle" and "trace" each already mean
  something else here.
- **Verbs** — poll, pair by `inference_call_id`, upload to the broker's gen_ai ingest

### Capture host

"CaptureHost — the runner-owned handle that turns each agent's NATIVE instrumentation
into one normalized activity stream" (`packages/cli/src/runtime/trace/host.ts:2-3`).
It is the capture *sink*: it owns the batched activity uploader, the work-state signal
(spelled `busy` in the runner at HEAD), the loopback hook server and the transcript
reader, and every adapter pushes events through `enqueue(event)`.

- **Names** — runtime `CaptureHost`, `startCaptureHost`, `hookEndpointUrl`,
  `enqueue` / `enqueueImmediate` · CLI `--no-trace` (the runner starts no capture
  host) · docs [Trace pipeline](/docs/dev/trace-pipeline) "The capture host"
- **Not** — Capture source (what the host reads) · Broker (the server the uploader
  ships to) · the codex app-server stream, which is presence, busy and stderr-printer
  only — the header says so, and names the `RolloutReader` as codex's sole content
  source (`packages/cli/src/runtime/trace/host.ts:38-41`).
- **Verbs** — start, enqueue, close

### Capture primacy

Which channel inside a runner is authoritative for content, and which contributes only
presence. A per-runner property of the capture wiring, stated as a phrase rather than a
field. The three values are in wide use in the runner and the contributor docs; the
superordinate is coined here, because the property is asserted everywhere and named
nowhere. *(defined in code only)*

- **Names** — docs [Trace pipeline](/docs/dev/trace-pipeline) headings "Claude Code:
  transcript-primary" and "Codex: rollout-primary, layered" · runtime
  `packages/cli/src/runtime/trace/host.ts`, `trace/hook-server.ts`,
  `agents/codex/rollout-parser.ts`
- **Not** — Capture tier (a whole-runner declaration of what is captured; both
  transcript-primary and rollout-primary runners declare tier 3) · Work state (the
  live signal the presence-only channels drive)
- **Values** — `transcript-primary`: Claude's `llm_exchange` / `tool_action` /
  `user_prompt` come from the session transcript, hooks contribute presence ·
  `rollout-primary`: codex's content comes from the rollout, the app-server stream
  contributes presence, busy and the operator's stderr view · `presence-only`: a
  channel that drives the work-state signal and metadata and emits no content. A
  presence-only channel is not a tier-1 runner — it is one channel inside a tier-3
  one.

### Hook server

"Loopback HTTP endpoint for Claude Code hook events"
(`packages/cli/src/runtime/trace/hook-server.ts:2`): a `node:http` listener on
127.0.0.1, torn down with the session, that the agent's in-process SDK hook callbacks
POST every payload to, routing on `hook_event_name`. It is presence-only — it drives
the work-state signal and relays each distinct `transcript_path` — and emits no
`tool_action` or `user_prompt` content.

- **Names** — runtime `HookServer`, `startHookServer`, `ClaudeHookEventName`,
  `hookEndpointUrl`; hook forwarders `buildHookForwarders`, `FORWARDED_HOOK_EVENTS`
  (`packages/cli/src/runtime/agents/claude-agent.ts`) · CLI doctor check
  "loopback hook server bindable"; `--no-trace` help "no hook forwarders" · docs
  [Trace pipeline](/docs/dev/trace-pipeline)
- **Not** — the webhook receiver at `POST /hooks/:slug`, reached by
  `csuite notifications` (alias `hooks`) — the external-notification ingress, a broker
  route with per-endpoint verification and no shared code with this · generic callbacks
  in UI and server code, which are hooks only in ordinary software English · Capture
  source (the hook server carries no content; it points at one)
- **Values** — `ClaudeHookEventName`: `PreToolUse` `PostToolUse` `PostToolUseFailure`
  `UserPromptSubmit` `Stop` `SubagentStop` `Notification` `SessionStart`
  (`packages/cli/src/runtime/trace/hook-server.ts:75-86`), and `FORWARDED_HOOK_EVENTS`
  forwards every one of them (`packages/cli/src/runtime/agents/claude-agent.ts:135-144`)
  — the declared set and the forwarded set are the same, which is the invariant to keep.
  `PostToolBatch` is deliberately excluded and the header says why: the SDK fires it once
  per batch *in addition to* the per-tool `PostToolUse` that already drained each handle,
  and its body carries `tool_calls[]` rather than the `tool_use_id` this server matches
  on, "so routing it could only ever be a no-op" (`hook-server.ts:25-29`).
- **Verbs** — start, route, relay, close

### Capture source tag

`ActivityToolAction.source` — which producer a tool action came from, and the
discriminator between the two provenance classes the SDK names: **agent-native**
(`'transcript'`, `'codex_rollout'`) and **broker-audit** (`'tool_source'`). The docstring
and the code agree at HEAD (`packages/sdk/src/types.ts:2473-2476`).
*(defined in code only)*

- **Names** — SDK `ActivityToolAction.source` · DB `member_activity.event_json` at
  `$.source` · emitted by `transcript-reader.ts:248`,
  `agents/codex/rollout-parser.ts:270` and the broker's invoke route
  (`packages/core/src/app.ts:2918`)
- **Not** — query source `querySource` (which interleaved lane of the member's work made
  the call: `codex_main_thread`, `codex_subagent:<id8>`, `agent:<name>` /
  `agent:builtin:<name>`, `repl_main_thread`, `web_search_tool`, `web_fetch_apply`,
  `away_summary`; persisted as `raw_exchange.query_source` and rendered in the timeline
  and trace panel, where this tag reaches the view model and is never drawn) · dedup key
  `sourceId` / `member_activity.source_id` (the transcript line `uuid` the broker refuses
  duplicates on) · busy counter `ActivitySource` (`turn_active` / `tool_inflight`, the
  two in-flight counters behind work state, never persisted; `BusySource` survives as a
  deprecated alias) · the `SessionStart` hook's `source` (that session's origin:
  `startup` / `resume` / `clear` / `compact`)
- **Values** — `transcript` — Claude's session transcript reader · `codex_rollout` —
  codex's rollout parser · `tool_source` — the broker's own record of an invoke it ran.
  Comparing `input` across the three compares three different things; see Tool action.

## Action verbs

The rest of this ontology names things. This section names what you do to them. Four
verb families run at once in the tree — CRUD (`create`/`update`/`delete`), browser
(`list`/`view`), English (`add`/`remove`/`send`) and POSIX (`ls`/`rm`/`mv`) — and
nothing arbitrated between them, so one operation is spelled one way in the toolbox an
agent reads and another way in the CLI a human types. The rule that settles it is the
[verb grammar](#verb-grammar) in Conventions; the entries below say what each verb
means and what it is not. Nothing in the tree states the rule today: [MCP
tools](/docs/dev/mcp-tools) groups the toolbox by domain (`docs/dev/mcp-tools.mdx:19-38`)
and names no convention at all.

### Verb

The name of an operation, drawn from a closed set, spelled the same on every surface
that offers it. The repository already uses the word this way for a CLI subcommand —
"The internal `csuite mcp-bridge` verb" (`packages/cli/src/index.ts:15`).

- **Names** — CLI `csuite <verb>` · docs [MCP tools](/docs/dev/mcp-tools), [CLI reference](/docs/reference/cli)
- **Not** — `ServiceVerb` (`'claude' | 'codex' | 'stub'`, which runner installs and restarts its own systemd unit — `csuite <verb> install-service`, `packages/cli/src/commands/install-service.ts:1-3,37`; the CLI's own `USAGE` already writes it `csuite <runner> install-service`, `packages/cli/src/index.ts:82`) · `ContextControlVerb` (`'compact' | 'clear' | 'reload'`, "What a broker-originated context control asks a runner to do", `packages/sdk/src/types.ts:2502,2519`) · Action record (the past-participle name of an operation that already ran)
- **Values** — the closed cross-surface set is `get` `list` `create` `update` `delete` `set`/`delete` (a write-only value slot) `bind`/`unbind` `post`, plus four verbs that stay outside it because they name distinct operations: `archive` `write` `upload` `download`. Named lifecycle transitions are a per-domain register, not part of the closed set, and each is defined in its own family entry: `complete` `cancel` `reassign` `discuss` (objectives) · `refresh` `invoke` (tool sources) · `replay` (notification deliveries) · `approve` `reject` (enrollment) · `rotate` `revoke` (member tokens) · `resolve` (secrets) · `join` (channels). Each of these has its own route: `reassign`, the one most often described as having none, is `POST /objectives/:id/reassign` (`packages/sdk/src/protocol.ts:135`), reached by `client.reassignObjective` and by `csuite objectives reassign` (`packages/cli/src/commands/objectives.ts:200`)
- **Decided** — D53: rename the two code uses, `ServiceVerb` → `RunnerCommand` and `ContextControlVerb` → `ContextControlAction`, over having the ontology say "operation" and leave "verb" to the code (2026-09-03). `ContextControlVerb`'s spelling is on the wire (`ContextControlRequest.verb`, `packages/sdk/src/schemas.ts:1899`) and is an MCP input property (`packages/cli/src/runtime/tools.ts:295`), so that half is a protocol change

### Post

Append one message to one thread, fan it out to that thread's live subscribers, and
return delivery counts and the new message id. Three MCP tools implement it at three
scopes — the team's `general` channel, a direct thread, a named channel — and all three
land on the same `POST /push`.

- **Names** — SDK `Client.push()` · route `POST /push` · CLI `csuite push --body <text> (--agent <id> | --broadcast)` · MCP `broadcast`, `send`, `channels_post` · UI "Send" · docs [MCP tools](/docs/dev/mcp-tools)
- **Not** — Web Push (the browser notification transport: `PATHS.pushVapidPublicKey`, `PATHS.pushSubscriptions`, `packages/sdk/src/protocol.ts:36-37`, UI "Push on this device") · a post (the message the operation produces — `{postCount} posts` on an objective thread; the UI's noun, never its verb) · `objectives_discuss` (a different route, `POST /objectives/:id/discuss`, with a computed thread membership and a 16384-character body cap against `broadcast`'s 65536 — `packages/cli/src/runtime/tools.ts:445`, `:175`) · "Send" as a button label (an affordance word; the operation behind it is post)
- **Verbs** — post. `push` names the wire call and the browser transport, not the user-facing action; the three MCP scopes converge on `team_post` / `members_post` / `channels_post` under D41, recorded on **Message**, behind D42's alias window (`broadcast` and `send` are the only chat tools with no noun). `objectives_discuss` keeps its own verb.

### Get

Fetch one record's full detail by its stable key. `view` and `show` are aliases that
carry no distinct meaning: the SDK, which arbitrates under D42, has no `view*` method
anywhere on `Client`.

- **Names** — SDK `getTeam`, `getObjective`, `getChannel`, `getSecret`, `getVariable`, `getToolSource`, `getTeamProcess` · route `GET /objectives/:id`, `GET /secrets/:slug` · CLI `csuite team get`, `csuite secrets view` (alias `show`), `csuite tools show` · MCP `team_get`, `team_process_get`, `objectives_view`, `secrets_view`, `variables_view`, `tool_sources_view`, `notifications_view` · core `get()`, `getBySlug()`
- **Not** — List (a page of a collection) · Roster (a presence-bearing projection of the member list, defined under Roster — there is no `members_list` tool, so an agent that wants members must know to call `roster`) · `csuite fs get` (streams a file onto the caller's disk — see Upload / download, and the reason the CLI's `get` means two unrelated things)
- **Verbs** — get. Two MCP tools spell it `_get` and five spell it `_view` on no principle, and the CLI disagrees with itself: `csuite secrets view` works while `csuite tools view` fails, because `show` is the only spelling that command accepts (`packages/cli/src/commands/tools.ts:50`). The grammar's answer is `_get` everywhere; the five `_view` → `_get` renames are **not** in D42's scheduled set, which covers the high-severity names only.

### List

Return a page of a collection, optionally scoped. Every layer but the toolbox spells it
`list`; eight MCP read tools are bare nouns instead, so the collection an agent wants is
often not under the name it would guess.

- **Names** — SDK `listMembers`, `listObjectives`, `listChannels`, `listToolSources`, `listSecrets`, `listVariables`, `listNotificationEndpoints`, `listNotificationProfiles`, `listNotificationDeliveries`, `listActivity`, `listTelemetry`, `listTokens` · route `GET /objectives`, `GET /members`, `GET /channels`, `GET /history` · CLI `csuite member list`, `csuite objectives list`, `csuite tools list`, `csuite secrets list`, `csuite variables list`, `csuite notifications list`, `csuite auth list` · MCP `channels_list`, `objectives_list`, `tool_sources_list`, `variables_list`, `secrets_list`, `notifications_list` · core `list()`, `listAll()`, `listForMember()`
- **Not** — Get (one record by its key) · the eight bare-noun read tools, which are lists under names that do not say so: `roster`, `recent`, `team_status`, `connect_pending`, `notifications_deliveries`, `notifications_profiles`, `team_process_history`, `fs_shared` · Roster (a distinct referent, not a spelling of `members_list`: `GET /roster` returns the public `Teammate` projection plus presence to any member, `GET /members` returns full `Member` records including private `instructions` and requires `members.manage`)
- **Values** — the `notifications_*` group is the trap: `notifications_list` returns **endpoints** ("List every external-notification endpoint", `packages/cli/src/runtime/tools.ts:1379`), `notifications_profiles` returns shared auth profiles, `notifications_deliveries` returns delivery receipts. The REST layer names all three nouns explicitly — `/notifications/endpoints`, `/notifications/profiles`, `/notifications/deliveries` (`packages/sdk/src/protocol.ts:121-123`) — and the unsuffixed tool name silently means the first.

### Create

Insert a new record under a caller-supplied immutable key. Creation alone binds nobody
and stores no value: "Creating a secret stores NO value and binds NOBODY"
(`packages/cli/src/runtime/tools.ts:1181`). MCP and the CLI split opposite ways — MCP
says `create` six times and `add` once, the CLI says `create` twice and `add` four times
— so under D42 `members_add` becomes `members_create`, and `add` survives as a CLI alias.

- **Names** — SDK `createMember`, `createObjective`, `createChannel`, `createSecret`, `createVariable`, `createToolSource`, `createNotificationEndpoint`, `createNotificationProfile` · route `POST /members`, `POST /objectives`, `POST /channels` · CLI `csuite member create`, `csuite secrets add` (alias `create`), `csuite tools add`, `csuite variables add`, `csuite notifications add` · MCP `members_add`, `objectives_create`, `secrets_create`, `variables_create`, `tool_sources_create`, `notifications_create`, `notifications_profile_create` · UI "Create member", "Create channel"
- **Not** — "New" (a UI entry-point label for opening a form — see Affordance word) · Set (writes a value into a record that already exists) · Bind (grants a member access to a record that already exists) · Enrollment (`members_add` creates a member pending device enrollment; the device flow that binds a credential to it is a separate operation)

### Update

A partial merge of mutable fields on an existing record: the fields present in the
request change, nothing else does, and no history row is appended. `PATCH` everywhere on
the wire.

- **Names** — SDK `updateTeam`, `updateMember`, `updateObjective`, `updateChannel`, `updateSecret`, `updateVariable`, `updateToolSource`, `updateNotificationEndpoint` · route `PATCH /team`, `PATCH /objectives/:id`, `PATCH /channels/:slug` · CLI `csuite objectives update`, `csuite member update`, `csuite team set` (alias `update`) · MCP `team_update`, `members_update`, `objectives_update`, `secrets_update`, `variables_update`, `tool_sources_update`, `notifications_update` · UI "Save"
- **Not** — Write (a versioned full-text replacement that appends an edit row) · Set (a write-only value slot; and `TeamStore.setTeam`, "Create or replace the team singleton", against `updateTeam`, "Patch the team singleton; only the supplied fields change" — `packages/core/src/team-store.ts:267,277`, which is why `set` cannot also mean update) · `renameChannel` (the same `PATCH /channels/:slug` as `updateChannel`, whose request type strictly contains it — `packages/sdk/src/types.ts:706-713`) · `csuite tools enable|disable` (a shortcut for `update --enabled`; a field shortcut, not an operation, and the only one of the four registries that has one — whether it stays, spreads or goes is D52) · `complete` / `cancel` / `reassign` (named transitions — `objectives_update` "is for STATE, not conversation … and it never transitions to 'done'; call `objectives_complete` for that", `packages/cli/src/runtime/tools.ts:384-385`)
- **Verbs** — update. `csuite team set` is the documented CLI spelling today (`update` dispatches as an undocumented alias); the grammar reverses that pairing.

### Set

Replace the write-only payload attached to a record: settable, encrypted at rest, never
readable back through any command or endpoint. Its clear is `delete`, on the same path.

- **Names** — SDK `setSecretValue`, `setVariableValue`, `setToolCredential`, `setNotificationEndpointSecret`, `setNotificationProfileSecret`, and the matching `delete*` five · route `PUT`/`DELETE` on `/secrets/:slug/value`, `/variables/:slug/value`, `/tool-sources/:slug/credential`, `/notifications/endpoints/:slug/secret` · CLI `csuite secrets set-value`/`delete-value`, `csuite variables set-value`/`delete-value`, `csuite tools cred`/`cred-rm`, `csuite notifications set-secret`/`delete-secret` · MCP `secrets_set_value`, `variables_set_value`, `tool_sources_set_credential`, `notifications_set_secret`, `notifications_profile_set_secret`, and the matching `*_delete_*` four · DB event `EnvironmentEvent.action: 'value_set'` · UI "Set value", "Set credential", "Set secret"
- **Not** — Update (a partial field merge on the record itself) · `TeamStore.setTeam` (a full replace of the team record) · `csuite team set` (an alias of `csuite team update`, not a full replace) · UI "Remove value" / "Remove credential" / "Remove secret" (affordance labels for the clear; the operation is delete, and it clears the payload without deleting the record)
- **Verbs** — set / delete. `csuite tools cred` and `cred-rm` are the CLI's only abbreviated pair; they read `set-credential` / `delete-credential`, matching `set-value` / `delete-value` two commands over.

### Write

A versioned full-text replacement: the call carries the whole document and appends an
edit row bearing the actor, a `reason` and an amendment disposition. Only the team
process is written this way, and no CLI verb reaches it.

- **Names** — SDK `writeTeamProcess`, `TeamProcessEdit` · route `PUT /team-process`, `GET /team-process/history` · MCP `team_process_write` · DB `team_process_edits.reason`, `team_process_edits.disposition` · UI "Save" · docs [Team process](/docs/concepts/team-process)
- **Not** — Update (a partial merge that appends no history row) · `fs_write` (writes inline `text` or `base64` content into a file; the POSIX register inside `fs_*` — see Upload / download) · Set (a write-only value slot) · Message disposition (`MessageDisposition`, a runner's acknowledgement of one delivery — the same word, a different closed set, offered on the same MCP surface an agent holds)
- **Values** — `disposition` is `AmendmentDisposition`: `correction` (retroactive; the prior text was never validly binding) `scope_change` (forward-only; work already underway is not held to it) — `packages/sdk/src/types.ts:1807`
- **Verbs** — write. The name is load-bearing: this operation versions, where update merges and records nothing. Whether an operator gets `csuite team-process get|history|write` is D50.

### Delete

Remove a record and cascade what hangs off it — bindings, stored value, credential, tool
definitions, cache. The same inversion as Create: MCP says `delete` ten times and
`remove` once, the CLI documents `rm`; under D42 `members_remove` becomes
`members_delete` and `rm` / `remove` stay as CLI aliases.

- **Names** — SDK `deleteMember`, `deleteSecret`, `deleteVariable`, `deleteToolSource`, `deleteNotificationEndpoint`, `deleteNotificationProfile`, `deleteCustomTool` · route `DELETE /members/:name`, `DELETE /secrets/:slug` · CLI `csuite member delete` (alias `remove`), `csuite secrets rm` (aliases `remove`, `delete`), `csuite tools rm`, `csuite variables rm`, `csuite notifications rm` · MCP `members_remove`, `secrets_delete`, `variables_delete`, `tool_sources_delete`, `notifications_delete`, `notifications_profile_delete`, `tool_sources_delete_tool` · UI "Delete source", "Delete tool"
- **Not** — Archive (a soft delete: `channels.archived_at` is stamped, the row survives, and a new channel may reclaim the slug — `DELETE /channels/:slug`, store `archive()` at `packages/core/src/channels.ts:191`, UI "Archive channel") · `fs_rm` (the POSIX register inside `fs_*` is deliberate and stays) · `delete` on a value slot (clears the payload and keeps the record — see Set)

### Bind / unbind

Add or drop one row in a resource's bindings table, which decides whether that resource
resolves for that member. These are the verbs; the relation they write is a **grant**.

- **Names** — SDK `bindSecret`/`unbindSecret`, `bindVariable`/`unbindVariable`, `bindToolSource`/`unbindToolSource`, `EnvironmentEvent.action: 'bound' | 'unbound'` · route `POST /secrets/:slug/bindings`, `DELETE /secrets/:slug/bindings/:name` · CLI `csuite secrets bind|unbind`, `csuite variables bind|unbind`, `csuite tools bind|unbind` · MCP `secrets_bindings`, `variables_bindings`, `tool_sources_bindings` · core `bind()`, `unbind()` · DB `tool_source_bindings` · UI "Bind member", "Unbind", section "Access"
- **Not** — Member binding, the noun (the member↔resource relation these verbs write, which owns the word "binding" under D10) · Request template (the HTTP call a custom tool declares, spelled `CustomToolBinding` / `custom_tools.binding_json` today; `tool_sources_bindings` and `tool_sources_define_tool`'s `binding` argument sit in the same tool group carrying the two senses) · `FilesystemStore.grant()` and the `fs_grants` table (read access to one attached path, granted automatically by a message, never by `bind`) · granting a permission leaf (prose in [Permissions](/docs/concepts/permissions); no `bind` call exists for it) · Create (a source with `allMembers=true` needs no binding at all)
- **Verbs** — bind / unbind. `secrets_bindings`, `variables_bindings` and `tool_sources_bindings` are the only toolbox names that mutate while reading as a plural noun: they sit beside `_list` and `_view`, and only their descriptions say so — "Grant or revoke members' access to a source" (`packages/cli/src/runtime/tools.ts:936`). Under D42 all three read `<noun>_bind` / `<noun>_unbind`.

### Upload / download

Move a file's bytes between the runner's own disk and the csuite filesystem so that they
never reach the agent: "file bytes flow from the runner directly to POST /fs/write and
never enter an IPC frame or tool result"
(`packages/cli/src/runtime/tools.ts:1704-1705`).

- **Names** — route `POST /fs/write`, `GET /fs/read/<path>` · CLI `csuite fs put`, `csuite fs get` · MCP `fs_upload`, `fs_download` · code `uploadLocalFile`, `downloadLocalFile`
- **Not** — `fs_write` / `fs_read` (carry the content through the tool call as `text` or `base64`, so the bytes do enter the agent's context; `fs_write`'s description opens "Upload a file." — `packages/cli/src/runtime/tools.ts:1649` — and should open "Write a file from inline content", which is a description fix, not a rename) · `csuite team get` (reads a record — see Get) · Write (the versioned team-process replacement)
- **Verbs** — upload / download for the streaming pair, so the CLI and the toolbox name it the same way and `get` stops meaning two things. `csuite fs put|get` is the only CLI spelling today; the grammar makes `upload`/`download` the documented pair and keeps `put`/`get` dispatching.

### Action record

A stored row naming an operation that already ran, written in the past participle so that
one formatter can render an audit trail. *(defined in code only)*

- **Names** — SDK `ObjectiveEventKind`, `ChannelAuditAction`, `EnvironmentEvent.action` · DB `objective_events.kind`, `channel_audit.action`
- **Not** — Verb (the name of an operation you call; an action record names one that already happened) · `ContextControlVerb` (a request the broker sends, not a record of one that ran; its record is `ContextControlOutcome`)
- **Values** — `ObjectiveEventKind`: `assigned` `blocked` `unblocked` `completed` `cancelled` `reassigned` `watcher_added` `watcher_removed`, plus `amended` and `event_corrected`, which are "LEGACY READ-ONLY KINDS. No write path produces either" (`packages/sdk/src/types.ts:1788`) · `EnvironmentEvent.action`: `value_set` `bound` `unbound` · `ChannelAuditAction`: `create` `rename` `description` `member_add` `member_remove` `member_role` `archive` — imperative today, and `description` and `member_role` name the field that changed rather than what happened
- **Decided** — D51: migrate `ChannelAuditAction` to the past participle — `created`, `renamed`, `description_changed`, `member_added`, `member_removed`, `role_changed`, `archived` — over leaving three tenses in one file; the two enums that already agree outnumber the outlier, and `process_document_edits` → `team_process_edits` is the standing precedent for migrating a stored name (`packages/core/src/team-process.ts:147`) (2026-09-03)

### Affordance word

A UI label chosen for the control rather than for the operation behind it. Affordance
words are exempt from the verb grammar and name no concept: "Save", "New", "Refresh",
"Remove".

- **Names** — UI "Save" (the team-context editor and the team-process editor), "+ New member", "Refresh", "Remove value"
- **Not** — Update / Write (the two different operations behind the two "Save" buttons in the same team view — `TeamHome.tsx:400` calls `updateTeam`, `:520` calls `writeTeamProcess`) · Create (the operation behind "+ New member", the "New member" form heading and the "Create member" submit button — three labels for one operation in one file, `MembersPanel.tsx:120,262,324`) · Set / Delete (the value-slot pair behind "Set value" and "Remove value")

## Failure and error

Failure is not one concept here. It is ten structurally different objects that
share a handful of English words: a thrown class, a JSON response body, a bounded
token, a bounded reason, an outcome, a subsystem health verdict, a per-check
verdict, a process exit code, a severity band, and a tool result. There is no
shared base class and no shared envelope, and adopting one word for all of them
would erase distinctions the code argues for in its own comments. The distinction
that governs the rest: whether the system **chose** the negative (a refusal),
**suffered** it (a failure), or **lost the evidence** (a loss) — three registers
that must never borrow each other's words.

### Error

A thrown JavaScript `Error` subclass: an in-process control-flow object, raised and
caught inside one runtime. There are 35 of them, 32 extending `Error` directly, with
no shared base class and no rule for which ones carry a `code`. Nothing named
`error` on the wire is one of these. *(defined in code only)*

- **Names** — SDK `ClientError {status, body}` · core `SecretsError` `ChannelsError`
  `ObjectivesError` `ToolSourcesError` `NotificationsError` `TeamProcessError` `FsError`
  (`code: FsErrorCode`) · CLI `UsageError` `AgentAdapterError` `RunnerStartupError` ·
  web host `LoginError {code: string | null}`
- **Not** — Error response (the JSON body an HTTP caller receives; its `error` field
  holds a message string, not a class) · Fault code (the bounded token an error carries
  in `code`) · Tool-level failure (returned as a successful result with `isError: true`,
  never thrown)
- **Values** — one class name, one referent, with one live exception: `EncryptedFieldError`
  is defined twice with independent identity and both definitions are published — core's
  (`packages/core/src/field-crypto.ts:24`, exported at `index.ts:135`) and the server's
  (`apps/server/src/kek.ts:81`, exported at `run.ts:163`). Core's stores comment "let it
  propagate" naming its own class while `kekFieldCipher()` guarantees the server's is what
  arrives, so any `instanceof` check core adds fails silently. `ENCRYPTED_FIELD_PREFIX`
  (`'enc-v1:'`) is duplicated the same way. Nothing is wrong today because both call sites
  only re-throw; a reader who trusts the comment is already wrong.

### Fault code

The bounded machine token that names one failure, carried in a field called `code`.
Closed, code-owned, and never attacker-influenced text: the diagnostics store states
the rule as "errors NEVER stored as text. Classified to a finite code from a whitelist
(ENOENT, EACCES, …), else `unclassified`" (`packages/core/src/diagnostics.ts:47-49`).
Nine independent vocabularies, in four casing conventions, with no rule published
anywhere saying which shape a third surface will use. *(defined in code only)*

- **Names** — SDK `RunnerConditionCode` `MessageDispositionReasonCode` `DeviceTokenErrorCode` ·
  core domain-error `code` unions on `SecretsError` `ChannelsError` `ObjectivesError`
  `ToolSourcesError` `NotificationsError` `TeamProcessError`, and `FsErrorCode` ·
  core `DiagnosticCause` (25 values, `<module>.<event>`) · core `classifyError()` →
  `SafeFields.errorCode` · CLI `QuarantineReason` · CLI `CheckStatus` · route
  `code: 'member_name_required'`
- **Not** — Error (the thrown class; the code is a field on it, not the class) · Bounded
  reason (a code *plus* its fixed detail text; a fault code alone carries no prose) ·
  Exit code (a process status number, not a token) · `JsonRpcError.code` (the Codex
  bridge's numeric protocol code — the only numeric `code` in the repo)
- **Values** — four casings, no stated rule: snake_case (`no_credential`,
  `slug_taken`, `member_name_required`) · dotted `<module>.<event>`
  (`correlator.raw_capture_failed`, `retention.overflow`) · kebab-case
  (`invalid-utf8`, `no-exact-match-adapter`) · UPPERCASE, both errno passthrough
  (`ENOENT` `EACCES` `EPERM` `EISDIR` `ENOTDIR` `EMFILE` `ENOSPC` `EROFS` `ETIMEDOUT`
  `ECONNREFUSED` `SQLITE_BUSY` `SQLITE_FULL` `SQLITE_CORRUPT`, plus `SYNTAX` `TYPE`
  `RANGE` and the fallback `unclassified`) and CLI output (`PASS` `WARN` `FAIL`).
  The rule the codebase should have stated: snake_case for wire and stored codes,
  dotted where the code must name its emitter, errno passed through verbatim,
  UPPERCASE reserved for CLI operator output. `QuarantineReason` and
  `no-exact-match-adapter` are the only two that break it.

### Error response

The JSON body an HTTP caller receives on a non-2xx: `error` is a human-readable
message, `code` is a machine token, `details` carries the Zod issues of a rejected
payload. There is no SDK type for it — 265 sites in `packages/core/src/app.ts`
build the shape by hand, and only the six routes with a domain-error mapper emit
`code` at all. *(defined in code only)*

- **Names** — route `{ error }` · route `{ error, details }` · route `{ error, code }`
  (channels `app.ts:1663`, tool sources `:2425`, secrets/variables `:2968`, team
  process `:3384`, notifications `:3721`, files `mapFsError` `:6709`) · route
  `{ error, code, retryAfter }` (one site, `code: 'member_name_required'`) · SDK
  `ClientError.body` (kept as an opaque string) · SDK `DeviceTokenErrorResponse {error,
  errorDescription?}` · MCP `broker error <status>: <body>` (the bridge's agent-visible
  rendering, falling back to the `ClientError` message when the body is empty)
- **Not** — Error (the thrown class; a mapper turns one into this) · Fault code (the
  token in `code`; `error` never holds one, except on the route below)
- **Values** — `error`: a message everywhere except `POST /enroll/poll`, where RFC 8628
  puts the code in that field — `authorization_pending` `slow_down` `expired_token`
  `access_denied`, and the first two describe a perfectly healthy poll. That route is
  the named exception, not the pattern. `ObjectivesError` is the one coded domain class
  whose mapper drops the code (`mapObjectivesError`, `app.ts:4364`, returns
  `{ error: string }`), so its four codes never reach a caller.

### Bounded reason

`{ code, detail }` where the detail is fixed text owned by the code — "Fixed,
code-owned text only; adapters must never forward raw payloads"
(`packages/sdk/src/types.ts:347`). Raw adapter errors are classified locally and
discarded because they may carry credentials, endpoints, URLs or prompt fragments;
adding a code fails the build until its fixed message is supplied.

- **Names** — SDK `RunnerConditionFrame.reason`, `MessageDispositionFrame.reason`
  (`code: MessageDispositionReasonCode`), `ExecutorState.reason` (`code:
  RunnerConditionCode`) · CLI `RUNNER_CONDITION_DETAIL`, `classifyRunnerFailure()` · MCP
  `roster` output `executor=degraded(<code>)` · UI "DEGRADED · `{code}`" badge, tooltip
  "Runner degraded: `{code}`" · persisted `message_deliveries.reason_json` · docs
  [Presence](/docs/concepts/presence)
- **Not** — Narrative reason: free prose with no such contract —
  `NotificationDelivery.statusReason` (dispatch notes joined with `; `, column
  `notification_deliveries.status_reason`), `DeviceTokenErrorResponse.errorDescription`
  (an approver's rejection note), `ContextControlRequest.reason` (caller-supplied,
  forwarded to the agent verbatim), `ActivityContextControl.detail` (framework text,
  present on every non-`applied` outcome) · Reason list: codes with no detail at all —
  `TeamStatusMember.stalledReasons`, `TeamStatusObjective.staleSignals` · Fault code
  (the token alone, with no detail beside it)
- **Values** — `RunnerConditionCode`: `no_credential` `invalid_model`
  `model_unavailable` `server_overloaded` `auth_blocked` `unknown` ·
  `MessageDispositionReasonCode`: `degraded` `turn_failed` `expired` `unsupported`
  `operator_policy`

### Attempt outcome

How one attempted unit of work ended. Always qualified by its subject — turn
outcome, context-control outcome, delivery status — because every one of these enums
has its own members and every one of them contains a non-failure, so none is an
error enum.

- **Names** — SDK `RunnerTurnFrame.outcome`, `ExecutorState.lastTurnOutcome`,
  `ContextControlOutcome`, `ActivityContextControl.outcome`, `NotificationDeliveryStatus`,
  `DeliveryReport.acknowledgement.status` · core `PollOutcome.kind`, `LookupOutcome.kind`
- **Not** — Outcome (an objective's definition of done — a prose string the assignee
  must satisfy, defined with the objectives; the bare word belongs to that field, which
  is why this one is always qualified) · Message
  disposition (how a runner settled one delivered message: `MessageDisposition`
  `accepted` `acted` `handled` `deferred` `refused`, a wire contract named
  `disposition-v1` whose first and fourth values are nonterminal leases) · Amendment
  disposition (`AmendmentDisposition` `correction | scope_change` — an editorial
  classification of a team-process edit, surfacing as a field spelled `disposition`
  on the same wire and as the DB column `team_process_edits.disposition`; the word
  is shared, the referent is not) · Runner condition (whether a runner can act at
  all, not how one attempt ended)
- **Values** — `RunnerTurnFrame.outcome`: `acted` `no_action` `failed` ·
  `ContextControlOutcome`: `applied` `declined` `unsupported` `failed` — the repo's
  clearest three-way split, `declined` a refusal, `unsupported` an inability that
  retrying cannot fix, `failed` a break · `NotificationDeliveryStatus`: `delivered`
  `pending` `expired` `dropped` `rejected` `filtered` `duplicate` `coalesced` `failed`,
  and three of its doc-comments do not describe what the dispatcher writes.
  `delivered` means **acknowledged by a runner**, not pushed: the dispatcher sets it
  only when `acknowledged > 0`, or on an `acted`/`handled` disposition
  (`packages/core/src/notifications/dispatcher.ts:334-335,353-358`). `pending` also
  covers "queued while mid-task" and "message ids exist but nothing acknowledged".
  `failed` is declared, schema'd and styled but **never written**: treat it as
  reserved.

### Refusal

A negative the system chose on policy, as against one it suffered. It has no type
and no field of its own; it is carried by five enum values and one exit code, and
by the verb every test uses for a policy negative. *(defined in code only)*

- **Names** — SDK `MessageDisposition 'refused'`, `DeliveryReport.acknowledgement.status
  'refused'`, `ContextControlOutcome 'declined'`, `NotificationDeliveryStatus 'rejected'
  | 'filtered' | 'dropped'` · core `CredentialShapedBodyError` ("message refused: …") ·
  container exit `64` · docs [Presence](/docs/concepts/presence),
  [Deployment](/docs/deployment), [Stub runner](/docs/runners/stub)
- **Not** — Failure (`ContextControlOutcome 'failed'` — the runner tried and something
  broke) · Inability (`ContextControlOutcome 'unsupported'` — the ask was never
  possible) · Loss (evidence that went missing; a loss is never a decision)
- **Values** — `refused`: a runner settled a delivered message by declining it,
  terminally · `declined`: the agent was asked and did not do it, with a reason ·
  `rejected`: signature verification failed, or no secret was configured · `filtered`:
  the endpoint's filter rules dropped the delivery · `dropped` (notifications only):
  either every member target was offline and the policy said drop, or a subscriber
  refused the delivery — both decisions · exit `64`: the container refused a
  configuration "rather than silently not done"

### Loss

Evidence the product knows it no longer has. Six kinds, each with its own counter or
state and no superordinate name in the code; the diagnostics header already reasons
about them as one class. A loss word must never name a policy decision.
*(defined in code only)*

- **Names** — CLI `ActivityUploaderStats.dropped`, printed in the run summary as
  "captured N events (U uploaded, D dropped)" · CLI `QuarantineReason` (module-local to
  `otlp-relay.ts`, but operator-visible: the reason is written into the `swept.jsonl`
  manifest in the `.quarantine` sibling of the spool) · core `DiagnosticCause`
  `retention.overflow` `retention.fanout_truncated` `retention.unavailable` · core
  `CaptureHealth {state:'gap', unmatchedMarkers, since}` · core `Coverage 'indeterminate'`
- **Not** — `NotificationDeliveryStatus 'dropped'` (a policy decision in both of its
  causes — an all-offline target set, or a subscriber that refused; the one place
  `dropped` does not mean loss, and the collision the uploader counter should give up
  by renaming itself `lost`, which is what its own doc-comment already calls the
  events) · Refusal (a choice) · Completeness diagnostics (the retained record of these
  losses, with its own cause enum and point/incident split)
- **Values** — `dropped` (uploader): overflow-evicted, rejected after close, or dropped
  at final flush · `QuarantineReason`: `invalid-utf8` `oversized` `unreadable` · `gap`:
  completed exchange markers aged past the grace window without their stored bodies ·
  `indeterminate`: the answer's coverage, which is a state and not a zero

### Health

The current soundness of one named subsystem. Never bare: every health states its
subject, because two unrelated subsystems both spell their bad state `degraded`.
Field absence means the broker has no opinion — never "healthy".

- **Names** — SDK `CaptureHealthState` (`Presence.captureHealth`); `Presence.diagnosticsRetention`,
  whose union is inlined on the field because the SDK publishes no `RetentionHealth`
  type · core `CaptureHealth`, `RetentionHealth` · UI "CAPTURE UNCHECKED" badge · docs
  [Presence](/docs/concepts/presence)
- **Not** — Runner condition (whether a runner can act right now — a live self-report
  per subscription, `ready | degraded | unreported`, rolled up on `ExecutorState`) ·
  Check status (`CheckStatus` `PASS | WARN | FAIL`, one line of a local doctor report,
  not a subsystem verdict) · Incident (`HealthMode 'incident'` — a retained completeness
  failure with a named observed recovery, as against a `point` loss that is history the
  moment it happens)
- **Values** — `CaptureHealthState`: `ok` `gap` `unevaluated` — the internal
  `CaptureHealth` also carries `pending`, deliberately projected to `ok` because healthy
  correlation lag leaves every normal turn briefly unmatched and a visible "maybe" would
  flicker; `unevaluated` is explicitly not collapsed into `ok` · `RetentionHealth`:
  `healthy` `degraded` `unknown` — `unknown` is reachable and load-bearing, because a
  store that cannot say "I do not know" would recreate the problem it exists to prevent

### Check status

The verdict on one line of a doctor report — the local, broker-free environment
check a member runs before their first session. `PASS` and `FAIL` decide the exit
code through `DoctorReport.anyFail`; `WARN` is advisory and changes nothing.
**Doctor** is the command, the flag and the report object; **preflight** is the
silent run of that same doctor before every session start, where only a `FAIL` aborts.
Both are defined in Execution runtime, both are user-visible, and neither is a synonym
for the other.

- **Names** — CLI `CheckStatus`, `DoctorCheck {name, status, detail}`, `DoctorReport
  {checks, anyFail}` · CLI `--doctor`, `--skip-doctor` · CLI stderr "preflight FAILED —
  fix the above or pass --skip-doctor to bypass" · docs [CLI reference](/docs/reference/cli),
  [Conformance](/docs/dev/conformance)
- **Not** — Health (a broker's verdict on a subsystem, carried on the wire; a check
  status never leaves the CLI's stdout) · Exit code (a report of many checks collapses
  to one number; `WARN` never reaches it) · Message level (`warning` is an author's
  severity on a message, not a check's verdict) · Fault code (`PASS | WARN | FAIL` is
  the repo's only UPPERCASE enum, and the rule that keeps it legitimate is "UPPERCASE
  is CLI operator output")
- **Values** — `PASS` `WARN` `FAIL`

### Exit code

The CLI's machine contract with a shell caller or a supervisor. Four bands, three in
the CLI and one in the container entrypoint. The published table is currently
narrower than the behavior: `2` is documented as bad flags only, but a broker that
cannot be reached (`RunnerStartupError`) and a missing agent binary
(`AgentAdapterError`) are both converted to `UsageError` and also exit `2`, so a
supervisor branching on 1-vs-2 today gets the wrong answer.

- **Names** — CLI `fail(message, code = 1)`, `UsageError` · CLI `DoctorReport.anyFail`
  (the only input to the 0/1 choice under `--doctor`) · container `docker/entrypoint.sh`
  exit `64` · docs [CLI reference](/docs/reference/cli), [Deployment](/docs/deployment)
- **Not** — Check status (`PASS | WARN | FAIL`, per check inside a doctor report; only a
  `FAIL` reaches exit 1, `WARN` is advisory) · Fault code (a wire or stored token, never
  a process status) · `JsonRpcError.code` (the Codex bridge's numeric protocol code,
  unrelated to process exit)
- **Values** — `0`: success, including a doctor report with no FAIL · `1`: runtime
  failure · `2`: usage error, and today also startup and environment failures · `64`: a
  refused configuration, emitted by the container entrypoint and absent from the CLI
  table · `csuite claude` and `csuite codex` exit with the agent's own code when the
  agent ends the session

### Message level

The severity an author puts on one message, chosen from six bands, and the input to
whether a web push stays on screen. It is a property of the message, not of a log
line.

- **Names** — SDK `LogLevel` (`Message.level`, `PushPayload.level`,
  `NotificationEndpoint.level`, `NotificationOverrides.level`) · CLI `csuite push
  --level` · MCP `send` / `broadcast` / `channels_post` argument `level` · route query
  `?level=critical` on a notification hook · core `HIGH_PRIORITY_LEVELS`
- **Not** — Log level (the operator's stderr threshold, `csuite-core`'s own `LogLevel` =
  `debug | info | warn | error`, set with `CSUITE_LOG_LEVEL` — a different union
  published under the same type name from a different package; `warn` and `warning` do
  not interoperate) · Push severity (`normal | high`, derived from this; it lives on
  `packages/core/src/push-dispatch.ts`'s `PushPayload.severity`, a web-push envelope
  that shares its type name with the SDK's `PushPayload`, the documented `POST /push`
  body, which carries `level` and no `severity`) · Check status (`PASS | WARN | FAIL`,
  CLI operator output) · `ToastKind` (`info | success | warn | error`, a web-UI banner
  style)
- **Values** — `debug` `info` `notice` `warning` `error` `critical` — `warning`, `error`
  and `critical` map to push severity `high`, which sets `requireInteraction` so the OS
  notification stays until dismissed; `critical` also punches through debounce and
  busy-wait in the notification dispatcher

### Tool-level failure

A tool that fails returns a successful call carrying `isError: true` and the error
text in its content — "MCP convention is that tool failures are successful calls
with error payloads the model can read and self-correct on"
(`packages/core/src/tool-sources/custom-executor.ts:7-9`). It is never thrown and
never a non-2xx. This is the one failure doctrine the repo states uniformly, in
three implementations and two documents.

- **Names** — SDK `InvokeToolResponse.isError` · core `ToolCallResult`, `errorResult()`
  · CLI `errorResult()` in the MCP toolbox · route `POST
  /tool-sources/:slug/tools/:name/invoke` → 200 with `isError: true` · docs [MCP
  tools](/docs/dev/mcp-tools), [REST API](/docs/dev/rest-api)
- **Not** — Error response (authorization and registry problems stay HTTP errors, in a
  documented ladder: 404 unknown slug → 403 unbound → 409 disabled → 413 payload over
  1 MiB → 400 malformed payload → 404 unknown tool; an unreachable MCP upstream is 502,
  the single tool-execution failure that is not an `isError`) · Error (two throws reach
  the caller as 500: broker-side registry corruption, and `ToolSourcesError 'no_kek'`
  on a credential decrypt, which answers "credential unavailable" here and 503 on the
  admin write paths)

## Wire conventions

The spellings every HTTP client copies verbatim: query-parameter names, the paging
grammar, and the timestamp fields on the rows that come back. The distinction that
matters most is that a timestamp here answers one of two questions — when the producer
says the thing happened (`ts`), or when the broker stored the row (`receivedAt`) — and
the two differ by a whole upload batch. The second distinction is arity: `from`/`to`
and `before` each name more than one mechanism, and only some of those mechanisms
return every row.

### Query parameter

A name/value pair on an HTTP request's query string. Query parameters are `snake_case`;
the same knob is `camelCase` as a JSON body field or TypeScript property, and
`kebab-case` as a CLI flag. Four wire parameters do not follow the rule yet and still
read camelCase: `stalledMs`, `parentOrigin`, `clientKind`, `clientVersion`.

- **Names** — route `POST /hooks/:slug?if_offline=&if_busy=&level=` · SDK
  `NotificationOverrides.ifOffline` · CLI `csuite notifications add --if-offline` ·
  docs [External notifications](/docs/concepts/external-notifications)
- **Not** — enum value casing (the rule governing the values a parameter *carries* —
  `session_start`, `llm_exchange`, `queue` — not the parameter's name) · JSON body
  field (camelCase; `if_offline` and `ifOffline` are one knob at two layers, not two
  knobs) · a shared parameter name as evidence of a shared referent: `?code=` on
  `GET /setup/connect-platform` is a server-minted platform-bind code of at most 32
  characters, while `?code=` on the enrollment page is the eight-character Crockford
  user code a person reads aloud
- **Values** — one boolean encoding, `true` and `false`. `DELETE /fs/rm?recursive=` is
  the only boolean on the query string and it also accepts `1`, an alias the SDK never
  emits; the sibling `POST /fs/mkdir` takes the same flag as a JSON boolean in the body
- **Decided** — D44: snake_case query parameters, over camelCase (2026-09-03)

### Time window

The pair of inclusive epoch-millisecond bounds that restricts a read to a slice of the
timeline: *"Inclusive lower bound on ts (ms since epoch)"*. Both bound the row's own
producer instant — `ts`, or `ts_ms` on telemetry — never the instant the broker stored
the row.

- **Names** — route `GET /members/:name/activity?from=<ts>&to=<ts>` (also `/genai`,
  `/telemetry`) · SDK `ListActivityQuery.from`/`.to`, `ListGenaiQuery.from`/`.to`,
  `ListTelemetryQuery.from`/`.to` · core `ListActivityFilter.from`/`.to`,
  `GenAiQuery.from`/`.to`, `TelemetryQuery.from`/`.to` · docs
  [REST API](/docs/dev/rest-api)
- **Not** — `Message.from`/`.to` (sender and recipient of a message; MCP `send`'s `to`
  is a teammate) · `FsMoveRequest.from`/`.to` (source and destination paths; MCP `fs_mv`
  uses the same two words) · paging cursor (where the last page stopped, not which slice
  to read) · `EventLogTailOptions.since` (a core-internal lower bound that no route and
  no SDK method exposes)

### Paging cursor

The exclusive position where the previous page stopped: a `{ts, id}` pair sent as two
query parameters, both halves or neither, because *"a cursor with one half is a caller
bug that would silently read from the start of the range"*. It is composite rather than
scalar because *"A timestamp-only `to = ts - 1` cursor loses same-ts rows that fell
beyond the previous page boundary"*. The shipped wire spelling is `cursor_ts` +
`cursor_id`, accepted and validated on `/genai` even though the REST reference's line
for that endpoint omits it.

- **Names** — route `GET /members/:name/activity?cursor_ts=<ts>&cursor_id=<id>` (also
  `/genai`, `/telemetry`) · SDK `ListActivityQuery.cursor`, `ListGenaiQuery.cursor`,
  `ListTelemetryQuery.cursor` · core `ListActivityFilter.before`, `GenAiQuery.after`,
  `TelemetryQuery.after` · docs [REST API](/docs/dev/rest-api)
- **Not** — time window (`from`/`to`: which slice to read, not where to resume) ·
  `HistoryQuery.before` (a scalar epoch-ms bound — the same job by a mechanism that
  loses rows) · `ActivityContextControl.tokens.before`/`.after` (context size on either
  side of a compaction, not positions in a page walk)
- **Values** — direction is per endpoint and the shipped name states neither: activity
  pages newest-first (core `before`), gen-AI and telemetry page oldest-first (core
  `after`). A client that reuses the activity paging loop against `/telemetry` feeds the
  newest row back into a forward walk and never terminates.
- **Decided** — D44: split the wire name by direction — `after_ts`/`after_id` on the
  oldest-first endpoints, `before_ts`/`before_id` on activity — over keeping one
  `cursor_ts`/`cursor_id` with an added `order=asc|desc` (2026-09-03)

### `before` (scalar page bound)

The single epoch-millisecond upper bound `/history` and the delivery receipts take
instead of a composite cursor: *"Return only rows with `ts < before`. For pagination."*
Being exclusive on `ts` alone, it drops every row that shares the page-boundary
millisecond with the last row of the page before — the defect the composite cursor
exists to prevent, and the pattern the web UI's thread paging uses today. It cannot be
renamed `before_ts`: D44 gives that name to the composite cursor's timestamp half, so
the arity would still be invisible while D46 (whether `/history` grows the composite
cursor) is open.

- **Names** — route `GET /history?before=<ts>`,
  `GET /notifications/endpoints/:slug/deliveries?before=<ts>` · SDK
  `HistoryQuery.before` · core `EventLogQueryOptions.before` · docs
  [REST API](/docs/dev/rest-api)
- **Not** — paging cursor (composite `{ts, id}`; keeps same-millisecond rows) ·
  `ListActivityFilter.before` (core's word for that composite cursor — one word, two
  arities) · time window `to` (inclusive, and bounds the slice rather than the page) ·
  `ActivityContextControl.tokens.before` (a token count)

### `ts`

The instant the producer says the thing happened: the agent's clock on activity events,
the provider's on telemetry, the broker's on broker-authored messages. It is the value
every time window bounds, every ordered read sorts on, and the cursor's timestamp half
compares against. *(defined in code only)*

- **Names** — SDK `Message.ts`, `GenAiInference.ts`, `ObjectiveEvent.ts`, and every
  `Activity*` event variant · DB `member_activity.ts`, `gen_ai_inference.ts`, `events.ts`
- **Not** — `receivedAt` (the broker's receipt instant, not the producer's) ·
  `RunnerConditionFrame.at`, `RunnerTurnFrame.at`, `MessageDispositionFrame.at`,
  `channel_audit.at` (this same instant spelled `at` on IPC frames and audit rows) ·
  `TelemetryRecordRow.tsMs` and column `telemetry.ts_ms` (this same instant under a
  third name; `?cursor_ts=` on `/telemetry` compares against it) ·
  `TelemetryRecordRow.tsUnixNano` (*"the producer's own nanosecond stamp… `tsMs` is the
  millisecond value everything queries and sorts on"* — a different clock and
  precision, correctly named apart) · `AnthropicMessagesEntry.startedAt`/`.endedAt` (an
  interval, correctly named as a pair)

### `receivedAt`

The instant the broker stored the row: *"Server receive time (epoch ms); `ts` is the
capture time."* It is one clock for the whole broker, which is why capture health bounds
every window on it — spelled `created_at` on the activity table — and never on an agent's
stamp: *"Agent clocks are not used anywhere here: agent-side deltas produce ~33% negative
lags across hosts."* *(defined in code only)*

- **Names** — SDK `GenAiInferenceRecord.receivedAt`, `GenAiInferenceSummary.receivedAt`,
  `TelemetryRecordRow.receivedAt`, `NotificationDelivery.receivedAt` · DB
  `gen_ai_inference.received_at`, `telemetry.received_at`,
  `notification_deliveries.received_at`
- **Not** — `ts` (the producer's instant; the two differ by the whole upload-batch
  delay, and a consumer that reads one for the other reads a clock the agent never
  saw) · `ActivityRow.createdAt` and column `member_activity.created_at` (this same
  broker-receipt instant wearing the entity-creation word — *"the boundary is its
  `created_at` — broker receipt, single clock"*) · `createdAt` on configuration rows
  (when the entity came into being)

### `createdAt`

The instant an entity came into existence, paired with `updatedAt` on anything mutable.
On configuration rows that instant is also the event instant, so nothing distinguishes
the two words there — which is what makes the activity stream's borrowing of `createdAt`
for broker receipt invisible until a consumer trusts it. Every other instant on this
wire ends in `At`.

- **Names** — SDK `Objective.createdAt`, `Secret.createdAt`, `Variable.createdAt`,
  `ToolSource.createdAt`, `FsEntry.createdAt`, each beside `updatedAt` · DB
  `objectives.created_at` / `objectives.updated_at`
- **Not** — `ActivityRow.createdAt` (broker receipt on an ingested stream — the
  `receivedAt` concept, and the column capture health bounds on) · `receivedAt` (broker
  receipt, correctly named) · `objective.openedAt` / `objective.closedAt` (two fields
  that exist nowhere in the tree; the SDK's own paging recipe on `listActivity` invents
  them from the `objective_open` / `objective_close` activity kinds, where the entity's
  own boundaries are `createdAt` and `completedAt`) · `Presence.lastSeen` (the only
  `last*` instant on the wire without the `At` suffix, and bumped when a member
  registers or subscribes rather than on traffic)

### Duration (`*Ms`)

An elapsed time or a window length, in milliseconds, with the unit in the name — an
epoch-millisecond instant and a millisecond span are the same JavaScript number, and the
suffix is what tells them apart. `GET /team/status` takes its staleness window as
`stalledMs` and echoes it back as `stalledAfterMs`, so request and response name the
same window differently.

- **Names** — SDK `ActivitySessionEnd.durationMs`, `ActivityToolAction.durationMs`,
  `NotificationDeliveryPolicy.debounceMs`/`.queueTtlMs`/`.maxWaitMs`,
  `ToolSourceConfig.timeoutMs`, `TeamStatusResponse.stalledAfterMs`,
  `RosterResponse.activityWindowMs` · route `GET /team/status?stalledMs=<ms>` · CLI
  `csuite team status --stalled 30m` · MCP `team_status` argument `stalled` · core
  `CaptureHealthOptions.graceMs`
- **Not** — `ActivityLlmExchange.duration` (the same unit in the same union with the
  suffix dropped: *"Milliseconds between request start and response end"*) · the
  duration *string* `30m` / `2h` / `1d` that the CLI flag and the MCP argument take and
  parse into `stalledMs` — a human spelling, deliberately not the wire form ·
  `AnthropicMessagesEntry.startedAt`/`.endedAt` (the interval itself, not its length)

### `limit`

The maximum number of rows one read returns. The name is uncontested at every layer;
the number is not — each endpoint sets its own default and cap in the module that runs
the query, and the MCP layer caps tighter than the endpoint it calls.

- **Names** — route `?limit=<n>` on `/history`, `/members/:name/activity`,
  `/members/:name/genai`, `/members/:name/telemetry`,
  `/notifications/endpoints/:slug/deliveries` · SDK `ListActivityQuery.limit` (*"Max
  rows to return. Default 200, max 1000. Newest first."*) ·
  CLI `csuite notifications deliveries --limit <n>` · MCP `notifications_deliveries`
  argument `limit` · docs [REST API](/docs/dev/rest-api)
- **Not** — time window (bounds which slice is read; `limit` bounds one page of it) ·
  paging cursor (where the page starts)
- **Values** — default/max per endpoint: `/history` 100/1000 · activity 200/1000 ·
  gen-AI and telemetry 500/5000 · deliveries 50/500 · MCP `notifications_deliveries`
  20/100 — five times tighter than the endpoint's own cap. The tool description states
  that cap; the clamp does not, so an agent that asks for 200 receipts gets 100 rows back
  with nothing marking them short and reads the page as the end of the stream.

### Telemetry record filter (`event`)

The exact OTLP record or metric name a telemetry read filters on: *"Exact OTLP record /
metric name, e.g. `api_request`"*. The query parameter and the SDK field are `event`;
every layer beneath is `name`, down to the column. *(defined in code only)*

- **Names** — route `GET /members/:name/telemetry?event=<name>` · SDK
  `ListTelemetryQuery.event` · core `TelemetryQuery.name` · DB `telemetry.name`
- **Not** — `?name=` on `GET /subscribe` (the **member** name, on the same query
  surface) · `ActivityRow.event` (the whole event object; `event` here delivers a
  string) · `?signal=` (which OTLP signal the read is restricted to, `log` or `metric`) ·
  `TeamStatusObjective.staleSignals` (evidence that an objective went stale —
  `thread_post`, `pr_link`, `lifecycle`: a different sense of "signal" entirely) ·
  `?kind=` (`ActivityKind` on the activity read, repeated and OR-combined, which the
  core filter spells plural as `ListActivityFilter.kinds`; `request`|`response` on
  `GET /members/:name/genai/:id/raw`, which selects which half of one exchange)

## Guides job vocabulary

The words a new user meets first. They live in `docs/guides/**`, `README.md` and
`docs/why.mdx`, and none of them is an identifier, column, route, flag, tool name or UI
label anywhere in the tree. Each does one of three things: it names a union the code only
ever builds out of parts — **job**, **provisioned access**; it names a boundary the broker
never sees — **workstation**; or it has been claimed by a shipped surface that means
something else — **inbox** is a nav item about unread threads, **trigger** is a restart
cause, **job description** pointed at two oppositely-visible instruction blocks fifteen
lines apart. The three retirements below are done in the guides; they are recorded so
the words do not come back. The rule that governs the family: a guides word teaches the shape of a setup;
a code word names one thing you can create, bind or delete. Where the two compete for a
word, the code word keeps it and the guides word is retired here.

### Job

A durable standing assignment held by one agent member — an identity, provisioned access,
a route by which work arrives without a human relaying it, and a definition of done for
discrete work — evidenced afterwards by a trace. Each part is a defined concept (`Member`;
tool-source, secret and variable bindings; notification endpoints; an objective and its
required `outcome`), the union is not, and no layer below the documentation names it:
"Every job reduces to the same four-step setup"
(`docs/guides/give-an-agent-a-job.mdx:16`), "Six jobs, one shape"
(`docs/guides/jobs-gallery.mdx:140`).

- **Names** — docs [Give an agent a job](/docs/guides/give-an-agent-a-job) · docs [Jobs gallery](/docs/guides/jobs-gallery) · docs [Why CommandSuite](/docs/why)
- **Not** — Objective (one discrete unit of assigned work carrying its own required outcome, created by an originator and executed by one assignee; a job holds many over its life and outlives all of them) · Member (the seat — name, role, permissions, credentials — which is who holds a job, not the assignment) · Ticket or queue entry (the guides rule this reading out in their own words: "not job tickets", `docs/guides/multi-agent-team.mdx:181`; "not a job queue for hundreds", `docs/why.mdx:88`) · CI job (a workflow job in the release gate; contributor vocabulary, not product)
- **Decided** — D48: define Job as a documentation-layer composite with its decomposition stated in the entry, over dissolving it into its four parts (2026-09-03)

### Provisioned access

What the broker grants a member so it can do the work: tool sources whose credentials the
broker holds and calls with itself, plus the secrets and variables injected into the runner
environment at session start. It is an umbrella over three grants, never a record of its
own — the grant is always one source, one secret or one variable reaching one member,
either through an explicit binding row or through `allMembers: true` on the record itself.

- **Names** — docs [Give an agent a job](/docs/guides/give-an-agent-a-job) · docs [Jobs gallery](/docs/guides/jobs-gallery) · DB `tool_source_bindings` · `secret_bindings` · `variable_bindings`, plus the `all_members` column on each parent record · CLI `csuite tools bind` · `csuite secrets bind` · `csuite variables bind` · UI route kinds `tool-sources` and `environment`
- **Not** — Kit (the guides' former word for this, no longer used; the two live senses of the word are both contributor-side — `packages/cli/test/runtime/conformance/kit.ts`, "the shared scenario suite every AgentAdapter must pass before shipping", and the upstream Helm design layer this UI is ported from, `packages/web-ui/src/styles/theme.css:2404-2406`) · Permission (a leaf of broker authority such as `objectives.create`; tools and values are bound to a member, not held as leaves) · Tool source, Secret, Variable (the three constituents, each defined on its own)
