- **Verbs** — bind, unbind. `allMembers` grants the same reach with no binding row, so a member's access is never the binding table alone.

### Workstation

The machine a runner executes on — a dedicated user account, a VM or a box carrying only
that job's files. The broker neither registers nor enforces it: "under a runner, the
machine's boundaries are the agent's boundaries"
(`docs/guides/give-an-agent-a-job.mdx:88-90`), which makes the workstation the containment
boundary the product argues from and the one boundary no API call can check. **Blast
radius** is the guides' teaching phrase for that boundary's extent — "The workstation is
the blast radius" (`:162`) — not a second concept.

- **Names** — docs [Give an agent a job](/docs/guides/give-an-agent-a-job) · docs [Run an always-on agent](/docs/guides/always-on-agent) · docs [csuite codex](/docs/runners/codex) · docs [Members](/docs/concepts/members), [Presence](/docs/concepts/presence), [Tool sources](/docs/concepts/tool-sources)
- **Not** — "Machine" / "the box" (the same referent under two other words, used throughout [Run an always-on agent](/docs/guides/always-on-agent); workstation is canonical) · Device (what `csuite connect` enrolls — the broker stores no device record, only a token carrying a free-text `TokenInfo.label` such as "laptop" or "prod-vm"; one workstation can hold several device tokens, so the two are different cuts) · Runner (the program that runs an agent as a member; several runners can share a workstation) · Broker (the server the workstation connects to, which never sees it)
- **Decided** — D49: Workstation is a deployment concept, named here as explicitly outside the system, over modeling it as a product entity with an owner and an isolation policy (2026-09-03)

### The agent's inbox *(retired)*

The guides' former name for the surface through which the outside world reaches an agent.
The thing it named is a **notification endpoint**, which carries that name at every code
layer, and the guides now use it: `**Endpoints**` is the blueprint field in
[Jobs gallery](/docs/guides/jobs-gallery) (`docs/guides/jobs-gallery.mdx:29,48,68,86,107,125`)
and "the agent endpoints — anything that can POST can hand it work" is the phrasing in
[Give an agent a job](/docs/guides/give-an-agent-a-job) (`:102`). Recorded here so the word
is not reintroduced: **Inbox** is a shipped UI route showing the signed-in viewer's unread
threads and open objectives, and [Events and messages](/docs/concepts/events) tells the
same reader "agents don't check an inbox and people don't refresh; there is nothing to
poll" (`docs/concepts/events.mdx:13`).

- **Names** — docs [Give an agent a job](/docs/guides/give-an-agent-a-job) · docs [Jobs gallery](/docs/guides/jobs-gallery) · `README.md:52` ("endpoints any system can reach by webhook")
- **Not** — Notification endpoint (what the phrase actually denotes: `NotificationEndpoint`, `notification_endpoints`, ingress `POST /hooks/:slug`, `csuite notifications`, `notifications_create`, permission `notifications.manage`) · Inbox (the web UI's computed attention feed for a human viewer, defined under Conversation and delivery; a member-targeted notification arrives as a message from `hook:<slug>` and can therefore show up there as one `thread-unread` row, which is overlap of contents, not identity of concept) · Ambient event (how a delivered event actually reaches a running agent — the docs' "ambient input"; nothing accumulates anywhere for the agent to open later, so there is no container to check)

### Self-provisioned poller

A cron the agent installs on its own workstation that checks a source with local
credentials and POSTs anything new to a notification endpoint targeting itself. It exists
because an idle agent is not running: "there is no loop in which a 'check the mailbox' tool
call could happen" (`docs/concepts/external-notifications.mdx:206-207`), so a recurring wake
has to arrive through the front door. An agent holding `notifications.manage` wires it end
to end from its own toolbox — `notifications_create` an endpoint targeting itself,
`notifications_set_secret`, the cron script, then `notifications_deliveries` and
`notifications_replay` to debug it.

- **Names** — docs [External notifications](/docs/concepts/external-notifications), heading "The self-provisioned poller" (`docs/concepts/external-notifications.mdx:204`) · docs [Give an agent a job](/docs/guides/give-an-agent-a-job), "The self-provisioned poller" (`docs/guides/give-an-agent-a-job.mdx:117`)
- **Not** — "Self-provisioned trigger" (the jobs gallery's former second name for this pattern, since replaced by `**Endpoints**` fields carrying this entry's own phrasing — "its own cron — a weekly self-provisioned poller", `docs/guides/jobs-gallery.mdx:29`; do not reintroduce it, because "trigger" names no object and collides with **restart trigger**) · Restart trigger (what caused a runner's cold-restart cycle — `RestartReason` is `'instructions' | 'environment'`, the only code sense of the noun, and always qualified) · Notification endpoint (the registered receiver the poller POSTs to; a poller is a shape a member sets up, not a record the broker stores)

### Job description *(retired)*

The on-ramp's former phrase for a member's standing text, which named two
oppositely-visible blocks fifteen lines apart. Retired rather than assigned, because the
two differ in audience and in visibility: **role description** is public
(`Role.description`, wire kind `role_description`, UI hint "What this role does on the
team (public)") and **personal instructions** is private (`Member.instructions`, wire kind
`personal_instructions`, "Not visible to teammates"). [Give an agent a
job](/docs/guides/give-an-agent-a-job) now uses both names
(`docs/guides/give-an-agent-a-job.mdx:39-43`). Recorded so the phrase does not return:
putting a bookkeeping policy in `--description` publishes it to the roster; putting the
role summary in `--instructions` hides it from the teammates who route work.

- **Names** — docs [Give an agent a job](/docs/guides/give-an-agent-a-job)
- **Not** — Role description (`Role.description`, wire kind `role_description`, printed by the CLI as `role description:` and labeled in the UI "Role description" with the hint "What this role does on the team (public)" — "visible to every teammate in the roster and instruction packet") · Personal instructions (`Member.instructions`, wire kind `personal_instructions`, printed as `personal instructions:` — "Not visible to teammates") · Role title (`Role.title`, the short label such as `engineer`; neither field carries authority, which is held only as permission leaves) · "Standing instructions" / "Instructions spine" (the jobs gallery's names for the whole composed packet, `docs/guides/jobs-gallery.mdx:13,31`; that packet is Composed instructions, of which these are two of four blocks)
